### 5.1 The script machine: memory, operands and helpers **[V]** (`src/OVL07.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl07`: unit complete, 33 routines)

ovl07 is the half of the ECL interpreter that ovl03 (the opcode loop, now matched: `make match UNIT=ovl03`, unit complete) calls for everything below the opcodes.

- **The address space** **[V]** (`MemRegion`, `ovl07:06FB`; `ReadVmWord` `ovl07:0E0C`, `WriteVmWord` `ovl07:0C77`): a script address is a word. `$4900..$4CFF` are the words of `GameVars` (game variable *n* is address `$4900 + n`), `$6B00..$6EFF` the words of the second block (DS:49D6), `$9700..$98FF` the words of the third ($400 bytes, DS:49DA), `$9900..$B6FF` the bytes of the loaded script (`EclCode`, so the script can patch itself), and anything else is one of the engine's own variables, handled one by one. The source declares each block a second time with the script's index range (`TVmWords4900` and friends, `absolute` on the 0-based pointers), which is what makes the displacements `$6E00`, `$2A00`, `-$2E00` and `$6700` in the code.
- **The character window** **[V]** (`GetCharacterVar` `ovl07:07BC`, `SetCharacterVar` `ovl07:0A74`): in the second block, some addresses are fields of `SelectedCharacter` rather than words of the block (reads try the character first; writes store the word and then the field). The offsets are the script's own character layout, not `TCharacter`'s: for example `$6B18` is the constitution (+$14), `$6BBB..$6BC3` the first five money words (+$88..+$90), `$6C0C` the side and quick-combat state (0, $80 for a party member run by the computer, $81 for the other side), `$6DB1`/`$6DB4` the selected character's place in the party, `$6DCF` a value from the charisma (0 for 3, then 5 per point up to 20 at 7, 25 for 8..12, then 30, 35, 40, 50, 55 and 60 from 18), `$6E12` the 8X8 tile set (`SelectTileSet` on write), `$6E22`/`24`/`26` load a wall set into slot 1..3 when written with bit 7 set. Writing $6B00..$6B70 also reaches the memorized spells and the bytes after them (QUIRK `ovl07:0AA3`). The address `$6B00` read or written as a string is the character's name.
- **Engine variables** **[V]**: below `$BF68`/`$C04B`, `$B1`, `$FB`, `$FC` read DS:6F6E, 6F6A, 6F6C and `$33D` the facing; above, `$C04B..$C04F` read the party's column, row (signed), facing div 2, and the wall and event bytes of its cell (DS:6AAD..6AB1). Writes to `$C04B`/`$C04C` move the party and `$C04D` sets the facing (0..3 times 2, larger values taken modulo 4), each setting DS:84E6; `$C059`/`$C05F` set the special view codes with DS:84E5 (section 5.2.1).
- **Operands** **[V]** (`DecodeOperands` `ovl07:0000`, `OperandValue` `ovl07:0136`): a command is an opcode byte at `EclPC` followed by operands of a type byte and a low byte, plus a high byte for types 1, 2, 3 and $81. Type 0 is an immediate byte, 2 an immediate word, 1 and 3 (and $80 when read as a value) the word at that address; $80 is a packed string whose low byte is its length in bytes, $81 the address of a stored string. Strings go to `EclStrings[1..]` in operand order. `SkipCommand` (`ovl07:1EA6`) knows the operand count of every opcode (1 to 14; `$29` takes 14), so a script can jump over a command it does not run.
- **Packed strings** **[V]** (`DecodeString` `ovl07:1153`, `SixBitChar` `ovl07:06D3`): four 6-bit codes per three bytes; codes $20..$3F are themselves (space, digits, punctuation), 1..$1F become `A`..`_`, 0 is skipped. Phases 2 and 3 build a byte from the previous byte shifted left (6 or 4) plus the current one shifted right (2 or 4), then shift it right by 2 (`ovl07:11C5`, `11F3`): the arithmetic gives the plain 6-bit split of the three bytes.
- **Control and comparisons** **[V]**: `ScriptGosub` (`ovl07:1951`) pushes `EclPC` on a linked return stack of 6-byte entries (`ReturnStack`) and jumps to the operand's address; `CompareStrings` and `CompareWords` set the six flags `CompareFlags` (=, <>, <, >, <=, >=) that the conditional opcodes test [I]. `InitScript` (`ovl07:01B6`) resets the machine for a new script (the flags, the return stack, `EclPC := $9900`, the five entry addresses, `VarNoCasting`, `VarForceWallView := 1`) and clears game variables `$4A00..$4A1F` and words `$6E79..$6E82` unless DS:49F8 asks to keep them once.
- **Helpers for opcodes** **[V]**: `LoadEcl` (section 4.2.2); `AskMenu` (`ovl07:1684`, the prompt-line menu the shops and the Temple use: `~` marks the hotkeys, letters and digits are lowered by $20 first (QUIRK `ovl07:15BF`: digits become `P`..`Y`), the result is the hotkey's 0-based position, 0 for Return when allowed, $FF otherwise); `AskListMenu` (`ChooseFromList` with a prepared title); `ShowEncounterPicture` (`ovl07:054E`: the SPRIT sprite by distance in the 3D view, then the PIC picture or HEAD and BODY close-up); `OpenStepsAhead` (cells without a wall ahead, up to 2); `StepPartyForward`; `SetupDuel` (`ovl07:1A6F`: `DuelMode`, and a computer-run copy of the selected character named `ROLF` with copies of its items on the other side); `ScaleMoney` (all seven money words times a `Real`, truncated); `LoseItems` (`ovl07:1DF0`: each item is lost on a d100 roll at most the chance, which drops for good by 90 for an item weighing more than 255 and by 50 for one more than 24); `MovementRange` (the party's fastest and slowest `Movement`, +$11C, doubled by affect $27 and halved by affect $2A); `DamageWithMessage` (`ovl07:20CC`: `dies.` when the damage exceeds the hit points plus 10, else `is hit FOR n points of Damage.`, then `ApplyDamage`).
- **Map corrections and frames** **[V]**: the map listed 39 entries; six (`07FE`, `08A2`, `0B48`, `0E38`, `1307`, `20A9`) are inside routines or a literal pool. Nine routines have frame bytes no statement uses, declared as unused locals (QUIRKs `ovl07:0139`, `01B9`, `0344`, `0414`, `0551`, `0E0F`, `1156`, `1A72`, `20CF`). Idioms proven here: `Hi * 256 + Lo` (not `shl 8`) gives `mov cl,8; shl ax,cl` with the right operand loaded first; `S := Concat(S, Ch)` allocates its string temporaries in the opposite order from `S := S + Ch`; a `case` with one arm and `else` compiles to `mov al,x; cmp al,k` where `if x = k` gives `cmp byte ptr [x],k`.
- **The command handlers (ovl03)** **[V]** (`EndCommand` `ovl03:0001`, `GotoCommand` `ovl03:00F4`, `GosubCommand` `ovl03:0111`, `CompareCommand` `ovl03:0126`, `ArithmeticCommand` `ovl03:01A3`, `RandomCommand` `ovl03:0234`; all matched): the dispatcher `ovl03:2F2D` runs one handler per `CurrentOpcode`. Opcode 0 (`EndCommand`, no operands) clears the text windows (a window drawn with empty text when DS:84E2 is set, a second one when game variable 230 is set), selects the character at DS:4436 when DS:4430 is set, empties `ReturnStack`, moves EclPC past the command and puts the text cursor at column 1, row 17. Opcode 1 jumps, 2 calls (`ScriptGosub`), 3 compares: strings `EclStrings[1]` and `[2]` when either operand type has bit 7 set, else two words. Opcodes 4 to 7 add, subtract, divide and multiply the first two operand values into the address the third operand names (QUIRK `ovl03:01FC`: subtraction is the second value minus the first). Opcode 8 writes a random value from 0 to the first operand (QUIRK `ovl03:024D`: 255 is not raised, so the roll stops at 254). Every address operand is `MakeWord(OperandLow[N], OperandHigh[N])`. The original's Real conversion of a roll is `Trunc(Random(n))` (probe `tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl03/RNDTRUNC.PAS`).
- **Opcodes 9 and 10 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`StoreCommand` `ovl03:0292`, `SelectCharacterCommand` `ovl03:02E0`; both matched): opcode 9 stores its first operand at the script address the second operand names: a word by `WriteVmWord` when the first operand is a value, the string `EclStrings[1]` by `WriteVmString` when its type has bit 7 set. Opcode 10 selects the party member at the 0-based position the operand names: the walk starts at the head of the party list (`PartyListHead`, the list `PartyIndexOf` in ovl07 walks) and follows `Next` (+$104) until the position or the end of the list, and a position past the end leaves the selection unchanged. It sets `ReselectCharacterPending` first, so the next command 0 selects `ReselectCharacter` again. When the operand's bit 7 is set and the script has just cleared the character's name and status (`ActiveFlagClearedByScript` and `NameClearedByScript`, set by `SetCharacterVar`), the selected member leaves the party (`RemoveSelectedCharacter`, as `ovl15:18E5` does) and is redrawn (`DrawPartyRoster`), and `ReselectCharacterPending` is cleared when that member is `ReselectCharacter`. Map corrections: `02A5`, `02D5`, `02DA` and `02DF` lie inside `StoreCommand` (the map had it at 19 bytes; it is 78), and the 22 entries from `02E4` to `0375` lie inside `SelectCharacterCommand` (212 bytes, not 4).
- **Opcodes 11 and 12 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`AddEncounterGroupCommand` `ovl03:0450`, `ShowEncounterCommand` `ovl03:03B4`; both matched): opcode 12 takes a sprite bank and a picture bank (kept in `EncounterSpriteBank` and `EncounterPicBank`, passed to `ShowEncounterPicture`) and a largest distance, which it stores in word 704 of `GameVars2`; it shows the encounter picture at the steps the party can see ahead (`OpenStepsAhead`, which also stores them in word 705), cut down to word 704. Opcode 11 adds a group: operand 1 is the kind `LoadCharacter` loads (`LoadMonster`), operand 2 the number of members (at least one), operand 3 the block of the `CPIC` combat picture loaded into the icon slot `EncounterIconSlot`. The loaded character goes to the tail of the party list `PartyListHead`, then the rest of the group follows, each a copy with its own item and affect lists (prepended, so the lists come out reversed); every member gets the icon `EncounterIconSlot`, which then goes up by one. The members are counted in `EncounterMemberCount` (`EncounterMemberCount`), and the command adds none at 63. Its icons start at 8 (`InitScript` sets `EncounterIconSlot` to 8), while `AddPartyMember` gives party members icons 0 to 7, so the group is an encounter's combatants, not the party [I]. The map had 757 bytes for opcode 11 and six entries inside it (`0745`, `074A`, `0775`, `07A5`, `07AA`, `0803`), none a routine; it is 967 bytes with its 5-byte literal.
- **Opcodes 13 to 15 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`ApproachEncounterCommand` `ovl03:0817`, `ShowCloseUpCommand` `ovl03:0856`, `ReadNumberCommand` `ovl03:0951`; all matched): opcode 13 has no operands. While word 705 of `GameVars2` (the steps the party sees ahead) is above 0, it takes one off and redraws the encounter picture at that distance (`ShowEncounterPicture`) [I: the script reads this as the picture coming nearer]; EclPC moves past the command on every path. Opcode 14 has one operand, a picture bank. $FF hides the close-up: when `AreaViewKind` is 1 or `PreviousAreaViewKind` is above 1 and a close-up or the 3D sprite is up (`CloseUpShown` or `Sprite3DLoaded`), `ShowAreaView` draws the area view again and those flags clear (`CloseUpHidden` is set); both `PictureShown` flags clear. Any other bank draws the close-up as `ShowEncounterPicture` does, after clearing the picture window (3, 3 to 13, 13) unless `VarForceWallView` is set: the PIC picture (`LoadSpriteList`, `DrawSprite` of `PictureAnimation`) when word 737 of `GameVars2` is $FF, else the HEAD and BODY pictures (`ShowHeadBody`). Opcode 15 decodes two operands but reads only the second: it asks the player for a number (`ReadNumber`, empty prompt, colour 10) and stores the word at the script address the second operand names (`WriteVmWord`). The map had three entries inside the code of opcodes 13 and 14 (`0845`, `084A`, `08E5`); they were not routines and are removed.
- **Opcodes 16 to 20 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`ReadStringCommand` `ovl03:0994`, `ShowTextCommand` `ovl03:0A09`, `ReturnCommand` `ovl03:0A9F`, `CompareBothPairsCommand` `ovl03:0AED`; all matched): opcode 16 asks for a line of text (`ReadInputLine`, colour 10, at most 40 characters, an empty prompt) and stores it as a string at the script address of operand 2 (`WriteVmString`); an empty answer is stored as one space. Opcodes 17 and 18 show the text of operand 1 in the text window of columns 1 to 38 and rows 17 to 22 (`ShowPagedText`, colour 10): a number is first turned into its text by `WordToText` and kept in `EclStrings[1]`, a string operand is already there. Opcode 17 leaves the window and the cursor as they are; opcode 18 clears the window and puts the cursor at column 1, row 17. `DelayPerGlyph` is 1 while the text is shown, which makes `DrawTextRun` wait after each glyph. Opcode 19 returns from a `ScriptGosub` call: EclPC moves past the command and, when `ReturnStack` is not empty, takes the return address of its top entry (`ReturnPC`), frees the entry and makes the entry below it the top. Opcode 20 compares operand 1 with 2 and operand 3 with 4 as words; when both pairs are equal `CompareFlags[0]` (=) is set, else `CompareFlags[1]` (<>), and the six flags are cleared first. Opcodes 22 to 27 (`ovl03:0B52`) test these flags. Purpose unknown: what the script compares this way. The map had three entries inside `CompareBothPairsCommand` (`0B39`, `0B43`, `0B48`, parts of its jump and move instructions); they are removed, and the routine is 101 bytes, not 76.
- **More opcodes (ovl03)** **[V]** (`RunIfCompared` `ovl03:0B52`, `StartEclBlock` `ovl03:0BD1`, `LoadAreaGraphics` `ovl03:0C74`, `BitwiseCommand` `ovl03:0DEF`, `CopyIndexedWordCommand` `ovl03:0E5E`; all matched): opcodes 22 to 27 run the next command only when their flag of CompareFlags (=, <>, <, >, <=, >=) is set, else `SkipCommand` steps over it. Opcode 32 saves WallViewMode in word 242 of GameVars, sets it to its operand (a block number), builds `GameDir + 'ECL' + TileSet + '.dax'` (the path goes to `SaveAndExit` first, whose job is not known yet), loads that block with `LoadEcl` and resets the machine with `InitScript`. Opcodes 33 and 55 load the area graphics: 33 the 3D map (`Load3DMap`, word 197 of GameVars) and the 'BACPAC' picture set, 55 the wall sets of three slots (`LoadWallSet`), a slot given as $FF makes its two words of `WallSetSlotPairs` $FFFF. Opcodes 47 (and) and 48 (or) combine two word operands into a byte, compare it with 0 and write it to the third operand. Opcode 42 copies the word at the table address (operand 1) plus a byte index (operand 2) to operand 3. Purpose unknown: what words 231, 232 and 713 of the game variables control, and what sets AreaStepRedrawOnce.
- **Opcodes 21, 28, 29, 43 and 53 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`ChooseFromListCommand` `ovl03:0F08`, `ClearEncounterCommand` `ovl03:125D`, `ComputePartyValueCommand` `ovl03:12C6`, `AskMenuCommand` `ovl03:10D1`, `StoreIndexedWordCommand` `ovl03:0EBC`; all matched; the dispatcher `ovl03:2F2D` tests `CurrentOpcode` with `cmp al`, the call of each follows its test): opcode 21 shows a list menu: its first string operand is the prompt, shown in the text window (columns 1 to 38, rows 17 to 22), and operand 3 gives the number of lines, the next string operands; `AskListMenu` (ChooseFromList) lets the player pick one and the chosen index is written to operand 1. Opcode 43 asks a menu: operand 2 is the number of options, each a string operand; they are joined with '~' before each (the hotkeys AskMenu reads), and the 0-based position of the key pressed goes to operand 1 ($FF for a key that is no hotkey). A single option 'PRESS <RETURN> OR BUTTON TO CONTINUE' becomes 'PRESS <ENTER>/<RETURN> TO CONTINUE' and Return answers it. Opcode 53 writes operand 1 as a word at the table of operand 2 plus the index of operand 3, the store that opcode 42 reads back. Opcode 28 (no operands) clears the encounter: `EncounterMemberCount` and `EncounterMembersAdded` go to 0, the icon slot `EncounterIconSlot` back to 8 (as `InitScript` sets it), `PooledMoney` is cleared and the item list `PooledItems` is freed line by line (63 bytes each). Opcode 29 computes one byte over the party list: for each member the current hit points (`HpCurrent`, +$11B), `ArmourClassValue` above 60, `HitAdjust` above 39 and the class levels 0 and 5, weighted 1, 5, 5, 8 and 4, divided by 10, its low byte added to the byte written to operand 1. Purpose unknown: what the script does with that value. The map had two entries inside these routines: `ovl03:0FE2` is the loop tail of opcode 21 and `ovl03:1307` lies inside opcode 29; both are removed, and the map sizes were short (218 and 65 bytes, now 381 and 273). The byte add of opcode 29 is `Inc(Checksum, ...)`, which compiles to the `add byte ptr` the original uses; `Checksum := Checksum + ...` widens it and is 9 bytes longer.

- **Opcodes 30, 34, 35 and 36 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`ScanPartyCommand` `ovl03:1435` with its nested `WriteScanResults` `ovl03:13D7`, `CheckPartyHasRangerCommand` `ovl03:1629`, `RollSurpriseCommand` `ovl03:16C9`, `RunAreaEventCommand` `ovl03:179B`; all matched; the dispatcher tests `CurrentOpcode` with `cmp al` for each): opcode 30 scans the party list. Operand 1 of zero makes an affect search (`HasAffect`, operand 2 the affect id) whose result goes to operand 6, with 0 for the other three values. Operand 1 as the script address $6BA7 (`ThiefSkills[3]`) or $6C1B (`Movement`) writes the lowest, highest and average of that stat over the party to operands 3 to 5 and 0 to operand 6; the average divides by the member count, so an empty party divides by zero. Opcode 34 sets operand 1 to 1 when a member's class mask (+$2F) is 4 (Ranger) or 10 (Cleric/Ranger) [I: a ranger is in the party], and writes 0 to operand 2 (its Mark byte is set and never read: QUIRK `ovl03:1629`). Opcode 35 rolls two d6 against Width (operand 4 + 2 - operand 1) and Height (operand 2 + 2 - operand 3) and writes an outcome from 0 to 2 to word 715 of GameVars2 (script address $6DCB, `VarSurpriseSides`): the surprise roll of the two sides [I]: `StartCombatantRound` (ovl13:185) takes the initiative penalty off the party side for bit 0 and the monster side for bit 1, and `RunCombat` clears the word (ovl08:94). Opcode 36 is the area logic that `ShopMain` (ovl06) and `RunCombat` (ovl08) name as their caller: with no encounter pending (`EncounterMembersAdded` set by opcode 11, or `DuelMode`) it serves the shop request (word 876 of GameVars2) or else the temple request (word 738), and otherwise runs `EndBattle`; with an encounter it runs `RunCombat` and then `EndBattle`, loads the BACPAC set and the ICON pictures, and clears `DuelMode`. Both paths then set the view mode `ViewMode` to 3 (4 when `AreaViewKind` is 1) and clear the close-up flags. The map had an entry inside `RollSurpriseCommand` (`ovl03:1758`, a piece of its code), which is removed; the compiler's boundaries correct the sizes of `ComputePartyValueCommand` and `RollSurpriseCommand`.
- **Opcodes 37 to 40 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`SelectCaseCommand` `ovl03:18F8`, `AddLootCommand` `ovl03:19B0`, `LoseMoneyAndItemsCommand` `ovl03:1DB1`; all matched; the dispatcher tests `CurrentOpcode` with `cmp al` for each): opcodes 37 and 38 choose one of the script addresses that follow the command: operand 1 is the choice and operand 2 the count, and a second `DecodeOperands` reads the addresses, so operand I+1 is the address for choice I. Opcode 38 runs the chosen address as a subroutine (`ScriptGosub`); opcode 37 stores it in `EclPC`, whose other readers are not traced yet. Opcode 39 sets the seven `PooledMoney` values from operands 1 to 7 and adds loot to the list `PooledItems` from operand 8: a kind below $80 is block Kind of `ITEM<TileSet>.dax` (every 63-byte record is linked in), $FF gives nothing, and from $80 up it is a treasure table of Kind minus $80 rolls, each item built by `BuildTreasureItem` from two d100 rolls and, for some kinds, a d10 or d16 (the mapping is in the source); then `FormatItemLine` runs for every item of the list. Opcode 40 scales the money of the selected character (operand 1 = 0) or of every party member by (100 - operand 2) / 100 and loses items on a d100 roll at most operand 2 (`ScaleMoney`, `LoseItems`); operand 3 is not read. The map had two entries inside `AddLootCommand` (`ovl03:1B42`, `ovl03:1C13`, each in the middle of an instruction and without a caller): removed.

- **Opcode 41 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`EncounterActionCommand` `ovl03:1EF8`; matched): the encounter action menu. It shows the encounter the way opcode 12 does (sprite bank, distance limit in word 704 of `GameVars2`, picture bank, steps ahead cut to that limit), then asks COMBAT, WAIT, FLEE and either ADVANCE or PARLAY (`AskMenu`). ADVANCE is the fourth word only when steps remain and game variable 230 (`VarForceWallView`) is set; otherwise that word is PARLAY and a pick of it is choice 4. Operand 4 is the script address the outcome is written to (its two bytes, not `OperandValue`). Operands 5 to 9 are the kind of choices 0 to 4, and the kind selects the outcome table. The first three string operands are the prompt candidates; the prompt shown is the first non-empty one, searched from the candidate after the steps ahead and wrapping over 1 to 3 (QUIRK `ovl03:210A`: any other step count leaves the prompt unset). Operand 13 is the movement a flee needs, compared with the party's slowest; operand 14 is the monsters' movement, compared with the party's fastest. The word written is 0 with 'The monsters flee.', 1 for a fight or a refused flee, 2 for a flee, and 3 when a parlay path has no step left. A step closer, or 'Both sides wait.', asks the menu again. `SkipCloseUpIfNoPics` is set for the command, so `ShowEncounterPicture` skips the close-up unless monster pictures are on. The map entry `ovl03:238D` was the middle of this routine (no prologue; it jumps back into the menu loop): removed. The routine is 1712 bytes of code, not the map's 1173.

- **Opcodes 44, 46, 49, 50 and 58 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`ChooseAttitudeCommand` `ovl03:25CB`, `DamagePartyCommand` `ovl03:2775`, `ComparePartyItemCommand` `ovl03:266A`, `RedrawAreaViewCommand` `ovl03:2AA2`, `PauseCommand` `ovl03:271F`; all matched; the dispatcher `ovl03:2F2D` tests `CurrentOpcode` with `cmp al` for each): opcode 44 asks the player for an attitude from the menu HAUGHTY, SLY, NICE, MEEK, ABUSIVE under a blank title; operands 1 to 5 are the value of each attitude and the value of the chosen one is written to the address operand 6 names. Opcode 46 damages the party: operands 2 to 4 are a damage roll (dice count, sides, bonus); operand 1 holds the flags, whose bit 7 is the saving-throw mode of a targeted trap (bit 6 hits the whole party, bit 5 makes the hit unsaveable, the low five bits are the saving throw bonus and the low three bits of operand 5 the save kind) while with bit 7 clear operand 1 is the number of random hits, each stopped only by `ArmourClassValue`; operand 5's bit 7 picks the selected character as the target (else a random member) and its low three bits are the save bonus; saves use `SavingThrow` and `RollBeatsResistance` (ovl24) and each hit `DamageWithMessage` (ovl07). QUIRK `ovl03:2775`: with bit 7 clear bit 6 is not a mode and counts into the number of hits (64 or more when set). When no member has `Flag10D` (combat participation) set, `PartyDestroyed` is 1 and the window 'The END!' shows 'The entire party is killed!' for 3 seconds. Opcode 49 redraws the area view when the 3D encounter sprite (`Sprite3DLoaded`) is loaded and clears it. Opcode 50 searches the carried items of the party for the item type of operand 1 (`ItemType`, +$2E of each entry of `Items`, +$C8) and sets `CompareFlags` for the conditional opcodes 22 to 27. Opcode 58 pauses for the game speed (`DelayByGameSpeed`). Purpose unknown: what the attitude changes (44), which events use the party damage (46) and what 49 is for.

- **Opcodes 45 and 56, and the camp entries (ovl03)** **[V]** (`EngineActionCommand` `ovl03:2CCF`, `ShowSpecialScreenCommand` `ovl03:2E10`, `RunCamp` `ovl03:2DD3`; all matched): opcode 45 takes the two bytes of its operand as a word, biased by $7FFF, and runs the action that word picks: $2C90 redraws the area view (ShowAreaView) when `AreaRedrawArmed` is set and one of `CloseUpShown`, `Sprite3DLoaded`, `SpecialViewGate`, `PartyMovedByScript` or `HeadBodyWritten` is up, and records the event byte of the party's cell in `PartyCellEventByte`; $8000 and $8001 start a duel with or without the copy (SetupDuel); $BA03 plays `SoundStep` or `SoundFireball` by `ScriptSoundCode`; $C01E steps the party forward; $C018 takes the wall of the party's cell into `PartyCellWallId` when `AreaViewKind` is 1. Opcode 56 picks a screen by operand 1: 8 shows the ending narrative (ShowEndingNarrative, ovl18), 9 runs the camp. `RunCamp` runs the third script entry (`EclPreCampEntry`, one of the five entry addresses InitScript sets), the camp (MakeCamp, ovl15) and, when the camp leaves its EndCamp byte set, redraws the view and runs the fourth entry (`EclCampInterruptedEntry`). The map had three entries inside EngineActionCommand (ovl03:2D39, 2DBA, 2DCB), which are removed. Purpose unknown: what the script does with these actions and screens; operand 0 of opcode 56 runs `RunCharacterMenu`, not traced yet.
- **Opcodes 51, 52, 54, 57, 59 and 61 (ovl03)** **[V]** (`NewLineCommand` `ovl03:2AEB`, `PassTimeCommand` `ovl03:2AC1`, `AddComputerMemberCommand` `ovl03:2B7E`, `ChoosePartyMemberCommand` `ovl03:2B33`, `FindMemorizedSpellCommand` `ovl03:2C03`, `ClearPictureWindowCommand` `ovl03:2B14`; all matched; the dispatcher `ovl03:2F2D` tests `CurrentOpcode` with `cmp al` for each): opcode 51 starts a new text line (the cursor goes to column 1 of the next row and `GlyphRunPending` is cleared when set). Opcode 52 advances the game clock by its operand through `AdvanceGameClock` (digit 1, the one the camp's rest steps in fives); QUIRK `ovl03:2AC1`: the operand number is read from the frame byte that the routine has not set yet. Opcode 54 adds a computer-run member: `AddMonsterToParty` rolls the character of the kind in operand 1 and adds it to the party (`AddPartyMember`, `ovl17:1446`), then its `B84` byte becomes operand 2 halved with the high bit $80 (the morale of a member the computer runs, see `TCharacter`), and its side is 1 for kind $18 and 0 otherwise. Opcode 57 asks the player to choose a party member: it clears the ECL text window, copies the string operand 1 as the prompt and calls `SelectPartyMember` (mode 0, `SelectedCharacter` receives the member); the learning notes call that routine `SelectPartyMember` [H], its code is not traced here. Opcode 59 searches the memorized spells (`Spells`, slots 1 up to 82: the loop compares Spells[82] too) of the first party member for the value of operand 1 and writes the slot it finds, or $FF, to operand 2 and the member count to operand 3; QUIRK `ovl03:2C03`: the walk stops after the first member, because the search sets its exit flag on both outcomes, and the scan runs past the 21 spell slots into the record bytes after them. Opcode 61 clears the picture window (columns and rows 1 to 15, the frame of the close-up) and sets `GlyphRunInBox`. Purpose unknown: what the script does with the clock step (52), the chosen member (57), the spell slot and count (59) and the side and morale of the added member (54).

- **The script loop and the dispatcher (ovl03)** **[V]** (`RunScriptFrom` `ovl03:3171`, `DispatchCommand` `ovl03:2F2D`, `DrawGlyphRunCommand` `ovl03:2E7D`, `RunAreaStep` `ovl03:31B4`; all matched): `RunScriptFrom` sets `EclPC` to the entry address and, while `ScriptRunEnded` is clear (`EndCommand` and `StartEclBlock` set it) and `PartyDestroyed` is clear, keeps the previous `CurrentOpcode` in `PreviousOpcode`, reads the opcode at `EclPC` into `CurrentOpcode` and runs `DispatchCommand`, a `case` on that byte with one handler per opcode (opcode 31 and 62 and above have none). Opcode 60 draws a run of glyph words at the text cursor: each word's low byte plus 1 is a glyph (`DrawGlyph`, colour 15), and a word whose low byte is 0 or $FF ends the run; below the close-up box (row 13) with `GlyphRunInBox` set the run starts at column 2, row 5. `RunAreaStep` is one step of the area: the event byte of the party's cell goes to `PartyCellEventByte`, `AreaViewKind` is 1 with its old value in `PreviousAreaViewKind`, the entry `EclInitialEntry` runs, the map page kinds 2 to 4 come from `WallViewMode` ($19 to $1B) when `VarForceWallView` is clear (page 3 animates the doors), `ShowAreaView` draws, the entries `EclStepEntry` and `EclSearchEntry` run, and the step repeats while `StartEclBlock` set `ScriptBlockStarted`. Purpose of the entries and of `EclInitialEntry` is not traced. `RunWilderness` (`ovl03:32F8`, entry 1, called from the main program after the character menu; matched, 528 bytes) is the wilderness loop. It clears the area flags, restores `WallViewMode` from `VarSavedWallView`, sets `ViewMode` to 3 for map pages $19 to $1B when `VarForceWallView` is clear (else 4), loads the area script unless `SkipAreaScriptLoad` is set, and runs entry `EclInitialEntry`. Then it loops on `HandleWildernessKeys` until `PartyDestroyed` is set, which it clears on the way out. While word 714 of `GameVars2` is above 1 or the key is E, and the party lives: E runs the camp (`RunCamp`); otherwise the low bit of that word is saved in `SavedSearchMode`, the word is forced to 1, the area is drawn, entry `EclSearchEntry` runs and a step runs if `ScriptBlockStarted` is set, then the bit is restored. Otherwise entry `EclStepEntry` runs, and if no block started the wilderness menu runs and `SoundStep` plays when the party's cell changed (words 240 and 241 of `GameVars`, compared as ShortInt <> Word so the word is evaluated first). The map entries `ovl03:3379` and `ovl03:34AB` were a call operand and a compare inside this routine: removed. A `while True` around an if/else compiled 38 bytes longer, because it duplicated the key restart; the original is `repeat ... until PartyDestroyed <> 0` with that inner `while`. The initialisation part (`ovl03:3508`) is empty. `make match UNIT=ovl03`: unit complete.

### 5.2 The area view: wilderness blocks and the adventure screen **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl27`, `src/OVL27.PAS`)

The wilderness around Phlan is drawn as a 5x5 grid of area blocks. Every block covers 3x3 map
tiles and is one picture, drawn by `DrawCellPicture` (`ovl32:00F1`) at a cell position of the 3D view
window; the block at map position (column, row) comes from the area map, and entries 3 to 9 of
a fixed table can replace it while the game variables allow it (`DrawAreaView`, `ovl27:0000`).

- **The area map** at DGROUP `0x3648` (`AreaMap` in `src/POOLVARS.PAS`) is 36 rows by 44 columns
  of one-byte block codes **[V]** (row stride 44 at `ovl27:0040`; `ShowAreaView` clamps the view
  window to columns 0..39 and rows 0..31, `ovl26:0253..0287`, which is 44-5 and 36-5). The picture code
  drawn is the map byte minus one **[V]** (`ovl27:0049`). The shipped image fills the map with
  terrain codes; rows 34 and 35 are all `0x4E` (the letter N), and `ovl14:08BB` compares map bytes
  with `0x4E` specially **[I]**: N marks ground that draws no block. **[?]** Nothing in the code
  writes the map, so it is compiled-in data; whether the game rewrites parts of it at run time is
  open.
- **Nine fixed positions** are tested, and only entries 3 to 9 can replace a block: the table at
  DGROUP `0x0C30` (`SpecialBlocks`, ten records of Col, Row, Pic: Word; entry 0 is never read, its
  bytes are the tail of the previous table in the image). Entries 1 and 2 are compared with the
  block's column and row (`ovl27:006A`, index times 6 from DS:0C30) and then the `case` has no arm,
  so a match leaves the map code **[V]**. Entries 3 to 9 replace it with Pic minus one **[V]**
  (`ovl27:00B2`): entry 3 while word 396 is not 255; entry 4 while word 466 is
  nonzero, or bit 3 of word 380 is set, or word 415 is zero; entry 5 while bit 1 of word 416 is
  clear; entry 6 always; entry 7 while bit 0 of word 416 is clear; entries 8 and 9 while word 435
  is above 253. Entries 6 and 7 hold columns 165 and 157, beyond the 44-column map, so they can
  never match the window **[I]**: dead entries, or coordinates in a system not seen yet.
- **The party position on the area map** lives in the game variables, the word array the far
  pointer at DGROUP `0x49D2` (`GameVars`) leads to: word 195 is the column and word 196 the row
  **[I]** (`ovl26:0249` passes both minus 2, so the party block sits at the centre of the 5x5
  grid; `ovl26:023F` adds a per-zone column base from the byte table at `0x3C78`, whose entries
  for zones 2..4 are 0, 13, 26: three side-by-side strips of 13 columns **[V]** for the table,
  **[I]** for them being the wilderness zones). `ovl07:02E1` and `ovl17:1C20` index the same array
  dynamically **[V]**, consistent with the ECL scripts' variables **[H]** (coab `VmOpp.cs`).
- **`DrawAdventureScreen`** (`ovl27:0207`) frames the three windows of the adventure screen
  **[V]**: the 3D view rows 1..15 x columns 1..15, the side window rows 1..15 x columns 17..38
  and the text area rows 17..22 x columns 1..38, through `DrawWindow` (`03F1:02E8`), the
  window routine of res09 **[V]**: it clears the interior (`ClearWindow`), draws a frame from
  picture set `FrameSet` around it (`DrawWindowFrame`: the corner and edge pictures come from index
  `FrameSet*3+276` and are blitted by `Put8x8Symbol`) and centres the text on the top row
  (`DrawTextAt`). Its grid: a column is 16 pixels and a row 4 scanlines, 40x50 cells on a
  640x200 screen **[V]** (the multiplies in `ClearWindow`).
- QUIRK `ovl27:01CC`: `DrawAreaView` passes both cell coordinates as `BaseCol + 1 - 1` (and the
  same for the row): the additions cancel; the compiler must emit them to match the bytes. The
  fourth argument of `DrawCellPicture` is always 1 here (0 from `ovl31`) and the callee never reads it
  **[V]**.

#### 5.2.1 The area view dispatcher and the party marker **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl26`, `src/OVL26.PAS`)

- **The area view unit** **[V]** (`src/OVL26.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl26`: unit complete): the resident unit's view dispatcher calls `ShowAreaView` (`ovl26:016D`) for its view types 3 and 4 (`RedrawView`), and the wilderness map screen calls it after the text windows of `DrawAdventureScreen` (`DrawWildernessScreen`).
- **Two kinds of view** **[V]** (the `case` at `ovl26:018C`): `AreaViewKind` (DS:49FA) selects what to draw. Kind 1 is the 3D wall view: GeoLoad returns the event byte of the party cell (`EventByteAt`, wrapping the coordinates into 0..15; bit 7 marks an interior cell, 4.2.1), the wall id table `WallViewIdTable` (16 entries: two equal groups of eight ids 0,15,12,11,13,10,9,14) is indexed by the game state words at +$1FA (outside cell) or +$1FC (interior cell), the four wall view descriptors DS:6A60..6A63 go to `SetWallDescriptors` (a pure setter for the same globals), and `DrawWallView` draws the rest (5.2.2; the overhead wall map instead of the 3D walls while `WallMapMode`, DS:6AAC, is set; ShowAreaView clears it when the state word at +$1F6 is set). Kinds 2 to 4 are the map window: `DrawAreaView(LeftCol, TopRow)` draws 25 blocks of the 44-column map grid (DS:3648, stride 44 [V] from `ovl27:0040`), stepping 3 view cells per block (the source's `BlockCells`, the same 3x3 cells as above), and `DrawPartyMarker` follows.
- **Where the kinds come from** **[V]**: the area loader sets `AreaViewKind := 1` (`StartGame`), the encounter unit sets 2, 3 or 4 together with view type 3 (`RunAreaStep`, keyed on DS:84DC); the game state word at +$1CC forces kind 1. `MapBaseX` (DS:3C76) holds the base column of each kind: entries 2..4 are 0, 13 and 26 **[V]** (the map image), so the kinds are three pages of the 44-column grid; the window is the party position minus 2, clamped to columns 0..39 and rows 0..31, and the page base is added to the column.
- **The party marker** **[V]** (`DrawPartyMarker`, `ovl26:0000`): the marker cell is the party position within the window `ShowAreaView` clamped (0 and 1 stay at the edges, columns 2 to 41 / rows 2 to 33 become the centre cell 2, column 42 / row 34 become cell 3, column 43 / row 35 or more cell 4, ovl26:001F and ovl26:0043). PicLoad draws it (`DrawIcon`) with the icon set DS:6AAF and the six-frame walk animation `MarkerFrame` (DS:6DDC): frame mod 2 picks the mirrored half, frame div 2 + 9 the picture 9 to 11, and the frame steps modulo 6 after each draw. The wilderness keys H, M, P and K set the icon set to 0, 2, 4 and 6 **[V]** (`HandleWildernessKeys`, `ovl14:0989`, which also cycles it by +2/+4/+6 mod 8), and `WriteVmWord` maps a script value 0..3 to the same four (script address `$C04D`) **[V]**. The same byte is the party's facing: `StepPartyForward` (`ovl07:19DB`) moves the party north, east, south or west for 0, 2, 4, 6 and `WallIdAt` takes it as the direction **[V]**, so the marker icon shows the facing **[I]**.
- **Special view codes** **[V]**: while DS:84E5 is set, `ShowAreaView` refreshes descriptors 2 to 4 from DS:6F69 and DS:6F68 through three nested Byte functions (`WallDesc2FromCode`..`WallDesc4FromCode`, `ovl26:00C7..016C`): codes 8 and $41 give descriptor 0, code $0B gives 8, code 6 gives 1, everything else $0F, 7 and 6. The loaders and the script set the codes: `InitScript` sets $41 and 9 and sets DS:84E5, `StartGame` sets $DB and $E8 (ovl11:0435 lies inside StartGame, StartGame; whitepaper 3.2.2), and script writes to addresses `$C059` and `$C05F` through `WriteVmWord` set the code and DS:84E5 (section 5.1). What the descriptors select is **[?]** (they reach the screen through `DrawWallDescriptors` (`ovl29:0174`, matched: it draws all four of them through `FillPictureRect`, passing `@FillRectPalette` and `DrawBuf`), still not decompiled; the label strings near DS:0A26 include "1st Level" to "9th Level").

- **Special view codes** **[V]**: while DS:84E5 is set, `ShowAreaView` refreshes descriptors 2 to 4 from DS:6F69 and DS:6F68 through three nested Byte functions (`WallDesc2FromCode`..`WallDesc4FromCode`, `ovl26:00C7..016C`): codes 8 and $41 give descriptor 0, code $0B gives 8, code 6 gives 1, everything else $0F, 7 and 6. The loaders and the script set the codes: `InitScript` sets $41 and 9 and sets DS:84E5, `StartGame` sets $DB and $E8 (ovl11:0435 lies inside StartGame; whitepaper 3.2.2), and script writes to addresses `$C059` and `$C05F` through `WriteVmWord` set the code and DS:84E5 (section 5.1). What the descriptors select is **[?]** (they reach the screen through `DrawWallDescriptors` (`ovl29:0174`, matched: it draws all four of them through `FillPictureRect`, passing `@FillRectPalette` and `DrawBuf`), still not decompiled; the label strings near DS:0A26 include "1st Level" to "9th Level").
- **More game variables** **[V]**: besides the party position (words 195 and 196, above), `ShowAreaView` reads word 230 (+$1CC, forces the wall view), word 251 (+$1F6, asks for the overlay labels) and words 253 and 254 (+$1FA and +$1FC, the wall id table index for an outside and an interior cell) of `GameVars^`; their names are the `Var...` constants of `src/POOLVARS.PAS`. The words are read as constant indices of the same `TGameVars` array that `DrawAreaView` uses, and `GameVars^[VarMapX]` compiles to the same `les di; mov ax,es:[di+186h]` a record field did (both units match against the one declaration).
- **TP 6.0 idioms proven here** **[V]** (probes `build/probes/`, `make tpc`): a nested routine receives its parent frame as a hidden last parameter (the caller pushes `bp` right before `push cs; call near`) and its code is emitted just before the parent's body, so the matcher proves it inside the parent's pre-entry region and compares that range instruction by instruction; a `case` with an arm body longer than the short-jump range places the arm body at a distance with `je` plus a `jmp` to the next test (`ovl26:018C`); `x - 2` compiles to two `dec`s; `x mod 2` on a Byte uses signed `idiv` while `x div 2` uses `shr`; an empty `then` branch compiles to the branch and a `jmp` and is what `ovl26:001F` shows.

#### 5.2.2 The 3D view and the overhead wall map **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl29`, `src/OVL29.PAS`: unit complete)

- **Ten wall pieces** **[V]**: the 3D view is an 11x11 grid of 8x8 tiles at screen column and row 2..12. Every wall of a wallset is a list of 156 tile numbers cut into ten pieces (`WallPieceFirstTile`, `WallPieceWidth`, `WallPieceHeight` at DS:0C8C, 0C96, 0CA0): the front walls of the far, middle and near rows (pieces 0, 3, 6: 1x2, 3x4, 7x8 tiles), their left and right side walls (1/2: 1x4, 4/5: 2x8, 7/8: 2x11) and the edge of a far front wall (9: 1x2). The widths times heights add up to 156 exactly. `DrawWallPiece` draws one piece at its position (`WallPieceCol`/`WallPieceRow`, DS:0CAA and 0CBE, Integer) plus a sideways shift, skipping tiles outside the grid and tile number 0.
- **Painter's order** **[V]** (`DrawWallView`, `ovl29:05FD`): after the frame (`DrawWallDescriptors`) it starts two cells ahead of the party and steps back one cell per row: the far row looks at four cells each side (front walls 2 tiles apart, the edge piece where a front wall ends, then the side walls of three cells each side), the middle row at three cells (3 tiles apart), the party's own row at two (7 tiles apart). Front walls use the facing direction, side walls the left (Dir + 6) or right (Dir + 2) direction mod 8; the step tables DS:2736/273F give one cell. Off-map cells follow the wrap of 4.2.1.
- **The overhead wall map** **[V]** (`DrawWallMap`, `ovl29:0000`): while `WallMapMode` is set the view shows an 11x11 window of the 16x16 map around the party (window corner clamped to 0..5), one 8x8 symbol per cell, `0x104` plus 1, 2, 4, 8 for a north, east, south, west wall, and the party arrow `0x100 + Dir div 2`. The movement loop's A key toggles the mode unless game variable 251 is set (5.3). The map listed a second routine at `ovl29:0143`; it is the tail of `DrawWallMap` (341 bytes).
- **Idioms** **[V]**: a two-dimensional array index `A[Row, Col]` through a far pointer compiles to `Col` into `dx`, `Row * 16` into `ax`, `les di`, `add di,ax`, `add di,dx`, with the field offset in the displacement (`es:[di+0x300]`); a lower bound of 1 shows as a negative displacement (`es:[di-0x9C]`, `es:[di-0x3A8]` for `[1..3, 1..5, 0..155]`). In a sum of an array element and a `ShortInt`, `X[i] + S` loads `S` first (`cwde; mov dx,ax`) while `S + X[i]` does not; `if (F < 1) or (F > 3) then Exit;` is the `jb; cmp; jbe; jmp` guard at `ovl29:0E0F`.

### 5.3 The wilderness keys and commands unit ovl14 **[V]** (`src/OVL14.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl14`: unit complete)

Overlay 14 drives wilderness interaction for `RunWilderness` (`ovl03:32F8`): movement and view keys plus the Bash/Pick/Knock/Exit command menu on the area map.

- **Map corrections** **[V]**: the map split three routines into fragments (`ovl14_fn_077A` inside `073B`, `ovl14_fn_0889`/`08A2`/`08E5` inside `0818`, `ovl14_fn_0B48` inside `e2`); none has a `55 89 E5` prologue and aligned disassembly shows continuous code. The entries were deleted and the sizes corrected (`073B` 185, `0818` 354, `e2` 963) in `re/symbols.toml`.
- **No PIT read** **[V]**: the `E4 40` bytes at `ovl14:0B48` are the tail of `xor ah,ah` plus `inc ax` in the `(DS:6AAF + 2) mod 8` icon cycle, not `IN AL,40h`; per-routine disassembly from the true prologues shows no port I/O anywhere in the unit (the Entropy bullet is corrected above).
- **Signed bytes everywhere** **[V]**: the party cell `DS:6AAD`/`DS:6AAE`, the direction tables `DS:2736`/`DS:273F`, the record flag tables and the `Icon`/`Col`/`Row` parameters are all `ShortInt`, proven by `cbw` (not `xor`) widenings and signed `jb`/`ja`/`jle` comparisons (`ovl14:0696`, `069D`, `06A4`, `06D0`, `026A`); a `Byte` source compiles to the zero-extending forms instead (`make tpc` probes). Passing `ShortInt` to a `Byte` parameter is a type mismatch (Error 26), but `Byte` to `ShortInt` and `Integer` to either both compile.
- **The party list read by ovl14** **[V]** (`PartyListHead`, `DS:5D96`; `Rec` in `src/OVL14.PAS` is a `PCharacter`): the "code-table records" of earlier notes are the `TCharacter` records of the party list, chained through `Next` at +$104. Bash (`DoBash`) tests `Strength` (+$10, 18 = $12..$14 and the percentile `StrengthPercent` at +$16, 91..99 and 100 for 18/00); Pick (`DoPick`) rolls 1d100 against the thief skill `ThiefSkills[2]` (+$78) and needs `Status` (+$10C) 0; Knock looks for the memorized spell $1F in `Spells` (+$17, 21 slots; spell 31 Knock of `SpellTable`) and the menu gates are `ClassLevels[5]` (magic-user) and `ClassLevels[6]` (thief) above 0 (+$96, `AnyMemberHasClassLevel`). Identified in the P6 review from the field offsets against `TCharacter`; the earlier "owner [?]" is answered: the party and monsters (`ovl11` clears the head, `ovl03` and `ovl17` build the list).
- **The routines** **[V]**: `HandleWildernessKeys` (`ovl14:0989`, the entry) reads menu keys (A/C/V/E/S/L) and movement keys (H steps via `ClampPartyPosition`, P/K/M cycle the icon set by +4/+6/+2 mod 8) through `ReadPromptChoice`, and runs a smaller loop on map pages 2..4 (icons set directly to 0/2/4/6); `DoWildernessMenu` (`ovl14:0D6D`) builds the Bash/Pick/Knock/Exit menu from the `DS:6DDE`/`DS:6DDF`/`DS:6DE0` flags and the record checks, prompts through `ReadPromptChoice` and dispatches the key: in the `DoorKindAt` = 2 arm 'B' calls `DoBash`, 'P' calls `DoPick` (`ovl14:0ED6`) and 'K' calls `DoKnock`, with Knock offered when `FindMemberWithSpell($1F)` finds a record (`ovl14:0E14`), while in the = 3 arm 'B' calls `DoBash` and 'K' calls `DoKnock`, with Knock offered when `AnyMemberHasClassLevel(5)` is set (`ovl14:0F53`), but 'P' only clears the Pick flag (`mov PickMenuFlag,0` at `ovl14:1015`, no `DoPick` call); on success it moves with `MoveParty`, steps the map with `CheckMapCell` on pages 2..4, and always frees the `DS:6A5C` sprite list; `CheckMapCell` (`ovl14:0818`) reads the area-map cell ahead through a `case` (N `$4E` and the impassable ranges pass silently, river codes `$66..$6C`/`$72` print the stench message unless `GameVars^[VarRiverGate]` (word 418) is set, anything else moves there); `MoveParty` (`ovl14:073B`) steps with wraparound; `ClampPartyPosition` (`ovl14:068D`) steps with edge clamping; `FindSpellSlot`/`FindMemberWithSpell`/`AnyMemberHasClassLevel` search the records; `ClearIconBits`/`SetIconBits` clear/set an icon set's bit pair in the map cell byte through a 2-D array view (`GeoCells^.Cells[Row, Col]`, `array[0..15, 0..15]`).
- **TP 6.0 idioms proven here** **[V]** (`make tpc` probes): `with Rec^ do` spills the record pointer with `les di; mov [temp],di; mov [temp+2],es` and reloads it per field access; commutative `+` evaluates its right operand first (so `GameVars^[VarMapX] + step` and `X + MapBaseX` match and the swapped forms do not); a 2-D array access keeps the `$300` field offset as the displacement while a folded `+ $300` index does not; `case` on a value reused across labels keeps it in `al` (an if-chain reloads or compares direct); `if C then A else B` with a far B compiles to jump-if-NOT-C (short) plus a `jmp` to B, so the source polarity is read off the jump, not the layout; parenthesised 4-term `and` chains nest while 2-term chains (and nested single-statement `if`s) stay flat.

### 5.4 Items, money and shops **[V]** (`src/OVL06.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl06`: unit complete)

The shop is overlay 6 (`ovl06`, "Shop"). `ShopMain` (`ovl06:053E`) is its entry, called from
ovl03 when the party enters a shop. It marks the shop open (`DS:49F3`, read by res12), clears
the 28-byte money block at `DS:67F4` and `DS:6DC0`, redraws the shop (`res12:27D9`,
`res12:0767`), and loops on a menu (`ReadPromptChoice`) whose text comes from `TreasureLeft`:

- The full menu is "Buy View Take Pool Share Appraise Exit"; when `TreasureLeft` says there is
  nothing to take or share, the shorter "Buy View Pool Appraise Exit" is shown. One `case`
  dispatches the key: B buys, V views an entry (`ViewSelectedCharacter`), T takes (`TakeMoney`), P pools
  when the flag is clear (`PoolMoney`), S shares (`ShareMoney`), A appraises (`AppraiseGems`), E
  leaves, and G/O update the selected party member through `UpdateSelectedCharacter`. G selects the
  preceding member (wrapping from the head to the tail); O selects the next member, wrapping to the
  head. Other keys select the head **[V]** (`ovl19:27DF`). Leaving while money lies in the pool asks through `AskMenu` ("Do you want to
  go back and get your Money?", '~Yes ~No'); `AskMenu` returns the 0-based hotkey position, so
  the party leaves on No (1) and stays on Yes **[V]** (`dec ax` at `ovl07:1775`, `ovl06:053E`).
- The shop stock is the linked list at `DS:6810` of 63-byte item records, the `TMenuLine` type
  of `src/POOLVARS.PAS` (`TItem` is the same record): the item record of 4.2.5 in memory, with
  `Text` (a `string[40]`, the item name) at +0, `Flag29` at +$29, `Next` at +$2A, `Weight` at +$37,
  `Count` at +$39 and `Value` (the price) at +$3A **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl06`, field offsets
  by `tools/re/records.py`). `ChooseShopItem` (`ovl06:0030`) draws the window titled "Shop"
  (`DrawWindow`) and, for each entry, rewrites `Text` into a 30-character field with the name on
  the left and `Value` right-justified (a zero value becomes 1, `ovl06:0068`); it then runs the item
  picker `ChooseFromList` ("Items: " with a "Buy" button).
- `BuyItems` (`ovl06:0361`) takes the chosen item's `Value` as the price. It pays first from the
  selected character's money (`CharacterMoneyValueInGold(SelectedCharacter)`, a LongInt) and then from the money
  pooled in the shop (`MoneyValueInGold` on `DS:67F4`); if the price fits, the item is added to the
  character's items and the amount is deducted (`SetCharacterGold` for the character, `SetPooledGold` for the
  pool), otherwise it shows "Not enough Money.". `CharacterMoneyValueInGold` and `MoneyValueInGold` both sum five coin
  amounts, each multiplied by its LongInt rate from the table at `DS:0D24`, add 100 and divide by
  the LongInt at `DS:0D30` (`ovl19:28BE`, `ovl21:00A8`); the character's coins are five words at
  +$88..+$90, the pool's five LongInts at the start of the 28-byte block `DS:67F4`. Both deduction
  routines divide the amount by 5 and write five coin amounts (`SetCharacterGold` into the character,
  `SetPooledGold` into `DS:67F4`), and the money blocks hold seven kinds, the five coins first (5.4.1). Two 16-bit truncations are kept (QUIRKs): `BuyItems` keeps only the low word of
  `CharacterMoneyValueInGold`'s LongInt (`ovl06:03C7`), so a character with 65536 or more is compared and charged
  modulo 65536, and it passes only the low word of the pool's LongInt remainder to
  `SetPooledGold(Amount: Word)` (`ovl06:0449`), so a pool above 65535 is rewritten truncated; ovl04's
  `ChargeForCure` does the same (5.19).
- `AddInventoryItem` (`ovl06:0239`) appends a clone of an item to `SelectedCharacter^.Items`
  (+$C8, the character's carried items) and sets its var `Overloaded` to 1 when the item was
  refused, 0 when it was added (`ovl06:026D`, `ovl06:0277`). The matched `WouldOverloadInventory`
  (`ovl19:275B`) first calls `RecalcCombatValues`, then returns true when the character's byte at +$C7
  exceeds 15 or when `CarriedWeight` (+$102) plus the item's added weight exceeds
  `StrengthWeightAllowance(Character) + 1500`. Added weight starts at `Item^.Weight`; the code multiplies it
  by `Item^.Count` only when the count is nonzero. `AddInventoryItem` refuses the item and shows
  "OverLoaded" when the function returns true **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl19 FN=WouldOverloadInventory`,
  `ovl06:026D..0277`). The strength-capacity interpretation of `StrengthWeightAllowance` remains **[I]**.
- **The character sheet** **[V]** (`ShowCharacterSheet`, `ovl19:004A`, called by `ViewSelectedCharacter`, the View command, and by ovl16): it draws the window 1..38 x 1..22 and its name line (`DrawCharacterName`, with an "(NPC)" tag after it for a computer-run member, `B84` bit 7), then sex, race and age (`SexNames` `DS:0FEC`, `RaceNames` `DS:0F03`, `Age` +$30), the alignment (`AlignmentNames` `DS:0F53`, nine entries 0..8 through Chaotic Evil, the signed byte at +$A0), the class (`ClassNames` `DS:0D38`, 17 entries indexed by +$2F: 0..7 the single classes and 8..16 the real multi-class names Cleric/Fighter .. Magic-User/Thief **[V]**), the six ability lines (`ShowAbilityScore`, `ovl19:0901`: the score at +$10 plus the index, and for a Strength of 18 with a percentile the percentile in parentheses), the money (`ShowCharacterMoney`, `ovl19:050D`: each nonzero `Money` entry by its `MoneyKindNames` name), the class levels joined with '/', the experience, the combat block (`ShowCombatStats`, `ovl19:0608`: AC and HP through `DrawArmourClass` and `DrawHitPoints`; THAC0 as 60 minus +$110, `HitAdjust`, where the 60 is **[I]**; damage as dice count d sides with the bonus, bytes +$115, +$117 and +$119 that POOLVARS leaves in `Pad112`; encumbrance as `CarriedWeight`; movement as the +$11C byte doubled by affect $2A and halved by affect $27), the weapon and armor (`SlotItems[0]` and `SlotItems[2]`, with the armor label one column to the right: QUIRK ovl19:0467), the status (`StatusNames` `DS:1047`, nine entries 0..8 with Gone at 8, +$10C) and, when `ShowPortraits` is set and the tile set is 3, the head and body portraits (`ShowHeadBodyWindow`, ovl28). **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl19`). The movement affects are the reverse of the comment on `Movement` in POOLVARS, which says $27 doubles and $2A halves: the matched code doubles on $2A and halves on $27 **[V]**; the POOLVARS comment needs a human fix (its record is shared).
- **The View command and the item menu** **[V]** (`ViewSelectedCharacter`, `ovl19:0AB3`; `RunItemMenu`, `ovl19:0EF8`; `CanGiveUpItem`, `ovl19:0D70`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`): the prompt "View:" offers Items (I), Spells (S, when a spell is memorized), Trade (T, with money and outside view mode 5), Drop (D, with money) and Exit (E or Escape); the sheet is drawn again after Items, Spells and Trade. In view mode 5 (combat) `RestorePaletteColors0And8` runs on entry and `SwapPaletteColors0And8` on exit. The item menu lists the carried items under Ready (R, `ReadyItem`), Use (U), Trade (T), Drop (D), Halve (H, under 16 items), Join (J), Sell (S, view mode 1 only) and Id (I, view mode 1 only). Use needs a readied item (otherwise "Must be Readied") that `IsScroll` accepts, or one whose Affects[2] is above 0 and Affects[3] below $80, and then runs `UseItem`; it is not offered while game variable 229 (`VarNoCasting`) is set, and in view modes 2 to 4 or in combat for a combatant whose Pad2 is set. `CanGiveUpItem` runs before Trade, Drop and Sell: it refuses a readied item ("Must be unreadied") and, for a scroll with a pending scribe (bit 7 in Affects[1..3], section 5.22), asks "is it Okay to lose it?" after the message "<name> was going to scribe from that scroll". Drop shows "Your <item> will be gone forever" and asks "Drop It?" **[V]**.
- **Using an item** **[V]** (`UseItem`, `ovl19:1A8A`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`; the U key of `RunItemMenu` and `UseMagicItem`, `ovl09:03E4`): a scroll (`IsScroll` true) gives its spell by `ChooseSpellFromList` in its "on Scroll" list and is kept in `CurrentItem` (`CurrentItem`); any other item whose `Affects[2]` is above 0 and `Affects[3]` below $80 gives the spell `Affects[2]` and sets `ItemAffectUse` (`ItemAffectUse`), which shows the message "uses an item" with the item's line. A spell number above $38 is shifted down by $17 [V] (the code reads such an item byte as the spell plus $17; its purpose is [I]). The cast is `CastChosenSpell` with the character's `Quick` byte (+$10F, not `Flag10D`) [V]. In combat (view mode 5) a spell whose spell-table row has byte +$B set takes its result from `EndCombatTurn` [I]. A used scroll goes through `ConsumeScrollSpell`; an item with `Affects[1]` above 0 loses one of its count, or else one charge, and leaves the character (`RemoveInventoryItem`) with its last charge [V].
- **Selling an item** **[V]** (`SellItem`, `ovl19:1CEE`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`; the S key of `RunItemMenu` after `CanGiveUpItem`): the offer is half the item's `Value` in gold, and a stack of more than one item is offered its count times that, divided by 20 [I] (the $1C branch that would multiply without the division is never taken, QUIRK ovl19:1D1A) [V]. The screen shows "I'll give you N gold pieces for your NAME" on row 21 and asks "Is It a Deal?". On Y the item leaves the character (`RemoveInventoryItem`), "Sold!" is shown and the offer is paid in platinum (five gold each) and gold. `WouldOverload` (ovl21) checks the coins as weight: when they would overload the character, "Overloaded.  Money will be put in pool." is shown and the platinum beyond his room goes to the pool (`PooledMoney[4]`) [V].
- **Readying an item** **[V]** (`ReadyItem`, `ovl19:14D0`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`; the same routine serves the AI's item step, `ovl09:13CA`): a readied item is unreadied, a cursed one refusing ("It's Cursed"); otherwise the checks run and the last one that fails decides the message: the hands (`ds100` plus the item kind's `ItemTable[ItemType][1]` above 2 gives "Your hands are full!", not shown in combat for a quick-combat character), the body slot `ItemTable[ItemType][0]` (an item already in it gives "already using <name>", and the slot item is redrawn first; the kinds $49 and $1C also check slots 11 and 12, at +$F8 and +$FC), and the classes `ItemTable[ItemType][13]` allows against the character's `PadB0` ("Wrong Class"). `ItemTable` is indexed by item type, not race: the name is the one POOLVARS gives the table. A readied item is marked `Readied`, and its `Affects[3]` byte, when above $7F, is applied through `CallAffectHandler` with mode 0 (mode 1 when it is unreadied). The routine never stores the item in `SlotItems`: the slot pointers the sheet and the AI read are set elsewhere (not yet found) **[V]** (no store to +$CC..+$FF in its code). QUIRK ovl19:15BB: slot 9 is tested at `SlotItems[10]` (+$F4) but its "already using" message names `SlotItems[9]` (+$F0) **[V]**.
- **Trading an item** **[V]** (`TradeItem`, `ovl19:1734`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`; the T key after `CanGiveUpItem`): the screen is redrawn and "Trade with Whom?" picks the recipient through `SelectPartyMember`, starting from the viewed character (`ViewedCharacter`). No member picked, nothing happens; otherwise `ViewedCharacter` becomes the recipient, `WouldOverloadInventory` may refuse it ("Overloaded"), and the item then goes to the recipient through `AddItemCopy` and leaves `SelectedCharacter` through `RemoveInventoryItem`, the call the Drop action makes; the recipient's carried weight is recalculated (`RecalcCombatValues`). The two RES12 calls are known only by their pushes: `SelectPartyMember(Prompt, Mode, var Who)` and `AddItemCopy(Who, Item)` **[V]** (signatures from the callers' stack use; RES12 has no PCharacter in its interface, so `var Who` is a Pointer there).
- **Dropping money** **[V]** (`ChooseAndDropMoney`, `ovl19:24A7`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`; the D key of the View prompt, offered while the character holds money): the money window is drawn again, the coin kinds SelectedCharacter holds are listed under "Select type of coin" with their amounts, and "How much <kind> will you drop? " asks for the amount (`ReadAmount`, at most the money of that kind). `DropMoney` (`ovl21:0A01`) takes it away. The list is the one of the Trade command (`TradeMoney`, `ovl19:2156`) without a recipient; the list is built again while money is left. QUIRK ovl19:24EF: `Kinds` is counted and never read **[V]**.
- **Halving a stack** **[V]** (`HalveItem`, `ovl19:17ED`; the H key of the item menu): the item's Count (+$39) is halved: a new 63-byte record, a copy of the item with that Count, not readied and linked after it (the item's old Next moves to the new record), takes half and the item keeps the rest. A stack of one (Count below 2) gives "Can't halve that" **[V]**. The new record is never freed, as the code shows no FreeMem.
- **Joining stacks** **[V]** (`JoinItem`, `ovl19:189D`; the J key): every other item of `SelectedCharacter` that matches the chosen one (the same `NameNum[1..3]`, `ItemType`, `Plus`, `PlusSave`, `Cursed` and `Weight`, a nonzero `Count`, and `Affects[1]` below 2) is taken off the character (`RemoveInventoryItem`) and its count added to the chosen item, up to 255. When the total would pass 255 the chosen item is set to 255 and the match gives up what is needed, and the match becomes the item that gathers the rest. QUIRK ovl19:19A1: the `Affects[1..3]` tests compare the match with itself, so only `Affects[1] < 2` rejects a match **[V]**.
- **Identifying an item** **[V]** (`IdentifyItem`, `ovl19:1F52`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`; the I key of `RunItemMenu`): "For 200 gold pieces I'll identify your <item>" and "Is It a Deal? " take 200 gold from the character's money (`CharacterMoneyValueInGold`, then `SetCharacterGold`) or else from the pool (`MoneyValueInGold` on `PooledMoney`, then `SetPooledGold`); "Not Enough Money" when neither covers it. A paid item whose `HiddenNames` byte is set loses it, shows "It looks like some sort of <item>" and sets the list's redraw flag; otherwise it shows "I can't tell anything new about your <item>". The price is paid either way. The money is a LongInt, so the price is taken with `Dec` on the variable (`sub`, `sbb` on memory) **[V]**.
- **Trading money** **[V]** (`TradeMoney`, `ovl19:2156`; `make match UNIT=ovl19`; the T key of the View prompt): "Trade to?" picks the recipient (`SelectPartyMember`), kept in `ViewedCharacter`. The coin kinds the character holds are listed under "Select type of coin", each with its amount and its name right-aligned in eight columns, the list `ovl21` `TakeMoney` builds for the pool. The chosen kind is asked for ("How much <kind> will you trade? ", `ReadAmount`, at most the character's money of that kind) and `TransferMoney` moves it. The list is built again while money is left; the recipient is asked again when the list is left with money still held. QUIRK ovl19:2468: that test reads a flag the first pass never sets when the list is cancelled before any transfer **[V]**.

Map note: `re/symbols.toml` had split `ovl06:0239` at `ovl06:0311`; `0311` is one byte into a
`les di, [bp-8]` instruction, not a routine entry, and its stub was removed.

#### 5.4.1 Money: ovl21 **[V]** (`src/OVL21.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl21`: unit complete, 22 routines)

ovl21 ("Money") holds the coin arithmetic and the money commands the shop (5.4), the Temple (5.19)
and the treasure menu (5.20) share.

- **Seven kinds.** `TCharacter.Money` (+$88, seven words) and `PooledMoney` (`DS:67F4`, seven
  LongInts) are indexed alike: copper, silver, electrum, gold, platinum, gems, jewelry, the order of
  the name table `MoneyKindNames` (`DS:0FFA`, seven `string[10]`). `CoinCopperValues` (`DS:0D24`)
  values the five coins in copper: 1, 10, 100, 200, 1000. `MoneyValueInGold` (`ovl21:00A8`) sums the
  five coins in copper, adds 100 and divides by 200 (rounding to gold); gems and jewelry are not
  money until appraised. `SetCharacterGold` and `SetPooledGold` rewrite a gold amount as
  `Amount div 5` platinum plus `Amount mod 5` gold, clearing the other coins.
- **Weight.** Every coin weighs one unit of the carried weight `TCharacter.CarriedWeight` (+$102);
  `CarryLimit` is 1500 plus the strength allowance `StrengthWeightAllowance` (a table on the strength
  class: -350 for the weakest, up to several thousand), and `WouldOverload` tells whether Amount more fits and how much
  room is left. QUIRK `ovl21:04DB`: `TransferMoney` adds the coins' weight back to the giver instead
  of the taker.
- **Pool and Share.** `PoolMoney` (`ovl21:0508`) moves all the money of every member whose `B84` is
  0 or $B3 into `PooledMoney` and sets `MoneyInPool` (`DS:6DC0`). `ShareMoney` (`ovl21:063A`)
  divides each kind by the number of such members (`CountSharers`): each member below `B84` $80 takes
  his share, jewelry first, as far as his load allows, plus one coin of the remainder; the remainders
  then go to anybody who can carry them, and what is left stays pooled.
- **Take.** `TakeMoney` (`ovl21:0CEB`) lists the pooled kinds ("Select type of coin"), asks "How much
  <kind> will you take?" with `ReadAmount` (`ovl21:0294`: digits on row 24, Backspace, clamped to the
  maximum, Escape gives 0) and moves the amount with `TakeFromPool`. `DropMoney` puts dropped coins
  into the pool only in view mode 1 (shop, Temple) or 6 (treasure); elsewhere they are lost.
  `TreasureLeft` (`ovl21:0F7E`) reports pooled money and loot left for the menus.
- **Appraise.** `AppraiseGems` (`ovl21:19B2`) values one gem (d100: 10, 50, 100, 500, 1000 or 5000
  gp) or piece of jewelry (100 + Random(900) up to 2000 + Random(10000)) at a time; selling pays a
  fifth of the value in platinum (`GivePlatinum`, the overflow to the pool with "Overloaded.  Money
  will be put in Pool."), keeping makes an item of type $46 when the character can carry it and has
  fewer than 16 items. QUIRK `ovl21:1E74`: a character with no items loses the kept gem, and the
  kept item adds no weight.
- **Treasure items.** `BuildTreasureItem` (`ovl21:1010`, called from ovl03) fills a 63-byte
  `TMenuLine` for an item type: weapons and armour get a bonus of 1..3 (`RollItemBonus`, d20) with
  their name numbers, weight and value; types $3D and $3E get 1..3 random affects; other types take
  a fixed row of eight words of `TreasureItemRows` (`DS:0970`).

Map note: `07D1`, `0B48`, `0F21`, `1758`, `1F4B` and `2233` were inside routines (`ShareMoney`,
`TakeFromPool`, `TakeMoney`, `BuildTreasureItem`, `AppraiseGems`) and their entries were removed.

### 5.5 Combat: the round loop and the player's commands **[V]** (`src/OVL08.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl08`: 11 of 13 routines at L2)

- **Rounds and turns** **[V]**: `RunCombat` (`ovl08:007A`) runs rounds until one side is gone (DS:6814 or DS:6815 zero). In a round every character of the party list DS:5D96 (which holds every combatant, monsters included) gets `StartCombatantRound`, then `PickNextCombatant` repeatedly picks the character with the highest positive initiative (`TCombatant.Initiative`, the combat state at character +$108, signed), ties broken by a 1d100 roll, and `CombatTurn` plays it; the round ends when nobody has an initiative above 0. Delay sets the initiative to 1 (act last).
- **Round movement** **[V]** (`ComputeMovement`, `ovl13:0123`, called by `StartCombatantRound` at the start of the round and by `CheckMorale`, `TryFlee` and `FastestEnemyMovement`): it takes the character's `Movement` (+$11C), adds the party movement word (882 of `GameVars2^`, byte offset $6E4) for a party member, sets the result to 1 when it is below 1 or above $60 (**QUIRK** `ovl13:0161`: an over-fast combatant gets the slowest move, not the cap, so a port clamping to $60 would give it the fastest), doubles it into half squares in DS:681A, runs the movement hook kind $12 (which may change DS:681A again) and returns DS:681A; `StartCombatantRound` stores the return in `Combat^.Moves` (+$6, half squares).
- **Who plays** **[V]**: a character with `Quick` (+$10F) set is played by the computer (`QuickTurn`), the others by the player (`PlayerTurn`). Quick sets it (`SetQuickCombat`, which also forgets a remembered target on the character's own side, +$10E is the side), Space clears it for every character with byte +$84 below $80, and Alt-Q makes everybody quick. A pending spell (+$108^.+0) is finished through `CastChosenSpell` instead of a turn.
- **Commands** **[V]**: the menu is Move (while half-moves are left, +$108^.+6, shown halved), View Aim, Use (item count +$C7), Cast (a memorized spell at +$17..+$2B, the combat flag +$108^.+1 and game variable 229 zero), Turn (cleric level, not turned yet), Quick Done. Done opens Guard, Delay, Quit, Bandage, Speed, Exit; Speed is the GameSpeed (0..9) menu of DS:49E2. The keys accepted besides the letters are the scan codes of Alt-Q, Alt-R, Alt-X and Alt-M (Magic On/Off, DS:6E30), with the key flag of `ReadPromptChoice` set.
- **Movement** **[V]** (`MoveCharacter`): the keypad scan codes H, I, M, Q, P, O, K, G are the directions 0 to 7 clockwise from north; a step towards a combatant attacks it (`MeleeAttack`: Not with that weapon, else `ConfirmAttackTarget`/`SweepEnemies`/`e11`/`e12`), a step off the map asks Flee:, a step onto ground whose cost (`CombatTiles`, DS:2744, 4-byte entries; 5.5.1) is above the half-moves left says Blocked. Esc takes the move back.
- **Parting shots** **[V]** (`PartingShots`, `ovl13:08B2`, called by `MoveCharacter` (ovl08) and `MoveStep` (ovl09) just before the step): collects the enemies adjacent to Who before the move (`CollectEnemiesInRange(Who, 1)` into DS:6DE4), temporarily shifts Who's cell in `CombatantTable` (DS:5F27) by `DirStepX`/`DirStepY[Dir]` to collect the enemies adjacent at the destination, shifts it back, and keeps the enemies adjacent before but not after, the ones Who leaves behind. For each of them still in the fight (`Flag10D` set), not `IsHelpless`, accepted by `AttackTargetAllowed(Enemy, Who)` and carrying neither affect $4A nor $4B, it scans the directions `Combat^.IconSet + 6` to `+10`: an enemy with an initiative above 0 or not being drawn (`BF` 0) attacks at once, otherwise only when `InDirArc` (ovl30) faces Who (direction = the scan offset mod 8). It marks the enemy's unused attack slot in the two bytes at +$113/+$114 (slot 2 for a monster whose `Attacks` is 0), stores it in `Combat^.Pad4[4]`, and `PerformAttack(Enemy, Who, 1, nil, CombatScratchFlag)` makes the attack; the enemy's remembered `Combat^.Target` is restored and, while Who is still in the fight, its panel is redrawn. **Map correction** **[V]**: the entries `098F`, `0999`, `0B2A`, `0B2F`, `0B3E`, `0B48`, `0B4D`, `0B57`, `0B5C` and `0B61` were inside this routine; the compiler's boundaries removed them (their `{@stub}` lines are gone too).

the attack is refused when a hook sets DS:681E (a nil target fails, attacking oneself passes). `ResolveCombatantAttacks` (`ovl13:1406`, the routine `PerformAttack` wraps; the map's `14A1`, `1583`, `1713` and `1758` entries lie inside it) takes a helpless target with an automatic kind-3 hit (`ClearAttackerAffect`, both attack slots cleared), else switches the readied weapon to its large-foe dice when the target is big (CombatSize above $80 or a footprint kind above 1), runs hook $B on the target after `RecalcCombatValues`, works out the needed to-hit value (`RollToHit`; a turn affects at most six. `AttacksThisRound` (`ovl13:0E52`) halves the doubled attack budget in DS:681A, adding the odd unit on the even-numbered combat rounds (DS:6DE3). **Map correction** **[V]**: the entries `0FBF` and `104E` were inside `SweepEnemies` and `11D8` inside `TurnUndead`; It then animates the picture along the line between the two cells (`WalkCombatLine`, one frame for the missile, four for an icon, two for the rest) between the launch sound `SoundAttackLeaves` and the impact sound `SoundAttackArrives`. The bytes-per-item word of the combat picture `CombatAnimationPicture` is quartered for the text adapter and never read (QUIRK `ovl13:26DD`).
- **Choosing an attack target** **[V]** (`ConfirmAttackTarget`, `ovl13:2998`, `MeleeAttack` calls it before an attack): the attack goes ahead without a question when Target is on the side opposing Who (`OpposingSide`) or Who is run by the computer (`Quick`); otherwise the player is asked `Attack Ally: ` (`AskYesNo`), and a no refuses the attack. A yes also turns the fight into a brawl: `AllyAttackConfirmed` and word 819 of `GameVars2^` are set, every party member the computer runs (Status 0, `B84` above $7F) is moved to the monster side (`Side` 1) and loses its remembered `Combat^.Target`, and `RecountCombatSides` (res12) refreshes the side counts.

Compiler note **[V]**: the string literals under `$O+` go into stack temporaries (`StrCopy`) whose addresses are the pushed arguments, and a concatenation allocates a 256-byte `string[255]` temporary; probe `tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl13/PRB2AA0.PAS`.
- **The manual aim cursor** **[V]** (`RunAimCursor`, `ovl13:2DFA`, the loop `RunAimTargets` runs to pick a target): it clears the aim record (`FillChar`), puts the combat cursor on the current target's cell and loops until a key ends the aim (`while not (Key in [#0, #13, 'E', 'T'])`). Each pass moves the cursor a step in `Dir` (`MoveCombatCursor`, `DirStepX`/`DirStepY`, clamped to 0..$31 by 0..$18), reads the cell (`GetMapCell`), walks a line from the shooter (`WalkLine`, steps halved) and, when `Automatic` is set, shows `Range = ` with the halved distance (`WordToText`, `DrawTextAt`) or clears the line on a blocked walk. The cell's occupant or the body lying there (`BodyTable`) becomes `Target` and its panel is drawn. The aim is legal only when the halved distance is within `Range`, the tile is passable (`CombatTiles[Tile].Cost <> $FF`), the target passes `AttackTargetAllowed` and, for a ranged weapon with `Automatic` set, `ChooseAttackItem` finds ammo that can melee (`RangedWeaponCanMelee`) any adjacent enemy; with no target, `AimFlag` 0 makes it illegal. The prompt `Center Exit` (prefixed `Target ` when legal) is read under the title `(Use Cursor keys) ` (`ReadPromptChoice`, flag `CombatScratchFlag`): Enter or `T` records the cell and target in `Aim` and, when `Automatic` is set, resolves the attack through `ResolveAimedAttack` (else sets `Done`), H/I/M/Q/P/O/K/G set `Dir` 0..7, `#0` or `E` cancels (redraws the cell and clears `Aim`), `C` re-centres the cursor and any other key clears `Dir`. **Map correction** **[V]**: the entries `2DC8`, `2F57`, `2F7B`, `2FD3`, `30FB` and `31D7` were inside this routine; the compiler's boundaries removed them (their `{@stub}` lines are gone too). Compiler note **[V]**: `Inc(CurX, DirStepX[Dir])` is an 8-bit in-place `add byte` where `CurX := CurX + DirStepX[Dir]` widens to a 16-bit signed add; a sparse `case` loads the selector once (`mov al,[Key]; cmp al,...`) where an if-chain compares memory each time; probes `tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl13/PRBDIRS.PAS`.

When only the enemies are gone and DS:49F4 is 0 the game asks Continue Battle:.
