#### 5.5.3 The computer's turn ovl09 **[V]** (`src/OVL09.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl09`: unit complete, 14 routines and one proven nested)

- **The turn** **[V]** (`QuickTurn`, `ovl09:000F`, the entry, called by CombatTurn/PlayerTurn for a character with `Quick` set): reads a key (`HandleCombatKey`), clears the prompt line, ends the turn through `EndCombatTurn` when the character has left the fight (`Flag10D` 0), rerolls the step pattern (`StepPattern`, +$15) when it is 0 or above 4 or a d4 comes up 1 (a d8 gives the new pattern, an 8 a d2 + 4, else a d4), and runs `CheckMorale` when no key was pressed; a panicking character (`Panic`, +$14 set) that is not forced to flee (`ForcedFlight`, +$10 0) shows 'flees in panic' through `ShowCharacterMessage`. While the turn lasts it then uses a magic item (`UseMagicItem`), finishes a pending spell (`Combat^.PendingSpell` through `CastChosenSpell`), takes the Turn action (`TryTurnUndead`, Turn Undead for a cleric: `TurnUndead`, the Turn that CombatMenu offers, `ovl08:0795`), casts a memorized spell (`CastMemorizedSpell`), uses the best item (`UseBestItem`) and reads keys again, looping on `ChooseCombatTarget(Who, $FF, 1, 0)`: an initiative above 0 runs `AiTurn`, else the turn ends through `EndOrGuardTurn`.

- **Magic first** **[V]**: `UseMagicItem` (`ovl09:03E4`) walks the character's items for a castable one (`Affects[2]` the spell number, `Affects[3]` below $80 and `IsScroll` false) and uses it through `UseItem`; `CastMemorizedSpell` (`ovl09:0545`) collects the memorized spells, keeps those `SpellAccepted` accepts and casts one through `CastCombatSpell`. `UseMagicItem` gives up at once when `CanUseMagicItem` (the combat byte +$108^.+2) is zero, when the actor's side count `SideCounts[OpposingSide(Who)]` is zero, or when game variable 229 (`VarNoCasting`) is set. `CastMemorizedSpell` collects the memorized spells only when `CanCast` (+$108^.+1) is nonzero, tries them only when the Magic toggle `MagicToggleOn` is on (Alt-M) or the character is computer-run (B84 above $7F) and its side count is above zero, and reads neither `VarNoCasting` nor `CanUseMagicItem` (CombatMenu hides its Cast offer with `VarNoCasting`). Both roll `RollDice(1, 7)` for the number of rounds (UseMagicItem's Range starts at 7 and falls by one per round; CastMemorizedSpell's level does the same). `SpellAccepted` (`ovl09:02EB`) is the range test they share: it refuses when the spell row's byte +$D is below the requested range, accepts a spell other than 3 whose +$E byte is 0, asks `FindNearbyTarget(Who, Done)` for spell 3, and otherwise collects Who's enemies the area pattern of `SpellAreaSize(Spell)` reaches (`CollectEnemiesInRange`, count `Tries`; the indexes land in DS:6DE4, from 1) and refuses when the nested `DifferentSideInArea` (`ovl09:0255`: `ScanTargets` around each collected enemy with the row's +$F byte as the line budget) finds a side unlike `OpposingSide(SelectedCharacter)`; a zero count or a zero +$F byte accepts.
- **Morale** **[V]** (`CheckMorale`, `ovl09:10D7`): clears `Panic` (+$108^.+$14), then a nonzero `ForcedFlight` (+$108^.+$10) forces flight ("is forced to flee" through `ShowCharacterMessage`, `Panic` set) and nothing else is checked. A character with B84 at or below $7F is not checked. Otherwise the morale is DS:6825 = `(B84 and $7F) * 2` (zeroed above $66), changed by the hook kind $11. The first test holds when that morale is below the percentage of hit points lost (100 minus `HpCurrent` * 100 div `HpMax`; `HpCurrent` is the current hit points) or is zero. Then DS:6825 takes DS:6E2F, the hook runs again, and the second test holds when it is below 100 minus `GameVars2^[Var2Morale]` (word 710 of the second game word block, $6B00..$6EFF, not `GameVars^[710]`), or is zero, or the character is on the party side (side 0). Only when both tests hold is there a roll (`FastestEnemyMovement` against half of `ComputeMovement`): at or below half, `Panic` is set and the affects $4A and $4B are removed (the character flees and the turn goes on); a higher roll with Intelligence above 5 surrenders (`DropCombatant(Who, 4, 'Surrenders')`, the end of the turn); otherwise nothing happens. So a party character rolls whenever the first test holds, and a monster of side 1 rolls only when both tests hold. `UpdateMonsterMorale` (`ovl13:2879`, called by `StartBattle` before the first round and by `EndCombatRound` every round) refreshes DS:6E2F from the monster side: it walks the battle's character list DS:5D96 and sums the maximum hit points (`HpMax`) of every combatant with `Side` 1 and the current hit points (`HpCurrent`) of those still in the fight (`Flag10D` set), then stores `20 * current div maximum * 5` (a monster out of the fight keeps its maximum counted and its current hit points out of the sum, so the percentage falls as the monsters are defeated).
- **Choosing an item** **[V]** (`ScoreItem`, `ovl09:128D`): the score of a candidate item is `DiceSizeNormal * DiceCountNormal` of its row in `ItemTable` (DS:5582, 129 rows of 16 bytes over the loader's file buffer, `TItemRow`; the field names follow coab's ItemData as `tools/re/items.py` decodes them [H]), plus eight times its `Plus` when `Plus` is above 0, plus twice `BonusNormal` when that is above 0, plus `(Attacks - 1) * 2` when bit 3 (`FlagAttack`, $08) of the row's `Flags` is set, plus 3 when `Hands` is at most 1. It is 0 when `Hands` plus the actor's `HandsInUse` (+$100) is above 3, when `Affects[3]` is $84 and the low nibble of `Affects[2]` differs from the actor's `Alignment`, when `Affects[2]` is $53, or when the item is cursed. An item of type $55 scores 8 when the actor's target's `Pad74[$76]` byte is above 0. The $84 and $53 rules and the $55 case are Purpose unknown. `UseBestItem` keeps the highest score (its attack-pick test, `ovl09:1543`).
- **The AI's item step** **[V]** (`UseBestItem`, `ovl09:13CA`, called by `QuickTurn` every turn and by `AiTurn` before attacking): takes the `Hands` of the slot-0 and slot-1 items off `HandsInUse` (+$100). It then scans the carried items: a slot-kind-0 item whose `ClassMask` shares a bit with the character's `ClassBits` (+$B0) is scored by `ScoreItem(Who, item)`; an item with `FlagAttack` ($08) or `FlagSlotItem` ($10) that scores above `AttackScore` (starting at 1) becomes the attack pick, an item without `FlagAttack` that scores above `OtherScore` the other pick (`OtherScore` starts at the character's own attack, `BaseDamage[1] * BaseAttacks[1]` plus twice `BaseDamageBonus[1]` when that is positive), and among the slot-kind-1 items the best by `Plus + 1` is `PlusPick`. The item to use (`Candidate`) is the attack pick when it scores above half of `OtherScore`, its flags name a slot (`FlagSlotItem`, or `FlagSlot11` ($01) and `FlagSlot12` ($80) with `FlagAttack`, which pick `SlotItems[11]` and `SlotItems[12]` at +$F8 and +$FC), and either `BothKinds` (range above 1 and flags $14 both set) or no enemy stands within one step (`CollectEnemiesInRange(Who, 1)`); otherwise the other pick. The slot-0 item (unless it holds the pick or is cursed, which skips the whole slot block), the slot-1 item (its `Hands` taken off again when it is not cursed) and the pick are readied through `ReadyItem` with `RecalcCombatValues` and `RefreshAttacks`. `HandsInUse` above 2 readies the slot-1 item (or the pick when slot 1 is empty or cursed); below 2 it readies the slot-1 item and then `PlusPick`, unless slot 1 holds `PlusPick` or a cursed item. A use ends the step through `DrawCharacterPanel`. Whether the slot items are the weapon and the armour is not proven here.
- **Stepping** **[V]** (`CanAffordStep`, `ovl09:0675`): attempt `Column` of the actor's step pattern turns the base direction by `StepTurnOffsets[StepPattern, Column]` (`StepPattern` is +$108^.+$15) (DS:02AE, 7 rows of 5, rows 1 and 2 are drawn by `RollDice(1, 2)` at `ovl09:08CD`) modulo 8; `ProbeStep` looks at the cells, a tile of 0 reports off-map and returns, and the step is affordable when no combatant is in the way, the tile cost is below the moves left and the step's cost (tile cost times 2, times 3 on a diagonal; `Moves + 1` on `TileKept` without one of the affects $20, $1E, $6F, $7D) is at or below the moves left.
- **The AI's move step** **[V]** (`MoveStep`, `ovl09:07F2`): shows `Move/Attack, Move Left = Moves div 2` through `DrawTextAt(0, 24, 10, ...)` and lets `HandleCombatKey` interrupt (True ends the step). It gives up through `EndOrGuardTurn` when no half-move is left, the initiative is 0, or a magic-user (`ClassMask` 5) is not panicking (`Panic`, +$108^.+$14; the combat record is $16 bytes, `CombatRecordSize` in ovl10). The direction comes from `BearingTo(Who, Combat^.Target)` while clear of panic, else from the party marker facing `PartyFacing` (+ 4 on the party side, mod 8) with a fresh `RollDice(1, 2)` step pattern (`StepPattern`). It tries pattern columns 1..5 through `CanAffordStep` (a panicking actor off the map ends through `TryFlee`), refuses a step back (`AiLastDirection` = (Turn + 4) mod 8), advances the pattern when refused (`AiStepTurns` counts) and may end the turn through `ChooseCombatTarget` or `EndOrGuardTurn`. A step taken faces the actor (`SetCombatantIcon(Who, Turn, 0, 0)`), moves through `PartingShots` (or `StepCombatant` once `Flag10D` is set past it) and `ApplyCloudTile`, an ended turn through `EndCombatTurn`.
- **Keys during the computer's turn** **[V]** (`HandleCombatKey`, `ovl09:0FC1`): Alt-M toggles Magic On/Off (DS:6E30), Space marks the character for quick combat, Alt-X asks `GodsIntervene`.
- **The AI's turn loop** **[V]** (`AiTurn`, `ovl09:0B3C`, returns 1 when the turn is over): clears the AI scratch (DS:443C/D/E), runs affect hook $0E (`HookTurnStart`), gives up at once when the initiative is 0, and loops: while `Panic` is set it keeps calling `MoveStep` until the moves run out or the initiative leaves 1..`InitiativeQuickAll` ($13); an initiative of 0 or $14 ends the turn without an action, and only then does it attack. The attack range is `ItemTable[Byte(SlotItems[0]^.ItemType)].Range - 1` (the item in slot 0 at +$CC; a result of 0 or $FF becomes 1) and the remembered `Combat^.Target` is dropped when it is not dying (`Flag10D`) or is on the party side. QUIRK `ovl09:0C8E`: that `Combat^.Target` is dereferenced (`Flag10D`, `Side`) before its nil test at 0CAC, so a character with no remembered target reads through a nil pointer. A target within line of sight (`WalkLine`, step count halved) is attacked, else a random enemy of the `CollectEnemiesInRange(Who, Range)` scan (DS:6DE4), or `UseBestItem` chooses one when `IsRangedWeapon` holds, `RangedWeaponCanMelee` does not and a 1-step scan finds an enemy. The attack runs `SweepEnemies` (ends the turn through `EndCombatTurn`), `NoteAttackBearing` and `PerformAttack` with a second pointer filled by `ChooseAttackItem`; after 20 turns it gives up through `EndOrGuardTurn`.
- **Map corrections** **[V]**: the entries `06AA`..`0718` were inside `CanAffordStep`, `0893`..`0B34` inside `MoveStep`, `0B48`..`0B96` inside `AiTurn` and `1583`..`1758` inside `UseBestItem`; the compiler's boundaries removed all four sets (their `{@stub}` lines are gone too). `ovl09:0255` is not a unit-level routine but a nested routine of `SpellAccepted`, proven through the parent's match.

#### 5.5.1 The combat map view ovl31 **[V]** (`src/OVL31.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl31`: unit complete, 23 routines)

- **The map** is a block behind the far pointer `CombatMap` (`DS:6716`): a 7-byte header, then 25 rows of 50 tile ids (`+7 + 50 row + column`). Header `+2`/`+3` are the map cell shown at the top left of the 7x7 cell view, `+4` makes the cursor icon visible, `+5` is the cursor's footprint kind, `+6` is the tile gate ovl30's `WalkLine` reads (5.18). A view cell is drawn with `DrawCellPicture` at 3 times its column and row; the picture is byte `+3` of the 4-byte combat tile entry at `DS:2744` (`CombatTiles`, the same table ovl30 reads from `DS:2745`). Byte `+0` of the entry is a cost: `$FF` refuses a combatant, and when a footprint covers several tiles the one with the largest cost decides a step (`ProbeStep`); tile 0 (off the map) and tile `$1E` always win **[V]** (code; the table bytes give `$FF` or 1 for the costs and picture = tile id - 1).
- **Combatants** are numbered 1..n: `CombatantTable` (`DS:5F27`, 4 bytes each: X, Y, an unread byte, the footprint kind; entry 0's kind byte is n), `CombatantRecord` (`DS:65BD`, far pointers to their character records), their place in the view `CombatantViewCol/Row` (`DS:604B`, `DS:6093`) and the occupant map `CellOccupant` (`DS:60DB`, 50x25 bytes, rebuilt by `RebuildOccupancy`). The layout leaves room for 72 combatants **[I]** (the tables are packed back to back). A **footprint kind** (`Footprints`, `DS:2844`, four cell offsets per kind) is 1 for one cell, 2 for two cells one above the other, 3 for two side by side and 4 for a 2x2 square **[V]** (table bytes); `PlaceCombatant` takes it from bits 0..6 of the character record's byte `+$6C`, and the icon `DrawIcon` draws is the record's `+$BF` with the icon set at `+9` of the combat record the character points to at `+$108` (`TCombatant.IconSet`, the byte ovl08's MoveCharacter saves and restores around a move taken back) **[V]**. ovl08 and ovl31 share one combat record type, `TCombatant`.
- **The view and the cursor**: `ScrollViewTo` moves the window's centre one cell at a time until a cell is within a margin of it (columns 3..46, rows 3..21) and redraws all 49 cells; `MoveCombatCursor(X, Y, Margin, Dir)` steps by the direction tables `DirStepX`/`DirStepY` (direction 8 does not move), scrolls, redraws the combatants, repaints the old cell and draws the cursor (icon `$19`) on every cell of the cursor footprint. `ShowCursorOn` shows it on a combatant with that combatant's footprint **[V]**.
- **Death and bodies** (only in view mode 5, `ViewMode`): `RemoveCombatant` draws the picture `DS:5F1A` over the dying combatant's cells, calls the sound unit (`PlaySound(SoundCombatantDies)`) and waits, and unless `NoBody` (`+$13` of its combat record, which also keeps ovl08's Bandage away) is set records a body (`BodyTable`, `DS:66DD`, 8 records of who, X, Y and the tile it covered; count `DS:6715`) and puts tile `$1F` on its cell (a `$1E` tile stays). `PlaceCombatant` with its Restore flag takes the body back and restores the tile. QUIRK `ovl31:0E41`: the check for an existing body record reads `BodyTable[SharedLoopIndex]`, a stray global (FreeSpriteList's loop counter), on every pass instead of the loop index **[V]** (code).
- `SwapPaletteColors0And8` and `RestorePaletteColors0And8` call `SetPaletteRegister` with (0, 8), (8, 0) and (0, 0), (8, 8) when `VideoAdapter` is 1; their purpose is **[?]**.

#### 5.5.2 The start of a battle ovl10 **[V]** (`src/OVL10.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl10`: unit complete, 16 routines at L2 and 7 proven nested)

- **Sequence** **[V]**: `StartBattle` (`ovl10:1E31`, entry 1, called by `RunCombat`) frees the picture animation and the HEAD/BODY sprites, then resets `HeadBankIndex` and `BodyBankIndex` to $FF. It prints `A battle begins...` on row 24, clears the round state (DS:6E30, DS:6DE3, `AttackRoll`, `BodyTable`, the lists DS:6DBB and DS:5D9A, word 819 of the second game word block), caps word 710 of that block at 100, then runs `CreateCombatMap`, discards waiting keys, prepares combatants, discards waiting keys, places them, and discards waiting keys again. It allocates the picture DS:6E36, puts the view origin 3 cells up and left of the first character of the party list, calls `DrawCombatScreen`, runs affect hook 8 for every character, calls `UpdateMonsterMorale` and sets view mode 5.
- **The map** **[V]**: `CreateCombatMap` loads the 24x24 sets `DungCom` (AreaViewKind 1, the 3D view) or `WildCom`, then `RandCom`, allocates the $4E9-byte `CombatMap` block, clears its cursor flag, sets its cursor kind to 1, clears the Head6 tile gate and builds it. The last `Load24x24Set` argument is not read (`ovl10:1244`). A **wilderness** map (`BuildWildernessMap`) starts all tile $17 (picture $16, the plain ground the scatterers look for), takes the area map block under the party (`AreaMap[VarMapY, VarMapX]`, the column moved by 13 or 26 for AreaViewKind 3 and 4) into `CombatBlock` (DS:465A), and `BlockTerrainFlags` maps that block code through a fixed range table to eight flag bits. A river (`PlaceRiver`, tiles $32/$33 one column right per row from a top column 34 - 5d4 moved left to the next column with (column + 2) mod 7 = 0; bridge tiles $34/$35 from the nested `PlaceBridge` on a d20 of 1 and always on the row after a bridge, or on one row of 7..15 when no bridge fell) appears on 35 or 75 in 100 (bits $20, $10), two-cell features (tile $24..$29, with $20..$23 above it for kinds 1..4) on up to 7 in 100 per ground cell (bits 2, 4, $40, 8; none for $80), single features from five cumulative chances out of 255 (tiles $2A..$2D, $2E..$31, $37..$39, $39..$3C, $3D..$41), and rare tiles $1A..$1D on d100 rolls of 98 and up (`ScatterRareTiles`). Bit $80 becomes bit 1 while game word 435 is $FF (the word ovl27 gates area blocks 8 and 9 with). A **dungeon** map (`BuildDungeonMap`, 0810) draws each area map cell within 6 columns and 2 rows of the party as a skewed block of the combat map, 6 columns right and 5 rows down per area column and row (cell (I, J) starts at column 6I + 5J + 21, row 5J + 10), from its west, north and east passages (`PassageKind`, 01AD: the OR of the nested `WallSideKind` of this side and of the neighbour's opposite side; a side is 1 a wall when `DoorKindAt` is 0, 0 open when `WallIdAt` is 0, else 3 a door; off the 16x16 map the east and west sides on the party's row (`PartyMapRow`) count as open, every other side as a wall).
- **Combatants** **[V]**: `PrepareCombatants` gives every character of the party list (DS:5D96, monsters included) a zeroed $16-byte `TCombatant` (`+$108`), sets `NoBody` for entries past the party size (word 830, `VarPartySize` of ovl05) and the starting icon set `FacingCombatDir[PartyFacing]` (DS:02F2: 7, 2, 3, 6; turned by 4 for side 1). A party-side `NoBody` member whose low-seven-bit B84 morale is 0 or greater than $66 becomes computer-run with B84 set to word 710 plus high-bit flag $80 [I]. `CheckMorale` (`ovl09:10D7`) reads `(B84 and $7F) * 2` as its morale threshold and zeroes values above $66. `PlaceAllCombatants` clears CombatantTable entries 1..255 even though the battle has at most 72 combatants (QUIRK `ovl10:0872`).
- **Placement** **[V]**: each side has an 11x6 grid in four groups (`PlacementCells`, DS:4440, 2 x 4 x 6 x 11 bytes), each group the column spans `PlacementShapes` (DS:0306) of the side's facing (group 1: shape 4). Side 0 stands at origin (0, 0) facing the party (`PartyFacing`), side 1 word 705 area map steps ahead facing back (`SideOriginCol/Row`, `SideFacing`, DS:4650..4657). Grid cell (C, R) of origin (X, Y) is combat map cell (C + 6X + 5Y + 22, R + 5Y + 10), the same skew as the dungeon blocks. `FindStartCell` walks rings of the grid from `RingStartCol/Row` (DS:02F6, DS:02FE), the first ring `FirstRingCells` long (half the side's count DS:6814/6815, rounded up), later ones 12, and falls back to groups 1..3 one area map step away in an open direction (`FacingWallDirs`, DS:02D2); `TryPlaceAt` takes a free grid cell whose map cell has no occupant and a passable tile (`ProbeStep`). QUIRK `ovl10:1434`: TryPlaceAt stores candidate X/Y before probing; a candidate rejected by ProbeStep leaves those attempted coordinates in CombatantTable[Index]. A character with `Flag10D` 0 is taken off again and leaves a body; one that finds no cell and has `NoBody` is cut out of the party list (`RemoveSelectedCharacter`).
- **QUIRKs** **[V]**: `IsBeyondGrid` (`ovl10:1403`) answers True only when a cell is off the grid in both directions. `BlockTerrainFlags`' ordered case table (`ovl10:08AA..0B14`) has eight overlaps, with the first listed range winning: 3, 4 and 7 map to 3; $28, $29 and $2B map to 1; $7C maps to 8; and $F5 maps to $20. For a block code in no range, `Flags` is unassigned and the routine returns that unset byte (QUIRK `ovl10:0B68`).
- **Dungeon drawing** **[V]**: `BuildDungeonMap` (0810, matched with its five nested routines once the matcher proved a nested routine called only by a sibling, `push word ptr [bp+6]; push cs; call`, 82cbb26) reads `PassageKind` of the west, north and east sides of every cell and draws them through `SetDungeonCell` (0221: picture + 1 into `CombatMap^.Cells[5J + 10 + SubRow, 6I + 5J + 21 + SubCol]`, skipped off the 50x25 map). `DrawFloorAndWest` (02BA) fills sub-rows 2..4 of sub-columns 0..5 with picture $16 and draws a west wall as the diagonal 4, 3, $D down those rows or a west door as pictures 8 at (1, 2) and 0 at (5, 4); `DrawNorthSide` (0377) draws sub-columns 3..4 of sub-rows 0..1 as 5 over $A for a wall, else floor; `DrawNorthWestCorner` (040F, sub-columns 1..2) and `DrawNorthEastCorner` (0615, sub-columns 5..6) pick their four pictures in a `for K := 1 to 4` case from North, West or East and the far sides of the corner (`PassageKind` of the cell to the north and of the cell to the side; both open is an open corner), the compiler proof of every picture table. `PassageKind` and `PlaceRiver`, whose nested routines the parent calls itself, match since the nested comparison of `c7265b6`.
- **Map corrections** **[V]**: the map listed 32 routines; 10 of them (`06FF`, `07AA`, `0B2F`, `0B48`, `0BEE`, `1582`, `1758`, `1A1F`, `1A91`, `1B83`) are inside the 22 real ones.

### 5.6 Class statistics: ovl23 **[V]** (`src/OVL23.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl23`: unit complete)

Overlay 23 recomputes what a character draws from its eight class levels (`TCharacter.ClassLevels`, +$96..+$9D). Its four routines are called by ovl12, ovl16, ovl17 and ovl22.

- **`RecalcClassStats`** (`ovl23:0000`) **[V]**: over classes 0..7, `Thac0` (+$2D, cleared first) becomes the largest `ClassThac0Table[class, level]` (DS:3C7C, 11 bytes per class) and `HighestLevel` (+$73, signed) is raised to the highest level (never lowered there). Class 2 (fighter) sets `Attacks` (+$A1) to 3 above level 6, else 2. Class 0 (cleric) above level 1 copies `ClericSlotTable[level, 1..3]` (DS:40BD) into the cleric row of `SpellSlots` (+$B2..+$B4; `SpellSlots[class 0..1, level 1..3]`, +$B2..+$B7, ovl15 indexes both rows), adds the wisdom bonus (`AddWisdomBonusSpells`) and sets `KnownSpells[n]` (+$33..+$6A) to 1 for every row n of `SpellTable` (DS:320A, 16-byte rows, spells 1..$38) whose byte 0 is 0 and whose level (byte 1, signed) has a slot; entries are only ever set. Class 5 above level 1 copies `MageSlotTable[level, 1..3]` (DS:41D5) into the magic-user row of `SpellSlots` (+$B5..+$B7). Then `ComputeSavingThrows`, and `ComputeThiefSkills` when the class 6 level is above 0.
- **`AddWisdomBonusSpells`** (`ovl23:01AF`) **[V]**: one more level 1 slot at Wisdom 13 and at 14, level 2 at 15 and 16, level 3 at 17, each only for a level that already has a slot: the AD&D wisdom bonus spells, which is why +$B2..+$B4 are read as cleric slots **[I]**.
- **`ComputeSavingThrows`** (`ovl23:0255`) **[V]**: five bytes at +$6D start at 20 and take the minimum of `SavingThrowTable[class, level, n]` (DS:426E, 8 x 9 x 5 bytes) over the classes with a level: the five saving throws **[I]**.
- **`ComputeThiefSkills`** (`ovl23:0329`) **[V]**: eight bytes at +$77 are `ThiefSkillTable[thief level, n]` (DS:3CF4) plus the signed `ThiefRaceTable[Race, n]` (DS:3D3C), plus the signed `ThiefDexTable[Dexterity, n]` (DS:3D4F) for the first five; a negative race value larger than the base gives 0. Eight skills with a dexterity adjustment on five is the AD&D thief table **[I]**.
- **Class indices** **[I]**: 0 cleric and 2 fighter are confirmed elsewhere (5.5, 5.19); 5 magic-user and 6 thief follow from the tables they select here.
- **Idioms** **[V]**: a table indexed `T[row, column]` with several dimensions compiles to the last index into a register first, then each earlier index times its row size, accumulated in `di` (`mov di,ax; add di,cx; add di,bx`), with the table's base as the displacement; the same arithmetic written as one computed index of a flatter array accumulates in `ax` instead, which is what blocked the earlier drafts. A table whose columns count from 1 is addressed from the byte before it, so that byte is declared as its own global (`ClericSlotTableBase`, `ThiefSkillTableBase`, ...) to make the index base name the same thing in our layout. `with Table[i] do` on a global array element spills the element's far address (`add di,base; mov [t],di; mov [t+2],ds`) and reloads it with `les di` for every field. In `A + B` of two table elements the right operand is loaded first (`ovl23:03CD`).

### 5.8 Keyboard input and menus (observed in the oracle)

- Input is **INT 16h only** **[V]** (oracle: no INT 09h hook, ports 60h/64h never touched): Crt `KeyPressed` (`0A40:02FD`, AH=01h) and `ReadKey` (`0A40:030F`, AH=00h, an extended key returns 0 then the scan code on the next call).
- The resident unit res04 is the keyboard layer and the way out of the game **[V]** (`src/RES04.PAS`, `make match UNIT=res04`: unit complete):
  - `ReadGameKey` (`0899:0059`) is the `ReadKey` game code calls. Ctrl-S (`#19`), when `SoundMode` (`DS:5372`) is not 2, saves that byte in `SoundModeBeforePause` (`DS:5373`), calls `PlaySound` (`08AE:00DC`) with `SoundStopAll` (`DS:2612`) and sets `SoundMode` to 2. A later Ctrl-S resumes only when `SoundMode` is already 2 and `SoundModeBeforePause` is in `[0, 1]` (`System_SetIn` at `0899:008B`): it copies the saved byte back and calls `SilenceSound` (`08AE:017B`). Any other second Ctrl-S does nothing. What 0 and 1 are is not shown here **[V]** (the bytes); reading the pair as a simple on/off switch was too strong. When `ParamStr(1) = 'STING'`, Ctrl-D (`#4`) toggles `DebugMode` (`DS:49F7`) and Ctrl-C (`#3`) quits through `QuitGame`: a developers' debug mode **[V]** (code), its effect **[?]**. Those tests use the first key. Then, unless that key is `#0` (the extended-key prefix), `while KeyPressed do Key := ReadKey` (`0899:00E4`) reads every key still waiting into `Key`, and the function returns that last key, not the first with the rest discarded **[V]**. The oracle's `key` script command therefore waits for the game to be idle.
  - The debug mode also shows traces: `ShowDebugTrace(Message: String40; Max, Current, Lost: Byte)` (overlay 33, matched as a whole) does nothing unless `DS:49F7` is set; then it clears the top text line (`ClearRect(0, 0, 39, 0)`: columns 0 to 39 of character row 0), shows the three numbers as `Max , Current , Lost` in colour 14, shows the message through `ShowMessageLine` (also colour 14, and it waits for a key, 5.15), and clears the line again **[V]**. Its only caller is `ovl12:2233` (the training and rewards unit), which passes the label `'max,cur,lost'` and a character's maximum, current and lost hit points (`hp_max` at +$32 of the character record, 4.2.5, and the bytes at +$11B and +$75), in that order.
  - `FlushKeyboard`, called before prompts (the code wheel, 5.12), is meant to flush the keyboard but returns at once: its first statement is `Exit` and the flush loop after it is dead code (QUIRK, `0899:010A`).
  - `DiscardWaitingKey` (`0899:0122`) calls `ReadGameKey` once when a key is waiting and throws the result away, so Ctrl-S and the STING keys still run. `ReadGameKey`'s drain can still consume keys past that one.
  - `QuitGame` (`0899:0000`) ends the program: it copies `SoundModeBeforePause` into `SoundMode` and calls `PlaySound(SoundStopAll)`, the pause-path call, not `SilenceSound`. What that call does to the sound is not shown in this unit; the sound unit's proven stop is `08AE:13B1` (5.14) **[V]**. Then `Intr($10)` with AH=0 and AL = `SavedVideoMode` (`DS:4A1C`), through the `Registers` record at `DS:4A02`, and `Halt`. No writer of `SavedVideoMode` is in the decompiled units, so where that byte was saved is **[?]**.
- The menu unit is ovl25 (`src/OVL25.PAS`) **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl25`: `unit: complete`, 4682 bytes; GetMenuLine, CountMenuLines, ReadPromptChoice with its nested SplitPromptWords and DrawPromptWords, ClearPromptLine, ChooseFromList with its nine nested routines LastHeadingBefore, DrawListPage, FirstNonBlank, LastNonBlank, HighlightListLine, UnhighlightListLine, SkipHeadingLines, ScrollListPage and MoveListCursor, AskYesNo, NewMenuList, FreeMenuList and the empty initialisation part matched. Three of ChooseFromList's nested routines, FirstNonBlank, LastNonBlank and SkipHeadingLines, are called only by sibling nested routines, which pass their own static link, still the parent's frame (`push word ptr [bp+6]; push cs; call`, `ovl25:098D`, `09A2`, `0CB4`); the matcher proves such a callee from the proven sibling since commit 82cbb26). The pieces:
  - **The prompt line** `ReadPromptChoice` (`ovl25:03BD`) draws a title in column 0 of text row 24 and a row of words after it (`"Heal Exit"`, `"Yes No"`). Its nested `SplitPromptWords` (`ovl25:00CB`) cuts the words at every capital letter or digit (the set `['0'..'9','A'..'Z']` at `ovl25:00AB`), assuming one separator, up to 20 words; `DrawPromptWords` (`ovl25:01DA`) draws them a character at a time through `DrawGlyph`, capitals and the chosen word (`PromptWordChoice`, `DS:6B2B`, kept between calls) in the hot colour, the rest in the word colour. Keys: `,` and `.` step the chosen word with wrap-around; a capital or digit that begins a word chooses it and is returned (after `UpCase`); Space returns `' '`; Enter returns #13 only when one of the two colours is nonzero; Escape returns #0. An extended key (ReadGameKey's #0 prefix) is read and dropped unless the caller passes `ExtKeys`; then its scan letter is returned with the var `ExtKey` set to 1, and the keypad digits 1..9 are turned into the scan letters of the keys they carry (`'OPQK MGHI'`, `KeypadScanLetters`, `DS:288C`; `'\'` gives `'7'`). On return `ExtKey` is copied into `LastPromptExtKey` (`DS:6B2C`) and game variable 511 is cleared **[V]** (code).
  - While it waits, the prompt **animates the picture** of the sprite list at `DS:6ABE` (`PictureAnimation`: a count byte, the current frame, then 8-byte frames of a Longint delay and a sprite pointer, the same bytes `LoadSpriteList` fills): it draws the current frame at column 3, row 3 (`DrawSprite`) and steps to the next after `Delay * 10` GameClock hundredths, that is `Delay` tenths of a second, when the caller passes `Animate` or game variable 511 is nonzero (then it also steps every pass). A Longint `PromptTimeout` (`DS:6B2E`) above 0 makes the prompt return `PromptTimeoutKey` (`DS:6B32`) after that many hundredths, counted from the last animation frame (QUIRK `ovl25:0530`) **[V]** (code); who sets the timeout is **[?]**.
  - **The list menu** `ChooseFromList` (`ovl25:0D9A`) shows a list of 46-byte menu lines (`NewMenuList`/`FreeMenuList`, the Text, Flag29 and Next part of `TMenuLine`) in a window; a line with `Flag29` set is a heading, drawn in the first colour and skipped by the highlight. The first shown line is `ListMenuTop` (`DS:6B29`, kept between calls). The prompt is the caller's words plus `" Next"`/`" Prev"` when the list goes on below or above the page and `" Exit"` when asked; End and Home (scan letters `O`, `G`) move the highlight down and up inside the page, PgDn/PgUp and the words Next/Prev turn the page, Escape, `E` and #0 return #0, and any other key returns itself with the highlighted line in `Selected` and its 0-based number in `Choice` **[V]** (code; the End/Home behaviour matches the oracle's character creation lists). The arrow keys have no effect in these lists **[V]** (oracle: UP/DOWN/LEFT/RIGHT/TAB/SPACE leave the screen unchanged): `ChooseFromList` handles only the scan letters G, O, I, Q **[V]** (code, `ovl25:0FF4..102A`).
  - `AskYesNo` (`ovl25:10D1`) asks with `"Yes No"`, the highlight on No, until the key is Y or N; `ClearPromptLine` (`ovl25:06FB`) blanks row 24 **[V]**.
- Character creation flow **[V]** (one run of `tests/sessions/create-character.keys`): main menu (C) > race list > sex > class > alignment > rolled character sheet with "Keep this character? Yes No" > name prompt > "Head Body Keep" > icon editor ("Parts Color-1 Color-2 Size Exit") > "Is this icon OK? Yes No" > main menu. The game writes `SAVE\RAD.CHA` (285 bytes, the character record of 4.2.5) and `SAVE\CHARLIST.TXT` (`RAD`, CR LF) **[V]**; decoding `RAD.CHA` with `tools/re/records.py` gives the race, class, sex, age and six scores shown on the sheet (human 7, fighter 2, male 0, age 19, STR 15 INT 13 WIS 11 DEX 12 CON 16 CHA 12 in the pinned run).
- The Create command is `CreateCharacter` (`ovl16:056C`, 6502 bytes) **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl16`; the map's 57 entries `07E1`..`1EC6` inside it were fixup and ModRM artefacts and are gone). It allocates a zeroed `TCharacter` (285 bytes) with resistance (`BaseArmourClassValue` +$A9) 50, `Thac0` 40, `Flag10D` 1, both picture banks 1, a random `RandomTag` and the six figure colour pairs from `FigureColorSlots`, then runs four `ChooseFromList` menus in the window (1,2)..($26,$16) with an empty title: 'Pick Race' (Dwarf, Elf, Gnome, Half-Elf, Halfling, Human; line 6 is stored as race 7, so Half-Orc is not offered), 'Pick Gender' (`SexNames`, stored in `Sex`), 'Pick Class' (the race's row of `RaceClassChoices`: a count, then `ClassMask` numbers) and 'Pick Alignment' (the class mask's row of `ClassAlignmentChoices` into the signed byte +$A0); Escape in any of them leaves at once and leaks the record. The race gives permanent affects (`AddAffect` Duration 0, Data $FF): Dwarf $5A $61 $1A $2F, Gnome $61 $12 $2F $30, Halfling $5A $61, Elf $6B, Half-Elf $7C, and `Figure[0]` 1 for Dwarf, Gnome and Halfling else 2. The class mask sets level 1 in each class it combines (8 Cleric/Fighter, 9 Cleric/Fighter/Magic-User, $A Cleric/Ranger, $B Cleric/Magic-User, $C Cleric/Thief, $D Fighter/Magic-User, $E Fighter/Thief, $F Fighter/Magic-User/Thief, $10 Magic-User/Thief), calls `ComputeThiefSkills` for a thief, takes `Thac0` as the highest `ClassThac0Table` entry and `ClassBits` (+$B0) as the sum of `ClassItemBits[class]` (the item-usage bits 2, $10, 8, $40, $80, 1, 4, $20), then `ComputeSavingThrows`. The starting age is the roll of `StartingAgeTable[Race, ClassMask]` (a `TAgeEntry`: base age, dice count, dice sides; Dwarf cleric 250 + 2d20, fighter 40 + 5d4) for a single class, and for a multi-class the maximum of the first combined class's entry (cleric for 8, 9, $B, $C; magic-user for $D, $F, $10; fighter for $E), with no case for $A. The sheet is shown (`SelectedCharacter` is replaced by the new record) and each ability is rolled until the player keeps the character: the best of six `RollDice(3, 6)` for each of the six scores, then the race's age brackets (`RaceAgeBrackets`, five words per race: young adult, middle age, old, venerable, the Dwarf row 50, 150, 250, 350, 450) change the score once for every bracket the age is above, cumulatively (Strength +1 above the young-adult age, -1 above middle age, -2 above old, -1 above venerable; Intelligence +1 middle and +1 venerable; Wisdom -1 at or below the young-adult age and +1 per later bracket; Dexterity -2 above old, -1 above venerable; Constitution -1 per bracket from middle age; Charisma none), the score is clamped to the race's limits (`RaceAbilityLimits[Race, column]`, Strength by gender through `Sex`) and raised to the class minimum (`ClassAbilityMinimums[ClassMask, ability]`); a fighter with Strength 18 gets `StrengthPercent` = Random(100) + 1 capped by the race, and the Cleric multi-classes (masks 8..12) get at least 13 Wisdom. QUIRK `ovl16:1236`: four racial adjustments of the roll (race 1 +1 Constitution -1 Charisma, race 2 +1 Dexterity -1 Constitution, race 5 -1 Strength +1 Dexterity, race 6 +1 Strength +1 Constitution -2 Charisma) test the race menu's loop variable, which is 7 after the loop, not the chosen race: they never apply. The class loop then sets the first-level slot of a cleric (`SpellSlots[0, 1]`) and of a magic-user (`SpellSlots[1, 1]`), gives a cleric every first-level cleric spell of `SpellTable` (and `AddWisdomBonusSpells`) and a magic-user Detect Magic, Read Magic, Shield and Sleep (`KnownSpells` 11, 18, 19, 21), rolls the gold dice of `StartingGoldDice` (class 0..7: 3d6, 3d6, 5d4, 5d4, 5d4, 2d4, 2d6, 5d4) and sets the gold (`Money[3]`) to ten times their total divided by the number of classes; `RollHitPoints` (all classes) gives `HpB1` and `HpMax`, which the constitution adjustment (`ConstitutionHitPointAdjustment`) and the class count turn into the first-level maximum (a negative adjustment is capped to leave at least 1), `HpCurrent` copies `HpMax` and `HpB1` is divided by the class count. Fixed starting values: `Attacks` 2, `BaseAttacks[1]` 1, `BaseDamage[1]` 2, `StrengthBonusFlag` 1, `BaseMovement` 12. Afterwards the name is asked until it is not empty, `ChooseHeadAndBody` and the icon editor `EditCharacterIcon` run, `SaveCharacter` writes the file and the record is freed.
- The training hall's Train command is `TrainCharacter` (`ovl16:29AE`) **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl16`). It refuses an unconscious character and one with less than 1000 gold, unless the STING cheat `StingCheat` is set (the J key of `RunCharacterMenu`). For each class with a level it applies the racial level cap `IsMaxClassLevel` (`00BA:2F57`) inlines, and trains the class when experience has reached `ExperienceTable[class, level+1]` and that threshold is above 0. Experience already at `ExperienceTable[class, level+2]` is cut to one below it, so one visit raises one level. Unless the cheat is set, the hall's word `GameVars2^[680]` must share a class bit (`ClassTrainingBits`) with the character and with a trainable class. After `AskYesNo`, it charges 1000 gold (`PayGold`), raises each trainable class, rebuilds the derived stats (`RecalcClassStats`) and, when the magic-user level rose, asks for one new spell. A class that rises also gives back one level drained by `DrainLevels` (`ovl12:2122`): while the character's drained-level count (`+$74`) is above 0, `+$75` (the hit points lost with them) loses `+$75 div +$74` and `+$74` drops by one, so training restores one drained level per class raised **[I]** (the two fields are ovl12's `DrainLevels` and ovl22's `CastRestoration`). Hit points gained are `RollHitPoints` of the trained classes, shared across every class with a level, floored at 1, added to `HpB1` and, after the constitution adjustment, to `HpMax`; the current hit points (`HpCurrent`) keep the same gap below `HpMax`. QUIRK `ovl16:2CDA`: the first class-refusal `Exit` is followed by a jump nothing reaches. QUIRK `ovl16:2F95`: the `KnownSpells[ChosenSpell] := 1` after the magic-user rise also runs if the spell list returned `Listed = 0` with `ChosenSpell` 0; `KnownSpells` is declared `[1..$38]` (`+$33..+$6A`), so index 0 is `HpMax` (`+$32`), which then becomes 1 before the hit-point gain is added.
- The icon editor is `EditCharacterIcon` (`ovl16:382D`, 1796 bytes, `src/OVL16.PAS`) **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl16`: matched with its nested `DrawIconCell` `34DF` and `CopyPictureSlot` `35BE`; the map's 13 entries `3C75`..`3EC9` inside it are stub-segment flat readings, not routines). It is the last step of `CreateCharacter` and the I key of the camp's Alter menu (`AlterMenu`, `ovl15:1C59`). It edits `SelectedCharacter^` in place: the CHEAD and CBODY picture blocks (`CHeadBlock` 0..13, `CBodyBlock` 0..31), the icon size (`Figure[0]`: 1 small, 2 large) and the six colour pairs `Figure[1..6]` (low nibble colour 1, high nibble colour 2). A working copy of the combat icon lives in picture slot 12 (`PictureSlots`): `CopyPictureSlot(CombatIcon, 12, 1)` copies the combat icon's tile bytes and pixels into it and recolours it with the colour pairs (the recolouring of `BuildCharacterIcon`), and `DrawIconCell` shows it at cell row 4 under the caption 'new' (the icon as it was on entry stays at row 2 under 'old'). The prompt line is `IconEditorPrompts[State]` (`DS:07E8`, five 41-byte lines) shown by `ReadPromptChoice` with no title: state 1 `Parts color-1 color-2 Size Exit`, state 2 `Head Weapon Exit`, state 3 `Weapon Body Hair Shield Arm Leg Exit` (the word `xxxx` of the image is replaced by `Hair` for color-1 and `Face` for color-2 with `Delete`/`Insert` at column 13), state 4 `<Small|Large> Keep Exit` (the size the player can switch to) and state 5 `Next Prev Keep Exit`, which steps the block (Head wraps 0..13, Weapon 0..31) or one colour nibble (`mod 16`). The part keys of state 3 pick the `Figure` index: Weapon 6, Body 1, Hair or Face 4, Shield 5, Arm 2, Leg 3. Keep saves the value in a local, Exit or Escape restores it, and leaving state 1 restores the last kept values, copies the working icon back into the combat icon slot (`CopyPictureSlot(12, CombatIcon, 0)`) and asks `AskYesNo` 'Is this icon ok? '; anything but Y starts again, and when `AreaViewKind` is above 1 the `ICON` pictures 0 and 1 are first reloaded into slots 11 and 12. `SwapPaletteColors0And8` and `RestorePaletteColors0And8` (palette register calls on adapter 1, purpose unknown) bracket the editor. Compiler note **[V]**: the 6-byte save and restore of `Figure[1..6]` is a structure assignment (`SavedFigure := TFigureBytes(Figure).Colors`), which calls `0AF8:0A3F` (the assignment helper), not `Move` (`0AF8:1685`); `tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl16/FIGCOPY.PAS`.
- The title sequence is `ShowTitleScreen` (ShowTitleScreen, entry point called from the main program before the code wheel) **[V]** (decompiled and matched as a whole, `src/OVL01.PAS`): it backs up `GameDir` and clears it, loads the `title` picture (`LoadPicture`) and shows it (`ShowPicture`), waits (`WaitKeyOrTimeout(5)`), and, when the `F` flag (`DS:49D1`) is set, reloads, reshows, frees the title picture (`ReleasePicture`, `0464:2595`: it frees the pixel buffer at +$13 and the block, then stores nil in the pointer, no palette write), waits for a key or a tick deadline (`GameClock` + 500) and shows the credits (`ShowCredits`); with `F` clear it only restores `GameDir` and returns. `WaitKeyOrTimeout(Pause)` loops until the `GameClock` tick count passes its start plus 100 times `Pause`, or a key is waiting, then calls `FlushKeyboard`, which does nothing (QUIRK `0899:010A`) **[V]** (code).
- `ShowCredits` (ShowCredits) draws two empty windows and some thirty green/cyan/yellow name and role lines (`DrawWindow`, `DrawTextAt`) and waits the same way **[V]** (code).
- QUIRK ovl01:0729: the EGA-only palette animation inside the title wait is gated on a running sum: every pass of the wait loop adds `Random(4)` (0..3) to `Total` (ovl01:0721..0726, `add`/`adc` into a Longint), and when the sum passes 50000 the animation runs and the sum restarts from 0 (ovl01:073E..0743) **[V]** (code). How many passes the loop makes before its 500-tick deadline depends on the CPU speed, so whether the animation fires does too: about 33,000 passes are needed at the mean of 1.5. In the oracle run it did not fire (about 30,000 `Random(4)` calls, a sum near 45,000, see below) **[V]**; on period hardware **[?]**. A port must keep the animation, not drop it as dead code.
- The window unit is res09 **[V]** (decompiled and matched as a whole, `src/RES09.PAS`):
  `DrawWindow(LeftCol, TopRow, RightCol, BottomRow, FrameSet, TextColor, Text)` clears the
  rectangle (`ClearWindow`, `03F1:0137`), draws one frame set around it (`DrawWindowFrame`,
  `03F1:0000`) and centres `Text` on `TopRow` in colour `TextColor` (`DrawTextAt`,
  skipped when `Text` is empty). Coordinates are `ShortInt` (sign-extended; the frame reaches
  one cell past the window, so -1 is legal), colours and the frame set are `Byte`, and `Text`
  is a `string[40]` value parameter, which the compiler copies to a hidden stack temporary on
  entry (`System_StrLCopy`, `0AF8:0B25`, limit 40) **[V]** (code).
  - `DrawWindowFrame` draws four corner tiles, the top/bottom edges and the left/right edges
    through `Put8x8Symbol`, with tile base `FrameSet * 3 + $114` (`$114` = 276, the value 5.2 already
    reports for the adventure screen frame) **[V]** (code). The last two arguments of `Put8x8Symbol`
    are 0 here; what they select is still open **[?]**.
  - `ClearWindow` fills the rectangle with 0 by video hardware, selected by the `VideoAdapter`
    global (`DS:4A1E`) **[V]** (code): 0 fills two text-scanline planes (absolute byte planes
    over `$B800`/`$BA00`, 80 bytes per scanline row, 4 scanlines per text row), 1 draws through
    `FillPlaneRect` twice, 2 fills four graphics planes (`$B800`/`$BA00`/`$BC00`/`$BE00`, 160
    bytes per row, 2 rows per text row). The values are proven: res08_init sets 1 for `E`,
    2 for `T` and 0 otherwise (`042B:019B..01F9`) **[V]**; what `FillPlaneRect` draws is still
    open **[?]**.
  - Callers **[V]** (`make packet UNIT=res09`): `DrawWindow` is called for dialogs and screens
    from ovl01, ovl02 (the code wheel), ovl03, ovl04, ovl05 and ovl06; the frame and clear
    routines are also called directly from ovl08, ovl13, ovl14, ovl15, ovl16, ovl19 and res12.

### 5.11 Saving and loading (ovl17 SaveLoad, complete)

Unit ovl17 reads and writes the saved-game files of section 4.4: the per-character
`CHRDAT<slot><n>.SAV/.ITM/.SPC` (character 285, item 63, affect 9 bytes) and the roster
`charlist.txt`. Most of its routines are still stubs; the one matched so far is how a loaded
character joins the party.

- **Adding a party member** **[V]** (`AddPartyMember`, `ovl17:1446`, `make match UNIT=ovl17`): given a
  `TCharacter`, it clears its `CombatIcon` (`+$BF`) to $FF, links it at the tail of the party list
  `PartyListHead` (or makes it the head when the list is empty, setting `SelectedCharacter` too), then marks
  which of the eight combat map icon numbers 0..7 are already held by a member and gives the new
  member the lowest free one (`SelectedCharacter^.CombatIcon`). Finally it counts the member in the
  party size word (`GameVars2^[VarPartySize]`, word 830) and recalculates its class statistics
  (`RecalcClassStats`, ovl23). So every party member carries a distinct combat icon 0..7, and the
  party list `PartyListHead` is the single list the combat and camp units walk **[V]** (5.5, 5.20).

- **The saved-character list** **[V]** (`ReadSavedCharacterList`, `ovl17:0010`; `RewriteSavedCharacterList`,
  `ovl17:01B8`): `charlist.txt` in `SaveDir` holds one saved character name per line. The reader makes
  the save directory (`CreateSaveDir`), reads the file into a list of 46-byte menu lines (the Text,
  Flag29 and Next fields of `TMenuLine`) and prefixes `'* '` to a name the party list `PartyListHead` already
  carries; the writer rewrites the file from such a list, skipping the lines that begin with `'*'`,
  and prompts for the save disk while the file cannot be created. Both use the map disk prompt
  `'Put save disk in <drive>:'`.
- **Saving a character** **[V]** (`SaveCharacter`, `ovl17:069E`; `make match UNIT=ovl17`): with an
  empty name it adds the character's name to `charlist.txt` (a new line at the tail, then the rewrite
  above) and writes `SaveDir + MakeDosFileName(name) + '.cha'`; with a name it makes the directory and
  writes `SaveDir + name + '.sav'`. Either way it `Rewrite`s the file with record size 1 (prompting
  for the save disk while the open fails), `BlockWrite`s the 285-byte character, closes it and, after
  `Erase`ing any old file, writes the 63-byte items of `Who^.Items` to `.itm` and the 9-byte affects
  of `Who^.Affects` to `.spc`.
- **Loading a character** **[V]** (`LoadCharacterFile`, `ovl17:0AEC`; `make match UNIT=ovl17`): the
  read counterpart of `SaveCharacter`. `UseSaveDir` picks the save disk (the name becomes
  `SaveDir + MakeDosFileName(name)` and the prompt names SaveDir's drive) or the game disk (the
  prompt is `InsertDiskPrompts[TileSet] + GameDir's drive + ':'`); `SavedGame` picks `'.sav'` (a saved
  game) or `'.cha'` (an available character). It `OpenGameFile`s `name + suffix` and `BlockRead`s the
  285-byte character into `Who^`, clears `Who^.Next`, `.Items`, `.Affects` and `.Combat`, and, when
  `name.itm` / `name.spc` exist, reads the 63-byte item and 9-byte affect records to end of file into a
  `GetMem` chain, then flushes the keyboard.
- **Loading a monster** **[V]** (`LoadMonster`, `ovl17:0EC4`; `make match UNIT=ovl17`): builds
  `MON<Str(TileSet)>CHA.DAX` / `SPC` / `ITM` and reads block `Kind` from each `poolrad` archive through
  `LoadDaxBlock`. A missing CHA block shows "Unable to load monster" and quits; otherwise it `Move`s
  the 285-byte record into `Who^`, clears `Items`, `Affects`, `Next` and `Combat`, and builds
  `Who^.Figure`: `Figure[0]` is 1 for the races 1/3/5 and 2 for 2/4/6/7, `Figure[1..3]` and
  `Figure[5..6]` are random nibble pairs `Roll + ((Roll + 8) shl 4)` from `RollDice(1, 6)`, and
  `Figure[4]` is `$C4`. The SPC and ITM records (9 and 63 bytes) are copied out of the buffer (offset
  up to the block size) into `GetMem` chains while `OpenNoPrompt` marks the load. Called by ovl07
  `LoadCharacter` and AddMonsterToParty.
- **Adding a monster to the party** **[V]** (`AddMonsterToParty`, `ovl17:1262`; `make match
  UNIT=ovl17`): gives up if the party already holds eight members (`GameVars2^[830] > 7`), has
  `LoadMonster` build a 285-byte record, sets its `+$AB` byte to `Random(256)`, collects the classes
  with a positive level (`ClassLevels[0..6]`, index 2 when none qualifies) into a small array, rolls
  one of them (`RollDice(1, Count)`) and picks the body picture bank from the DS:08C5-region tables
  by `Who^.Sex` and the roll (the table of class 0, 5, 6 or the default), then the head bank the same
  way (`RollDice(1, 8)`). Finally `AddPartyMember` and `BuildCharacterIcon(True)`. Called by
  AddComputerMemberCommand.
- **The combat icon picture** **[V]** (`BuildCharacterIcon`, `ovl17:0482`; `MergePictureData`,
  `ovl17:032F`): with 8X8D tile set 3 selected, it loads the selected character's CHEAD picture (the
  name suffixed from `FigureBankTable[Sel^.Figure[0]]`, block `Sel^.CHeadBlock`) into slot 11 and its CBODY
  (block `Sel^.CBodyBlock`) into slot `Sel^.CombatIcon`, then merges the CHEAD halves into the CBODY
  halves (OR the tail tile bytes, AND the pixel bytes) so the head masks the body; when asked it also
  builds a 16-entry colour map from the six nibble pairs `Sel^.Figure[1..6]` through `FigureColorSlots` and
  applies it to both halves.
- **Saving the game** **[V]** (`SaveGame`, `ovl17:1D58`; `make match UNIT=ovl17`): prompts
  `'Save Which Game: '` for a slot letter A..J (a set of `[#0, 'A'..'J']`, Escape cancels), makes the
  save directory and `Rewrite`s `SaveDir + 'savgam<slot>.dat'` (prompting for the disk while the open
  fails). It first copies the three loaded wall-set slots (block id and slot) into the ECL variable
  words: `WallSetSlotPairs[I].A/.B` into the words at `$4AF9 + I` / `$4AFC + I` (I = 1..3), `GameSpeed` to
  `$49FC`, the packed picture flags
  (`ShowMonsterPics * 2 + ShowPortraits`) to `$49FF`, and `TileSet` to `$6E12`. Then it `BlockWrite`s
  `TileSet` (1), `GameVars^`, `GameVars2^` ($800 each), `GameVars3^` ($400), `EclCode^` ($1E00), the five
  party-map bytes `PartyMapColumn..`, `AreaViewKind` and `ViewMode` (1 each), a one-byte member count and
  the eight 41-byte `'CHRDAT<slot><n>'` names. It closes the file, then for each party member calls
  `SaveCharacter(member, 'CHRDAT<slot><n>')` and erases the member's standalone `.cha/.itm/.spc` files
  (nested under the `.cha` test), and ends with `HeapErrorStop := True` and a cleared prompt line. Reads the
  slot key, `SaveDir`, the game state and the party list `PartyListHead`; called by the heap error handler,
  ovl15 `MakeCamp`, RunCharacterMenu and SaveAndExit.
- **Loading the game** **[V]** (`LoadGame`, `ovl17:1602`; `make match UNIT=ovl17`): the read
  counterpart of SaveGame. Inside `'Load Which Game: '` it repeats until the player picks a letter
  A..J whose `SaveDir + 'savgam<letter>.dat'` exists (or Escape), then `OpenGameFile`s the slot and
  `BlockRead`s the state back in the same order, restoring `ShowMonsterPics := word shr 1`,
  `ShowPortraits := word and 1` and `GameSpeed` from the ECL block and zeroing `$6E3E`. For the
  member count it loads each name (`MakeDosFileName(DeleteAllOccurrences(name, '.'))`) with
  `LoadCharacterFile(name, Who, 1, 1)` into a fresh 285-byte `GetMem` record and adds it with
  `AddPartyMember`, then erases the members' standalone `.cha/.itm/.spc` files and rebuilds every
  member's combat icon (`BuildCharacterIcon(True)`). Finally it restores `TileSet` from `$6E12` and
  the view: the 3D view (`AreaViewKind < 2`) gets `Load3DMap(GameVars^[VarGeoBlock])` and the three
  wall sets (`LoadWallSet` of the `WallSetSlotPairs` pairs whose block id is not negative); a map view
  (`AreaViewKind > 1`) gets the party marker picture instead (`LoadPictureSlot('ICON', 0, 11)` into
  picture slot 11, the scratch slot `BuildCharacterIcon` freed). It then animates the doors when
  `GameVars^[VarRiverGate] <> 0` and flushes the keyboard. Called by RunCharacterMenu.
- **Save and exit** **[V]** (`SaveAndExit`, `ovl17:22D5`; `make match UNIT=ovl17`): called by
  ovl03:0BD1 with the current area's ECL file name. When that file exists (a normal area) it returns
  at once; otherwise it shows `'Save and exit  Insert disk #<TileSet>'` and, when the player presses
  its hotkey S, steps the party one cell back in its facing (`Dec` of `DirStepX/DirStepY[PartyFacing]`,
  clamped into the 16x16 map), restores the tile set through `RestoreTileSet`, calls SaveGame and
  quits. So the "Save and exit" path is the end of the game, when the next area has no ECL script.

**Boundary note** **[V]** (`make packet UNIT=ovl17`, before the routines were matched): the map split
several ovl17 routines into mid-instruction entries (`ovl17_fn_0818/084A/0865` inside `SaveCharacter`,
`ovl17_fn_0B48` inside `LoadCharacterFile`, `ovl17_fn_0F99` inside `LoadMonster`, `ovl17_fn_0AE5` a
literal pool). Each real routine has one `push bp; mov bp, sp` prologue and one `retf`; the phantom
entries share the parent frame. `make match WRITE=1` retires them as each real routine matches.

### 5.12 Copy protection: the code wheel

- The check is `CheckCodeWheel` (`ovl02:00FD`, entry point of overlay 2, matched as a whole: `src/OVL02.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl02`: unit complete) **[V]**. At most three times it picks `r = Random(13)` (`System_Random`, `0AF8:11C0`), draws two rune glyphs and a path pattern from a table at the very start of DGROUP (`DS:0002`, 13 records of 21 bytes, the `TCodeWheelEntry` type in `src/POOLVARS.PAS`; DS:0000 holds two bytes of Turbo Pascal's own), calls the keyboard flush `res04:0104` (which does nothing, 5.8) and asks "INPUT THE CODE WORD:" **[V]**. The question window is drawn by `DrawWindow(1, 1, $26, $16, 0, 0, '')` and the instructions, the runes and the path by `DrawTextAt`, `DrawGlyph` (the runes as font characters at the four corners of a small grid) and one concatenation of five spaces with the record path **[V]** (code).
- Record layout **[V]** (`start-unpack.exe` image at `0C7C0h`, and the lcalls in `ovl02:01A2..02FD`): `+02` Pascal string[6] with the answer; `+09`, `+0A`, `+0B` glyph indices, each drawn as character `index + 40h` in the game's font (the two rune pictures the prompt asks to match, then a third); `+0C` a fourth index drawn only when it is not FFh; `+0D` Pascal string with the path pattern drawn under the runes (`- - - -`, `-..-..-`, `.......`).
- The 13 answers, in record order **[V]**: BEWARE, ZOMBIE, NOTNOW, COPPER, DRAGON, EFREET, FRIEND, JUNGLE, KNIGHT, SAVIOR, TEMPLE, VULCAN, WYVERN. The typed word is compared with `System_StrCompare` (`0AF8:0C10`, flags in ZF) after truncating the function result of `ReadInputLine` (the line reader of res06) to 40 characters; the failure message is "Sorry, that's incorrect." (`ovl02:00E4`) through `FlashMessageLine`; after the third failure `res04:0000` ends the program **[V]**.
- The line reader `ReadInputLine` is a string function: TP 6.0 passes the caller result pointer as the first (deepest) argument and the callee `retf 8` pops only the prompt and the two numbers, leaving the result pointer for the caller to consume (here: the truncating assignment `Answer := ReadInputLine('INPUT THE CODE WORD: ', 13, 6)`, `0AF8:0B25` with max 40) **[V]** (the frames: `retf 8` = 8 bytes of parameters, the observed 12 pushed).
- Which question appears depends on `RandSeed`, which comes from the clock, so it varies from run to run on real hardware. The oracle's virtual clock makes it repeat; its `wheel` script command reads the answer from the table by watching that `Random(13)` call (`tools/oracle/protection.py`) and types it, leaving the game's own check untouched.

### 5.13 Random numbers

- The generator is Turbo Pascal 6.0's, in System at `0AF8:120F` (step), `0AF8:11C0` (`Random(range)`) and `0AF8:11D6` (`Random` real) **[V]** (code read; every logged call of the oracle satisfies the formulas below, and the logged results equal `AX` at the return of `Random(range)` for the first 300 calls, `tests/unit/test_oracle.py`).
  - `RandSeed` (DS:4428, low word first) `:= RandSeed * 08088405h + 1` modulo 2^32.
  - `Random(range) = hi16(RandSeed) mod range`, except the step runs first: with `range` 0 the seed still advances and the result is 0 **[V]** (isolated differential fuzz of `0AF8:11C0`, P3 spike; the earlier wording did not say the step runs). This is TP 6's `div`, not the multiply-and-shift of TP 7, which matters for anyone lifting a routine that compares sequences.
- `Randomize` is `0AF8:1247`: `INT 21h AH=2Ch`, then `RandSeed := DX:CX` (seconds and hundredths in the high word, hours and minutes in the low word) **[V]**. The game calls it once, during the title screens, about 16.6 million instructions after start (`RandSeed` is written only there in the first 80 million instructions), so everything the game decides at random is a function of the clock at that moment and of the sequence of `Random` calls after it.
- The title sequence calls `Random(4)` from `ovl01:0721` over 30,000 times in its first 80 million instructions **[V]**, so the number of calls before the wheel question depends on how long the title screens ran. A run is reproducible only if both the seed and the timing of those screens are reproducible (`clock` script command and `--trace rng`, appendix C).

### 5.14 Time, input and sound hooks

What the game takes from the outside world, found by `make timing-scan` and by reading the routines (Capstone dumps of the addresses below). Basis of the platform layer of the C translation (`src/platform/platform.h` on branch `track-c-hybrid`).

- **Keyboard** **[V]**: Crt `KeyPressed` is `0A40:02FD` (`INT 16h AH=1`, ZF; a pending extended scan code is kept in `DS:8501`); `ReadKey` is `0A40:030F` (`INT 16h AH=0`, returns the scan code of an extended key on the second call, maps a zero scan code to Ctrl-C `3`) and then runs `0A40:0143` (when `CheckBreak` is on: drains the keyboard buffer in a loop that ends when it is empty, writes `^C` and raises `INT 23h`). `KeyPressed` was called 508,736 times in the two recorded sessions: menus and prompts poll it.
- **Time of day and date** **[V]**: Dos `GetTime` is `0A32:00A2` (`INT 21h AH=2Ch`, also what `Randomize` reads, 5.13) and `GetDate` is `0A32:006C` (`AH=2Ah`).
- **Delay** **[V]**: `Delay(ms)` is `0A40:029C`: a counted loop (`0A40:02BD`, `loope` against a byte that never changes) repeated `ms` times with a loop count calibrated at start up in `DS:84FC`. The calibration is Crt's initialisation, `0A40:002E` **[V]**: it waits for the BIOS tick count at `0040:006C` to change, then counts loop iterations until it changes again and divides by 55 (ms per tick). So `DS:84FC` depends on the emulated CPU speed and is not game state. `0A40:002E` is the only routine that reads the BIOS tick counter, and it does so through a register (`mov di,6Ch`; `es:[di]`).
- **Timer** **[V]**: the sound unit (res03) starts at `08AE:137C`: it saves INT 08h (`AH=35h`), installs its handler `08AE:13EA` (`AH=25h`) and writes `OUT 43h,B6h` and divisor `13B1h` = 5041 to port 40h, which is **13 times the BIOS rate, 236.7 Hz** (1,193,182 / 5041; the boot sequence note used to say about 297 Hz, which was wrong). The handler runs the voice stepper `08AE:1477` and a countdown at `DS:01DB` (a time-out that resets the video mode and terminates with exit code 255), and only every 13th call chains to the saved BIOS handler (`DS:01D7` counts 13 down), which keeps the BIOS clock at 18.2 Hz. Stop is `08AE:13B1`: restores the vector, divisor `FFFFh`, speaker off, Tandy chip silenced. The game's timer interrupt writes no DGROUP global outside its own stack frames (dynamic pass, `boot-title` and `create-character`).
- **Sound** **[V]**: the PC speaker is driven from PIT channel 2 (`OUT 42h` reload, `IN/OUT 61h` low two bits): `Crt.Sound(hz)` `0A40:02C9` (divisor = 1,193,181 / hz, gate 3), `Crt.NoSound` `0A40:02F6`, and the voice stepper `08AE:1477` over four voice records (stride 30h at `CS:022D`, see the next item). The Tandy 3-voice chip is written through port `C0h` (silencing: 9Fh, BFh, DFh, FFh).
- **The sound unit res03** **[V]** (`src/RES03.PAS`, `make match UNIT=res03`: the five Pascal routines at L2). Its Pascal part is a front end: `PlaySound(Sound)` (`08AE:00DC`; 0 sets both devices to tune 0 and the output flag off, 1 turns the output flag on, `FFh` sets both devices to tune 0, any other n starts tune n - 1 on the device `SoundMode` selects: 1 PC speaker through `SetSpeakerTune` `08AE:1742`, 0 Tandy through `SetTandyTune` `08AE:17AE`; nothing when `SoundMode` is 2, silent), `SilenceSound` (`08AE:017B`, runs the driver once to set both devices to tune 0) and `SetSoundDriverIdle(Idle)` (`08AE:01A5`: 0 starts the timer driver for good, so tunes play in the background; nonzero stops it, and `PlaySound` then starts it, waits 0.1 s with `WaitHundredths(10)` and stops it around each sound; the value is kept in `SoundDriverIdle`, `DS:5374`). `WaitHundredths` busy-waits on `TimeInHundredths` (`08AE:0000`, `GetTime` in hundredths since midnight; QUIRK `08AE:0033`: the minutes and seconds terms are Word products, so the minutes term wraps above 10 minutes). The rest of the segment, `08AE:01D7` to the end, is a hand-written assembly driver linked after the empty initialisation part (an external `.OBJ`: `8B EC` prologues, DS loaded with the unit's own segment `08AE`, so the voice records `022Dh` (speaker) and `03C7h` (Tandy), the tune tables `055Fh` and `0607h`, the output flag `020Dh` and the counters `01D7h` and `01DBh` above are offsets in the code segment, not DGROUP). Its source is `src/RES03.ASM` (Phase 5): `tools/re/asm86.py` assembles it and `make rebuild` links the object, byte for byte; it is documented there (header: devices, voice record, the stream language, the tables) and in the next item. The map entries `08AE:09E3` to `08AE:1221` are data inside it, not routines. [V]
- **Entropy**: an earlier revision credited `ovl22:0B89` with `IN AL,40h` and `ovl14:0B48` with a PIT read. Both were misaligned disassemblies of bogus map entries. The `E4 40` bytes at `ovl22:0B89` are the middle of `xor ah,ah; inc ax` in the level-4 spell-target loop of `ChooseSpellTargets` (`ovl22:0A91`, 285 bytes, matched 2026-10-13; the map entries `ovl22:0AE5`..`0B8E` are all inside it and were removed), so `ovl22:0B89` is not a routine and does not read the PIT. The `ovl14:0B48` bytes are the tail of `xor ah,ah` plus `inc ax` in the middle of `HandleWildernessKeys`, which cycles `(DS:6AAF + 2) mod 8` (corrected with `make match UNIT=ovl14`, 2026-10-12); aligned disassembly shows no port I/O anywhere in ovl14. The oracle returns a deterministic function of the instruction count for port 40h.
- **No retrace polling** **[V]**: no routine reads port 3DAh. No routine reads the BIOS tick counter other than `0A40:002E`.
- **Not a wait** **[I]**: `0AF8:01A5` and `0AF8:01E7` write characters with `INT 21h AH=06h` (output, DL is the character); an early scan counted them as keyboard polls.

### 5.15 Messages and running out of memory **[V]** (`src/RES06.PAS`, `src/OVL00.PAS`, matched)

- `ShowMessageLine(Color, Text)` shows a one-line message on row 24: it clears 40 columns of that row (`ClearTextCells(0, 24, 0, 40)`), draws the text in the given colour (`DrawTextAt(0, 24, Color, Text)`) and waits for a key through `ReadGameKey`. The text is a `string[40]`. Twelve routines call it, in overlays 0, 3, 5, 7, 17 and 33 and in res06 itself (`make packet FN=ShowMessageLine`).
- `GameClock` (`GameClock`, `0709:0C3F`) is the game's time of day in hundredths of a second: it reads `GetTime` and returns `Hour * 360000 + Minute * 6000 + Second * 100 + Sec100`. `Hour * 360000` is a LongInt multiply (the `System` runtime), but `Minute * 6000` and `Second * 100` are Word multiplies whose high word is dropped (QUIRK `0709:0C75`, `0709:0C85`, so a minute above 10 loses its carry). `WaitKeyOrTimeout`, `ShowTitleScreen`, `AnimateFinalPicture` and `ReadPromptChoice` call it.
- Overlay 0 is the heap error handler: its initialisation sets `HeapError := @HeapErrorHandler`, so Turbo Pascal calls `HeapErrorHandler(Size)` when `New` or `GetMem` cannot get `Size` bytes. A zero size returns at once with an unassigned result (`jbe ovl00:004C` to `ovl00:00A3`). For a non-zero size it shows "WARNING: Insufficient Memory" in colour 14 (yellow); if the Boolean `DS:49D0` is set it calls `SaveGame` (the save and load unit) and repeats until `DS:557D` says to stop, otherwise it shows "Insufficient Memory. Exiting Program". Then it always ends the program (`QuitGame`), so that path never returns. The function result is never assigned on any path (QUIRK `ovl00:00A3`).
- `DrawGlyph(Col, Row, Color, Count, Glyph, Cook)` (`0709:01DF`) **[V]** draws Count copies of a font glyph from cell Col of text row Row (cells are 8 by 8 pixels, 40 by 25). The font is the `GameFont` block, 8 bytes per glyph indexed `[Glyph, PixelRow]`; Cook nonzero maps the character through `UpCase` into 0..63 (the text glyph set). Exits for a cell past column 39 or row 24, a nil font, or Count 0. By `VideoAdapter`: 0 builds 16-bit pixel rows from `GlyphPixelMasks` and `GlyphColorPatterns` (colour through the four-colour map `DS:25D5`) into the two text-scanline planes `$B800`/`$BA00`; 1 writes each font byte to the two EGA pages per plane through the Sequencer Map Mask (`EgaPlaneMasks`, port `3C5h`), 0 where the colour lacks the plane; 2 builds two colour nibbles per pixel pair into the four graphics planes `$B800..$BE00`.
- `ClearTextCells(Col, Row, Color, Count)` (`0709:05EB`) draws Count spaces; `DrawTextAt(Col, Row, Color, Text)` (`0709:0626`) draws a string cooked glyph by glyph (QUIRK `0709:0647`: the row is tested against 39, not 24, so DrawGlyph does the real row check). `FlashMessageLine(Text, Color)` (`0709:0D68`) is `ShowMessageLine` without the key wait: clear row 24, draw, `DelayByGameSpeed`, clear. `ReadInputLine(Prompt, Color, MaxLen)` (`0709:09E3`) is the line editor of the bottom row: characters `' '..'z'` up to MaxLen echoed in white, Backspace, Enter or Esc to end (Esc returns the text too), result upper-cased `string[40]`. `ReadNumber(Prompt, Color)` (`0709:0B66`) repeats it with 6 characters until `Val` accepts a number in 0..65536 and returns it as a Word (QUIRK `0709:0BBF`: 65536 passes and becomes 0). All **[V]** (`make match UNIT=res06`).
- `ShowPagedText(Left, Top, Right, Bottom, Color, ClearFirst, Text)` (`0709:0768`) **[V]** is the word-wrapping pager behind the shop, temple, treasure and battle-end text: it keeps a cursor in the globals `GlyphCol`/`GlyphRow` (`DS:5E24`/`DS:5E25`, reset to (Left, Top) when outside the rectangle or when ClearFirst clears it through `ClearRect`), scans Text word by word (leading `BreakChars` skipped, the run stops at a blank or a break character, trailing break characters absorbed from `Text[J + 1]`), draws a word through its nested `DrawTextRun` (`0709:06A7`: one `DrawGlyph` per character, `Delay(GameSpeed * 10)` after each while `DS:4A00` is set) when it still fits before Right, else starts a new line with its nested `SkipSpaces` (`0709:0714`); past Bottom with text left it shows "Press any key to continue" through `ShowMessageLine(13, ...)`, flushes the keyboard, clears the rectangle and continues from the top. QUIRK `0709:0919`: the equal-to-Right-and-blank branch is dead (the outer test already took greater than Right). Coordinates past column 39 or row 24 (Bottom is tested against 39 too) and empty text return at once; `Width` is computed and never read; the frame has an unused byte local. Matched with both nested routines (`make match UNIT=res06`: `NESTED` lines).
- TP 6.0 idioms proven while lifting `DrawGlyph` **[V]** (`make tpc` probes `scratch/Q*.PAS` in the res06 worktree, then `make match`): `0AF8:182A` is `System.UpCase`; an `or` chain with a far exit flattens so every operand but the last jumps on true to one shared exit jump (Q55); a `for` loop emits init, a jump over the first increment, increment-first body, and a short `jne` back for near bodies versus `je` plus a near `jmp` for far bodies (Q39); a byte test `(x and Mask) > 0` compiles to `or al,al; jbe`, where `<> 0` gives `je`; indexing a 2-D byte array through a pointer loads the column into DX first and adds it after the row (`les di; add di,ax; add di,dx`); `Inc(W, n)` on a word local is one `add [bp-k],n`; `Color shl 4` on a byte loads the count with `mov cx,4`; comparing a Byte parameter with `< 0` keeps the never-true `jb` (ClearTextCells), while `x in [0..n]` would put a set constant in the literal pool.

### 5.16 The 3D sprite unit ovl28 **[V]** (`src/OVL28.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl28`: all 10 routines at L2, complete)

Overlay 28 draws the 8x8 sprites of the 3D view and the HEAD/BODY strip of the character screen.
The parts proven so far:

- `DrawSprite(Col, Row: Integer; Direct: Byte; Sprite: Pointer)` (`ovl28:0000`): a nil sprite draws
  nothing; with `Direct` set it blits the sprite one column and one row up and left (`Col - 1`,
  `Row - 1`) onto the screen buffer
  `DS:4A20` through `DrawPictureItemClipped` (mode 0, flag 1) and steps the display (`FlushPictureChanges`);
  otherwise it copies the sprite into the staging buffer with `ShowPicture`.
- `DrawHeadSprite` and `DrawBodySprite` (`ovl28:0050`, `ovl28:0070`): draw a sprite at (column,
  row) and at (column, row + 5); `ShowHeadBodySprites` (`ovl28:0697`) uses them to draw the HEAD
  sprite `DS:6B01`, and with its `WithBody` argument also the BODY sprite `DS:6B06`, through the
  staging buffer (both helpers pass `Direct` 0; neither path draws straight to the screen buffer).
- `LoadHeadBodySprites` (`ovl28:05D9`) loads the HEAD and BODY banks of the picture number
  `TileSet` (`DS:5376`) from the files `HEAD<number>` and `BODY<number>` through `LoadPicture` into
  `DS:6B01` and `DS:6B06` (recording the loaded number in `DS:6B00` and `DS:6B05`, `$FF` = none).
- `FreeSpriteList` (`ovl28:048F`) frees every loaded sprite picture of a sprite list (through
  `ReleasePicture`) and zeroes the list, clears the picture name `DS:6B0A` and sets the current bank
  `DS:6B1C` to `$FF` (not 0).
- The sprite list is a `$42`-byte record: a count byte at +0, a flag at +1, and seven slots of 8
  bytes from +2, each holding 4 bytes copied from the picture data and the picture pointer at +4
  (the `TSpriteList` type of `src/OVL28.PAS`) **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl28`).
- Each picture is one record of `AllocatePicture` (the same one `ovl32`'s `DrawIcon` copies): height
  and width at +0 and +2 (the record fields `Height` and `WidthCols`, after `ovl32`; `AllocatePicture`
  declares the two parameters `Height` and `Width` and whitepaper 3.2.2 calls them `Cols` and
  `Rows`), item count at +8 (1 for a sprite), the pixel byte size at +$11 (height times width
  times 2, doubled on a non-zero `VideoAdapter`) and the pixel pointer at +$13; the sprite's base
  screen column and row sit at +4 and +6 (`TSpritePicture`) **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl28`;
  whitepaper 4.2.3, 5.13).

- `Show3DSprite` (`ovl28:0708`) shows slot 1..3 of a sprite list at the sprite's base column and row
  plus 2 through `DrawPictureItemClipped`; another slot restores the saved video mode (`RestoreVideoMode`,
  `SavedVideoMode`) and ends the game with the unit's range error string.
- `ShowHeadBodyWindow` (`ovl28:04F9`) draws the HEAD/BODY picture window (columns 28..38, rows 1..11)
  of the character record `DS:5D92` and shows the HEAD and BODY sprites of its two bank numbers
  (its two `Str` calls are computed and never read, QUIRK `ovl28:056C`).

- `LoadSpriteList(Name, Bank, Mode, List)` (`ovl28:00A2`) loads block `Bank` of `<Name><TileSet>.dax`
  into a sprite list, unless the same file and block are loaded already (`DS:6B0A`, `DS:6B1C`; the
  test ignores the `TileSet` digit). The
  block is a count byte (clamped to 4 for `PIC` block 1) followed per sprite by 4 bytes, the height
  and width words, the X and Y words, one skipped byte, 8 palette bytes and `Row + 1` bytes of pixel
  data (`Row` is the picture's byte size minus one, twice the byte size on `VideoAdapter` 0). A
  sprite is loaded only while `MaxAvail` is at least 3900, and the count becomes the number loaded.
  When it is below 3900 the jump to the loop end also skips the offset advance, so every later
  sprite is read from the middle of the skipped one and the loaded slots keep a nil gap under
  `Count` (QUIRK `ovl28:0269`); the final `FreeMem(Prev, Row + 1)` frees with the last picture's
  `Row` (QUIRK `ovl28:046C`). `PIC` and
  `FINAL` pictures after the first are stored xored with the first one's data, which the loader
  undoes before decoding (`UnpackPicturePixels`); bit 0 of `Mode` adds a pass through `RemapPictureColors` on
  non-zero video adapters **[V]** (matched). Callers: `ovl03`, `ShowEncounterPicture`, `ovl18` (FINAL) and
  `RedrawView`.

### 5.17 The ending narrative and door animation ovl18 **[V]** (`src/OVL18.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl18`: complete, every routine at L2)

Overlay 18 (PoolNarrative) plays the game's ending and animates the area view doors:

- `ShowEndingNarrative` (`ovl18:02A4`, called by `ovl03:2E10`) shows the dragon's death and the
  Tyranthraxus and Bane speech as lines of text at column 1, rows $11 onwards, colour 10
  (`DrawTextAt(1, Row, 10, Text)`) and loads and draws the `FINAL<number>` pictures (the number is
  `Str(TileSet)` in DS:4713) through `LoadPicture` and `DrawPictureItemClipped`, running the animation
  `AnimateFinalPicture(3, 5, 2, x)`, `(3, 1, 7, x)` and `(3, 1, 8, 1)` between the speech blocks. It saves
  the view mode DS:49F3 in DS:4712, sets 7 and restores it.
- `AnimateAreaDoors` (`ovl18:06B3`, called by `ovl03:31B4` and `ovl17:1602`) draws or clears the doors
  of the area view: it calls `RemapPictureColors(@DoorRemapFrom, @DoorRemapToFirst, code, 1, bank)` for each entry of
  DS:0900 (0..10) and `(@DoorRemapFrom, @DoorRemapToSecond, code, 1, bank)` for DS:090B (0..17), where bit 7 of a code
  selects the second SQRPACI bank DS:5E56 instead of DS:5E52 and the low 7 bits are the picture
  number, and it steps the view with `DrawAreaView(DS:094D[I], DS:095F[I])` over 9 steps.

- `AnimateFinalPicture` (`ovl18:0006`, called by `ShowEndingNarrative`) **[V]** (`make match
  UNIT=ovl18 FN=AnimateFinalPicture`) loads the `FINAL<TileSet>` frames of one bank through
  `LoadSpriteList('FINAL', Bank, 2, List)` and shows them `Cycles` times: each frame is drawn with
  `DrawSprite(Col, Row, 1, sprite)` and held for that slot's 4 data bytes, read as a LongInt
  delay scaled by (`GameSpeed` + 3) ticks of the `GameClock` timer, before stepping to the next
  frame. The frame cursor is the sprite list's `Flag` field (the loader sets it to 1, `ovl28:01D6`);
  `Count` is compared as a byte here (`ovl18:00E1` uses a byte `cmp`/`jbe`) although `TSpriteList`
  declares it `ShortInt`, so the source casts it with `Byte()`.
