- **The cloud tile** **[V]** (`ApplyCloudTile`, `ovl24:0AE7`): a combatant standing on tile $1E without affects $1E, $1F, $20, $6F, $7D saves (kind 0): "starts to cough" (affect $1E, one round) or "chokes and gags from nausea" (affect $1F, 1d4 + 1 rounds), a stinking cloud by its texts **[I]**.
- **Map corrections** **[V]**: `ovl24:0475` (the middle of `RunAffectHooks`) and `ovl24:1758` (inside a set constant) were not routines; the unit has 23 routines and the empty initialisation part (`ovl24:1932`).

### 5.22 The camp: studying spells, resting and the Alter menu **[V]** (`src/OVL15.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl15`: unit complete)

- **The camp** **[V]**: `MakeCamp` (`ovl15:1E6F`, entry 1, called by ovl03) sets the view mode `ViewMode` to 2 (restored on leaving), shows "The party makes camp..." and runs the menu "Save View Magic Rest Alter Exit" (`CampMenuText`, `DS:0508`, a string[40] in the data segment like the other menu texts `CampMagicMenuText` `DS:0531`, `AlterMenuText` `DS:055A` and `PartyOrderMenuTexts` `DS:0584`). Save is `SaveGame` followed by "Quit TO DOS "; View is `ViewSelectedCharacter`. Extended keys (G and O) go to `UpdateSelectedCharacter`, which picks another character, in every camp menu.
- **Spell-list selection** **[V]** (`ChooseSpellFromList`, `ovl19:299C`): the first argument chooses both the list label ("in Memory", "in Spell Book", "on Scroll", "on Scrolls", "to choose from", "to be memorized", "to be scribed") and the list passed to `BuildSpellList`; the second argument and `Choice` go to `PickSpellFromMenu`. A zero `Listed` result returns zero. When `Choice` is negative, it opens a one-row window with an empty frame title, redraws the selected character, and draws "Spells " plus the list label at row 1. The function returns the spell byte from `PickSpellFromMenu` **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl19 FN=ChooseSpellFromList`).
- **Studying is pending until the rest** **[V]**: memorizing puts the spell number with bit 7 set into the first empty slot of `TCharacter.Spells` (+$17..+$2B, then sorted by `SortMemorizedSpells`); scribing sets bit 7 on a spell byte of a scroll (`TMenuLine.Affects[1..3]` of an item `IsScroll` accepts). `CancelPartyStudy` clears every pending spell and mark when the camp opens and again when it closes, and No to "Memorize/Scribe These Spells?" cancels them. During `RunRestPeriod`, `ProcessPendingScribedSpell` and `ProcessMemorizedSpell` clear the pending bit when their rest conditions are met and pass the spell and its message text to ConsumeScrollSpell/e8.
- **Rest time** **[V]**: `StudyRestMinutes` (`ovl15:0000`) is 15 minutes per spell level of every pending spell and scroll spell, plus 4 hours when there is one (6 hours when one is above level 2); the hours go to `TCharacter.RestHours` (+$2C). `RestParty` puts the party's largest value into `RestTime` (`DS:6DC2`, seven words: [1] minutes, [2] tens of minutes, [3] hours) for `RunRestPeriod`, whose byte result ends the camp when nonzero, and clears it afterwards.
- **Spell slots** **[V]**: `TCharacter.SpellSlots[class, level]` (+$B2..+$B7, class 0 cleric and 1 magic-user, the `SpellClass` byte of `SpellTable`, signed) less the spells of that class and level in `Spells` are the slots left (`SpellSlotsLeft`); `ShowSpellSlotsLeft` prints them under "Cleric Spells:" and "Magic-User Spells:". `MayUseMagic` refuses casting where game variable 229 is nonzero ("cannot cast spells in this area") and any study when `Status` is 1 or `Flag10D` is 0 ("is in no condition to ...").
- **Scribing** **[V]**: refused for a spell in `KnownSpells` ("You already know that spell"), one already being scribed ("You are already scibing that spell", sic) and one of a class and level without a slot ("You can not scribe that spell.").
- **Display** **[V]** (`DisplaySpellEffects`, `ovl15:0FBC`, matched with its nested `AddDisplayLine`, `ovl15:0E37`, which appends a 46-byte line to the list through the parent's `Last` pointer): a blank line 0, then for every member a heading line (`Flag29` 1) with the name and one line per affect the game can name (" <No Spell Effects>" when none), shown with `ChooseFromList` in the full text window (1,1)..($26,$16) titled "Display", with Exit. The affects by name: a spell affect takes the name of the first spell whose `SpellTable` byte +$A (`AffectId`) is that affect (`SpellNames`, `DS:28E9`, string[40] per spell 1..$38, "Bless" .. "Restoration"), others have fixed names ("Studying Manual of Bodily Health", "Prayer", "Poisoned", "Displaced" ...), unknown spell affects show as "Funky--n".
- **Alter** **[V]**: Order moves the picked member up or down the party list with wraparound (`MoveMemberUp`, `MoveMemberDown`; the list is `PartyListHead` linked by `Next`, +$104); Drop removes a member through `RemoveSelectedCharacter` ("bids you farewell" when `Flag10D` is set, else "is dumped in a ditch") or, for the last member, offers to quit to DOS; Speed moves `GameSpeed` in 0..9; Pics toggles `ShowMonsterPics` (`DS:49F6`) and `ShowPortraits` (`DS:49F5`).
- **Map corrections** **[V]**: 13 map entries of ovl15 were not routines (`0245`, `046C`, `07AA`, `07AF`, `080D`, `0889`, `08A2`, `08AC`, `0999`, `099E`, `1307`, `1BD7` inside routines, `08EA` the strings before `MemorizeSpells`); the unit has 22 routines, the nested `AddDisplayLine` (`ovl15:0E37`) and the empty initialisation part (`ovl15:1FD9`).

### 5.22.1 The rest clock and the rest: ovl20 **[V]** (`src/OVL20.PAS`, `make match UNIT=ovl20`: unit complete)

- **The clock** **[V]**: `RestTime` (`DS:6DC2`, `TRestClock`, seven words) is a mixed-radix clock. `ClockRadix` (`DS:363A`, seven words in the image: 10, 10, 6, 24, 30, 12, 256) says how many units of each digit make one unit of the next: `CarryRestClock` (`ovl20:02F2`) moves a digit at its radix into the next digit and loses the radix. The digit names are **[I]**: 1 minutes, 2 tens of minutes, 3 hours, 4 days, 5 months, 6 years (the radices 10, 6, 24, 30 and 12 and the camp's `RestParty`, 5.22).
- **Age** **[V]**: the top digit's carry (digit 6 at or above its radix 256) adds one to the `Age` word (`+$30`) of every character on the party list (`ovl20:037B`, the `inc word es:[di+30h]`), and the radix stays set: the top digit is never reduced, so once it reaches 256 every later carry call ages the party again (whether the game can reach that is not proven). The whitepaper's save layout has the age at `+$30` [V] (4.2.5).
- **Normalizing** **[V]**: `NormalizeRestTime` (`ovl20:03A3`) carries the clock, then turns the months (digit 5) into days at 30 a month and keeps the days at 99 or less. It runs after every change of the rest time: the Inc and Dec actions of the Rest menu (`RestTimeMenu`, `ovl20:0721`) and each countdown step (`SpendRestTime`, `ovl20:047A`).
- **Spending** **[V]**: `SpendRestTime(Amount, Index)` (`ovl20:047A`) takes `Amount` units off digit `Index`, lending from the next higher nonzero digit (each lent unit gives the radix to the digit below it). When no digit below the months has time left, it clears the clock. It does nothing while minutes, tens, hours and days are all zero. `RunRestPeriod` calls it with Index 1 and Amount 5, so the rest passes five minutes per step.
- **Game clock** **[V]**: `AdvanceGameClock` (`ovl20:03D5`, entry; callers `ovl08:0880` `EndCombatRound` (1, 1), `ovl14:073B` `MoveParty`, `ovl14:0818` `CheckMapCell` and `ovl14:0989` `HandleWildernessKeys` during movement, and `RunRestPeriod` (5, 1) per rest step; the registry also lists `ovl03:2AC1` **[I]**) adds `Amount` units to digit `Index` of the game clock: the seven game variables at 49C6h..49CCh (`VarsAt4900`, the words the ECL machine addresses; the copy and write-back address them as `GameVars` words 198..204). It carries on a local copy, writes the clock back and calls `AdvanceAffectDurations(Amount, Index)`, which spends that elapsed time on timed affects (below).
- **Rest loop and interruption** **[V]**: `RunRestPeriod` (`ovl20:0C9A`) draws the rest time, calls `RestTimeMenu` (and rests only when it returns 1), and returns 1 only after a random interruption. It marks the rest screen up (`RestWindowActive`, read by RES12 to place a message window) while it runs. Each five-minute step checks for a pending key and asks "Stop Resting? "; then it spends five minutes, advances the game clock, checks healing and study progress, and tests the encounter counter `RestInterruptCounter` against the `GameVars2` word `VarRestInterruptSteps` (`$2D2`). When the threshold is reached it resets the counter and interrupts if a 1d100 roll is no greater than the word `VarRestInterruptChance` (`$2D3`). The interruption message is "The Party is rudely interrupted!"; the result ends camp through `RestParty` (`ovl15:077A`).
- **Healing** **[V]**: `CheckRestHealing` (`ovl20:0871`) increments `RestHealCounter` once per rest step. At 0x120 (288 five-minute steps, 24 hours), it heals each party member one hit point (`HealCharacter`, flag 0), shows "The Whole Party Is Healed", redraws the selected character if anyone changed, delays and clears `RestHealCounter`.
- **Study timing** **[V]**: `AdvanceRestStudies` (`ovl20:0B0A`) decrements each member's `RestStudyCountdowns` byte at `DS:6DCF` on every step. At zero, when that member's `RestHours` is zero, it completes a pending scribed spell first, then a memorized spell, and sets the counter to three times the next pending spell's level. `AdvanceRestHour` (`ovl20:0BB2`) increments a shared counter on each call; every twelve calls (one hour) it decrements positive `RestHours`, and when a member reaches zero sets the same counter from the next pending spell level.
- **Completing study** **[V]**: `ProcessPendingScribedSpell` (`ovl20:09E7`) scans a member's carried items for a scroll (`IsScroll`) and spell bytes with bit 7 set. It marks the spell known, calls `ConsumeScrollSpell` with the character, item and spell, then passes `"has scribed"` and the spell to `ShowSpellMessage`. `ProcessMemorizedSpell` (`ovl20:0925`) clears bit 7 from a pending `Spells` slot and passes `"has memorized"` and the spell to `ShowSpellMessage`. Both return the level of a subsequent pending spell when the caller's `Handled` byte is already set. The `Dec` used to clear the byte flag is proven by `tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl20/PRB20_DEC.PAS`.
- **Strings and menu** **[V]**: `TwoDigitText` (`ovl20:0565`) gives a number as two characters: the text of `ByteToText`, with a `0` in front below 10 and only the first two kept. `ShowRestTime` (`ovl20:05F0`) draws `Rest Time:` at column 1 of row 17, then days and hours as `dd:hh` from column 12 (each in its colour, the selected field in 15, the others in 10) and the minutes (`10 * RestTime[2] + RestTime[1]`) at column 18; `Selected` is a `RestTime` digit or 0, and 0 (every caller but the menu) highlights nothing. The rest window `RunRestPeriod` clears is (1,17)-(38,22). `RestTimeMenu` (`ovl20:0721`) loops on `ReadPromptChoice` with the text `Rest   daYs Hours Mins   Inc Dec   Exit` (colours 13, 10, 15, extended keys on) until Rest (result 1), Exit or Escape (result 0); Enter counts as Rest. `Y`, `H` and `M` select the days, hours or minutes field (`RestTime` digits 4, 3 and 2); the left and right arrows (the extended scan codes K and M) step between them with wrap-around; Inc or the up arrow adds 1 to the field (5 to `RestTime[1]` on the minutes field) and Dec or the down arrow takes it off through `SpendRestTime`, each followed by `NormalizeRestTime`. The menu starts on the minutes field (`RestTime` digit 2).
- **Timed affects** **[V]**: `AdvanceAffectDurations` (`ovl20:0000`, called only by `AdvanceGameClock`) turns `Amount` units of digit `Index` into a count of the smallest units. While the digit is above 1 it multiplies by `ClockRadix[Pred(Index)]` and steps the digit down with `Pred`, not `Dec`: ShortInt `Dec` is a byte decrement, `Pred` is `mov/dec/mov`, and `Pred` as an index is `dec` then `cwde` (probe `tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl20/PRB20_A.PAS`). A zero count returns. Otherwise the count is spent in passes of at most 10. Outside camp (`ViewMode` is not 2) every member is marked due first (`FillChar` of `TimedAffectFlagsFrom0`, 72 bytes, to 1). In camp it returns at once when no `TimedAffectFlags` entry is set (`DS:471D`, indexed from 1, so member 1 is `DS:471E`; index 0 is never read). For each marked member it clears the flag and walks `TCharacter.Affects` (+$7F). Duration 0 is permanent and is skipped. An affect whose duration outlasts the pass loses the pass and the flag is set again. One that does not is removed through `RemoveAffect`. The walk keeps the list's tail on entry and, after a removal, resynchronizes a previous-node pointer from the new head until it sits on or just before the next affect. A second walk then sets the flag for any remaining affect whose duration is above 0. QUIRK `ovl20:0118`: the next-affect pointer is loaded from the head before the empty-list test, so a nil list reads through nil and the value is discarded. The frame is 28 bytes for 27 bytes of locals (the compiler rounds an odd frame up). Compiler-proven boundaries removed the false entries `099E`, `09A3`, `09A8`, `0B2A`, `0B48` and `0B89`; they lie inside `ProcessMemorizedSpell` and `AdvanceRestStudies`, not in separate routines.

### 5.22.2 The cast flow and the cast handlers: ovl22 **[V]**

`CastChosenSpell` (`ovl22:0C20`, the unit's entry, `make match UNIT=ovl22 FN=CastChosenSpell`) casts one spell; the combat and camp cast paths call it in the same shape they called `CastChosenSpell`.

- **The cast** **[V]**: with the spell number, `Quick` (the computer plays the cast, so no question), `Announce` and the picker's `Done`, it first refuses a spell whose target mode (`SpellTable[Spell].Pad2[7]`) is 0 outside combat: in the camp it shows the spell's name plus "is a combat-only spell..." and asks "Lose it? " (Yes forgets the spell through `ForgetSpell`), in the wilds it shows "That Item" and "is a combat-only item..." and asks "Use it? " (Yes sets `Done`). Then, when allowed, it shows "NAME casts SPELL" (`ShowSpellMessage`), and calls the picker in DS:6B84 (type `TSpellTargetPicker`, `ChooseSpellTargets` outside combat and `SelectSpellTargets` in it). `Done` 0 means nothing was picked: the player may try again ("Abort Spell? ", unless `Quick`) or the cast is given up ("Spell Aborted"). On a cast it animates the caster (`DrawCombatIcon($12)`, `CombatantX/Y`, then `InDirArc` upward from 0 to turn it towards the aim point `SpellAimX`/`SpellAimY`, a sound keyed on Fireball `$2F`/Lightning Bolt `$33`/else, and a `WalkCombatLine` line), clears the attacker's affect, sets `CastingSpell` and runs the spell's handler `SpellHandlers[CastingSpell]`, then clears `CastingSpell` and `SpellAppliedToArea` and ends with `ClearCharacterMessage`.
- **The spell menu picker** **[V]**: `PickSpellFromMenu` (`ovl22:008A`, entry 1, called only by `ChooseSpellFromList`) shows `SpellMenuList` through `ChooseFromList` in the window at columns 1..$26, rows 5 to the bottom ($0F for Mode 2, else $16), repeating until the key is one of Enter, C, E, L, M, S or #0. `Mode` is `ChooseSpellFromList`'s `DisplayMode`: 1 Cast (the use of an item calls it with 1), 2 Memorize, 3 Scribe, 4 Learn give the button word and the title 'Choose Spell: '; Learn shows no Exit. A negative `Choice` draws the window frame (rows 3 to the bottom) and restarts at 0. The result is the spell number of the chosen line (`SpellMenuSpells[line index without the headings]`), 0 after Exit or Escape; Scribe also copies `SpellMenuItems[index]` to `CurrentItem`. The list is freed before returning. `Choice` is a Word in its callers and reaches `ChooseFromList` (var Integer) through the typecast `Integer(Choice)` (`tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl22/VARCAST.PAS`) (`make match UNIT=ovl22 FN=PickSpellFromMenu`).
- **The handler table** **[V]**: `InitSpellHandlers` (`ovl22:3413`) fills the block at DS:6B84 with the overlay stubs of the cast handlers, element 0 the picker, elements 1..$43 one per spell number (68 far pointers, DS:6B84..6C93; 1 Bless, 2 Curse, 3 Cure Light Wounds, 4 Cause Light Wounds, 5 Detect Magic, ..., and several spells share one handler). It also stores `@MonsterBreath` in DS:6988 (no routine in the image reads it) and clears `RemovingAffects`, `ItemAffectUse` and the picker's chosen character `PickedSpellCharacter`, setting `RedrawItemWindow` to 1. `CastBless` (`ovl22:100F`) applies the current spell to the caster's side with "is Blessed" and `CastCurse` (`ovl22:1040`) to the opposite side with "is Cursed", both through `ApplySpellToSide` (`ovl22:0F4F`), which drops targets whose `Side` differs and, for spell 1 in combat, those the spell's area already reaches (`CollectEnemiesInRange`); `CastCureLightWounds` (`ovl22:106B`) heals the first target 1d8 and reports "is fully/partially healed" through `ShowHealResult`; `CastCauseLightWounds` (`ovl22:10A7`) deals 1d8 damage to the targets with no message.
- **Handlers shared by several spells** **[V]**: element N of the table is spell N, and several spells point at the same stub. `CastDetectionSpell` (`ovl22:10E9`) is spells 5, 11 (Detect Magic), 18 (Read Magic), 22 (Find Traps) and 29 (Detect Invisibility): it applies the current spell's own affect to the targets with "is affected", its only message. `CastProtection` (`ovl22:1123`) is spells 6, 7, 16 and 17 (Protection From Evil and From Good, the cleric and magic-user copies) with "is protected". `CastResistCold` (`ovl22:1162`, spell 8) with "is cold-resistant" and `CastBurningHands` (`ovl22:1190`, spell 9) dealing the caster level in fire damage (kind 9) are single-spell handlers, as is `CastEnlarge` (`ovl22:12A5`, spell 12): it gives the target strength 18/xx, the percentile from the caster level (0, 1, 51, 76, 91, 100 for levels 1..6, DS:4844/DS:4845), through `RaiseStrength` and adds the strength affect $0C.
- **Charm Person** **[V]** (`ovl22:11DD`, spell 10): it refuses a target that is not a person, i.e. whose `CreatureType` (+$9F) or combat footprint kind (`CombatSize` +$6C) is above 1, with "is unaffected"; otherwise it applies the charm affect $0B to the targets with the caster's `Side` in bit 7 of the affect's data (so the charmed creature joins that side) and runs the affect through `CallAffectHandler`. `CreatureType` is the byte after `Sex` (+$9E): MON*CHA has the humanoids at 0 or 1, the undead at 4 and a giant snake at $E (Snake Charm, `ovl22:190F`, wants $E).
- **The spells 13 to 21 handlers** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:136E`, `13D8`, `1439`, `1499`, `14C7`, `151B`): `CastReduce` (`ovl22:136E`, spell 13) has a target that fails its saving throw (kind 4, a spell) lose the strength affect $0C Enlarge adds and reports "has been reduced"; a target that saves, or that has no strength affect, is untouched. `CastFriends` (`ovl22:13D8`, spell 14) raises the target's `Charisma` (+$15) by 2d4, capped at 25, and passes the pre-roll Charisma as the affect's data with the restore flag ("is friendly"). `CastMagicMissile` (`ovl22:1439`, spell 15) deals `RollDiceCounted(Level div 2, 4) + Level div 2`, one d4 plus one point per two caster levels (the dice count goes to DS:681C). `CastShield` (`ovl22:1499`, spell 19) applies the spell's own affect with "is shielded". `CastShockingGrasp` (`ovl22:14C7`, spell 20) deals `Level + 1d8` electrical damage (kind $0C). `CastSleep` (`ovl22:151B`, spell 21) rolls a 4d4 pool into DS:4844 and charges each target DS:4845 by its `HighestLevel` (+$73, a monster's hit dice) and, at level 5, its `Race` (+$2E): 1, 2, 4, 6, 10 (Race 0) or 20; a target that is Animated (Status 1, the undead), already carries affect $35 or costs more than the pool is dropped from the target list (`SpellTargets[I] := nil`), the rest are slept ("falls asleep").
- **The spells 22 to 27 handlers** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:1658`, `17E9`, `1822`, `185B`, `190F`): `SpellNames` is at DS:28E9 as one `string[40]` per index 0..$38 (`SpellNames[1]` is 'Bless' at DS:2912), which names spells 22 Find Traps, 23 Hold Person, 24 Resist Fire, 25 "Silence, 15' Radius", 26 Slow Poison and 27 Snake Charm. `CastHoldPerson` (`ovl22:1658`, spell 23) rolls a saving throw whose bonus falls as more targets are picked (one target -3, or -2 for Hold Person itself, two -1, three or four none), draws the cast beam (`DrawCombatIcon($12)` and a `WalkCombatLine` line) to every chosen target but the first, and applies the spell's own affect ("is held"); a target that is not a person (`CreatureType` or `CombatSize` above 1, as Charm Person tests) counts as having saved. `CastResistFire` (`ovl22:17E9`, spell 24, "is fire resistant") and `CastSilence` (`ovl22:1822`, spell 25, "is silenced") only apply the spell's own affect. `CastSlowPoison` (`ovl22:185B`, spell 26, "is affected") needs the first target to not be Animated (Status 1) and to carry the Poisoned affect $37 (`HasAffect`); it brings a poisoned character at 0 hit points back to 1, applies the spell's affect, runs the revival affect $4E's removal handler (`CallAffectHandler`, ovl24 `AffectRevival`) and adds the poison-slowing affect $0F for $A units. `CastSnakeCharm` (`ovl22:190F`, spell 27, "is charmed") spends the caster's own current hit points (`HpCurrent`) as a pool: it walks the party list DS:5D96 and takes every giant snake (`CreatureType` $E) whose hit points fit the remaining pool. Map correction: `ovl22_fn_1758` was an entry inside `CastHoldPerson` (it has no prologue and holds that routine's loop back edge); `make match WRITE=1` removed it.

- **The spells 28 to 34 handlers** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:19C3`, `1A16`, `1A4F`, `1A8A`, `1ACC`, `1B11`): `InitSpellHandlers`'s table maps spell 28 Spiritual Hammer to `CastSpiritualHammer` (`ovl22:19C3`, entry 32), which applies the spell (Flag 1) and then runs the spell's own affect $17's handler on the first target (`CallAffectHandler`); spell 29 Detect Invisibility shares `CastDetectionSpell` (`ovl22:10E9`). `CastInvisibility` (`ovl22:1A16`, spell 30, "is invisible") and `CastRayOfEnfeeblement` (`ovl22:1ACC`, spell 33, "is weakened") only apply the spell's own affect; `CastKnock` (`ovl22:1A4F`, spell 31, "Knock-Knock") applies no affect at all (Knock's `SpellTable[31].AffectId` is 0), its door effect living in ovl14's wilderness code, which looks for Knock among the memorized spells; `CastMirrorImage` (`ovl22:1A8A`, spell 32, "is duplicated") passes a 1d4 caster level, the number of images. `CastStinkingCloud` (`ovl22:1B11`, spell 34, "Creates a noxious cloud") leaves a marker in the list DS:6DBB (a 19-byte `TCombatNode`: the caster, the aim cell `SpellAimX`/`SpellAimY`, four tiles and four flags for the four aim directions at DS:290E, and the cloud count), paints the cloud tile $1E into `CombatMap` and runs `ApplyCloudTile` (ovl24's stinking cloud saving throw) on the combatant of each cell; a cell that already holds a cloud borrows the tile of the earlier cloud that reaches it, a cell with a body borrows the body's tile (`BodyTable`). New: the four aim directions are the bytes at DS:290E inside `SpellNames[0]`, and `SpellAimX`/`SpellAimY` are signed (the original sign-extends them with `cwde`).
- **The spells 35 to 37 handlers** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:1F41`, `206F`, `2217`): `CastStrength` (`ovl22:1F41`, spell 35) rolls a class bonus die (1d4 for a magic-user level, 1d6 for a cleric or thief level, 1d8 for a fighter level; the last class tested wins) and adds it to the target's `Strength` (+$10), clamping the result at 18; a fighter level turns the overflow into the exceptional-strength percentile (10 per point above 18 plus the target's `StrengthPercent` +$16, capped at 100) and `RaiseStrength` applies it, after which the strength affect $26 is added for the spell's duration. `CastAnimateDead` (`ovl22:206F`, spell 36, "is animated") spends one caster level per corpse: for every member of the party list DS:5D96 with `Status` (+$10C) 6 and `CreatureType` (+$9F) 0 it places the body at its combat cell (`PlaceCombatant`), turns it into an undead on the caster's `Side` (CreatureType 4, `Quick` +$10F set, `SweepAttacks` (+$6B), +$72, the byte at +$76 and the memorized spells reset, B84 $B2 above $7F else $B3, and the combat `Target` cleared in combat) and revives it (`ReviveCombatant` at `HpMax`); the affect's data holds the caster's `Side` in bits 4..7 and the caster level in the low nibble. `CastCureBlindness` (`ovl22:2217`, spell 37, "can see") removes the blindness affect $21 (the affect `SpellTable[38].AffectId` leaves, the same affect the Temple's Cure Blindness removes) from the first chosen target. Map correction: `make match WRITE=1` removed the phantom entries `ovl22_fn_202B`, `ovl22_fn_20A9` and `ovl22_fn_2233`, which lie inside CastStrength, CastAnimateDead and CastCureBlindness. Compiler note: `X * 16` compiles to `mov cl,4; shl ax,cl` but `X shl 4` to `mov cx,4; shl ax,cl` (an untyped shift constant is a word), so the affect data uses a multiply (`tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl22/PRB27.PAS`).
- **The spells 38 to 40 handlers** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:225D`, `228A`, `232F`, `234C`): `InitSpellHandlers`'s table maps spell 38 Cause Blindness to `CastCauseBlindness` (`ovl22:225D`, "is blind", only `ApplySpellToTargets`), spell 39 Cure Disease to `CastCureDisease` (`ovl22:232F`) and spell 40 Cause Disease to `CastCauseDisease` (`ovl22:234C`, "is diseased", Flag 1). `CastCureDisease` calls `CureDiseaseAffects` (`ovl22:228A`, a function that sets `RemovingAffects` while it goes), which cures the six `DiseaseAffects[1..6]` at DS:0114 ($22 on its own, $2B with its companions $2C Cause Disease and $1F Helpless, $32 Dreaded Mummy Disease with $39) from `FirstSpellTarget` and returns whether it cured any; the handler drops that result in an unread local (QUIRK `ovl22:232F`), because `SpellHandlers` is a table of procedures. `SpellTable[39].AffectId` is 0 (Cure Disease leaves no affect), but `SpellTable[40].AffectId` is $22, so Cause Disease's Flag 1 does run its affect's handler on removal. Map correction: `make match WRITE=1` removed `ovl22_fn_228C`, a false boundary in the middle of `CureDiseaseAffects`'s `mov bp,sp` (the entry `ovl22:228C` disassembles `e5 83` as `in ax,83`). Compiler note: a `function F: Boolean; begin F := G; end;` reloads the result at the exit (`mov al,[bp-1]`) but a procedure's local set from the same call does not (`tools/re/tp_idioms/ovl22/PRB28.PAS`), which fixes `CastCureDisease`'s shape.
- **The spells 41 to 43 handlers** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:2385`, `24D6`, `2540`): the handler table runs past the 40 cast spells; `SpellNames` also names 41 Dispel Magic, 42 Prayer, 43 Remove Curse (DS:2F7A, 2FA3, 2FCC). Element 41 Dispel Magic and element 46 Dispel Magic (the cleric and magic-user copies) both point at the stub 0096:00FC = `CastDispelMagic` (`ovl22:2385`); element 42 Prayer is `CastPrayer` (`ovl22:24D6`, stub 0096:0101), element 43 Remove Curse is `CastRemoveCurse` (`ovl22:2540`, stub 0096:004D). `CastDispelMagic` walks the first chosen target's affect list (`Target^.Affects`, +$7F): for every affect whose `Data` (+3) byte is below $FF it takes the effect's level from the low nibble of `Data`, turns the caster level (`SpellCasterLevel(CastingSpell)`) and that level into a chance (50 percent, +5 for each caster level above the effect's level, -2 for each one below), rolls 1d100 and removes the affect (`RemoveAffect`) on a roll up to the chance; when anything went it says "is affected". QUIRK `ovl22:2385`: the outer loop runs `SpellTargetCount` times but its body always reads the same first target `FirstSpellTarget` (it is never indexed by the loop counter), so a cast with several targets rolls on the first one once per target and can say "is affected" more than once. `CastPrayer` applies the spell with the caster's `Side` scaled by 16 above the caster level in the affect's data (the same side-in-the-data trick as Charm Person and Animate Dead) and the message "is praying". `CastRemoveCurse` first cures the curse affect $24 (`CureAffect`) and says "is un-cursed"; otherwise it walks the character's carried items (`FirstSpellTarget^.Items`, +$C8), clears the `Cursed` flag (+$36) of the first cursed item it finds and says "'s item is un-cursed". This routine is also what the Temple's Remove Curse service calls: `ovl04:083A` stores the character in `FirstSpellTarget` and calls it. Map correction: `make match WRITE=1` removed `ovl22_fn_2383` (inside `CastDispelMagic`'s 'is affected' literal pool), `ovl22_fn_23B6` (a false boundary inside `CastDispelMagic`'s code) and `ovl22_fn_257E` and `ovl22_fn_25C9` (inside `CastRemoveCurse`).

- **The spells 44 to 48 handlers and their helpers** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:2604`, `263D`, `266B`, `2764`, `2892`, `28BC`): `SpellNames` names 44 Bestow Curse (DS:2FF5), 45 Blink (DS:301E), 46 Dispel Magic (the magic-user copy, DS:3047), 47 Fireball (DS:3070) and 48 Haste (DS:3099). Element 44 = stub 0096:0106 `CastBestowCurse` (`ovl22:2604`) only applies the spell with "has been cursed!" (its affect is the curse $24). Element 45 = stub 0096:010B `CastBlink` (`ovl22:263D`) only applies it with "is blinking" (affect $25). Element 47 Fireball and element 64 (whose spell name is empty, a monster's fireball) both point at stub 0096:0110 = `CastFireball` (`ovl22:266B`): it sets `SpellAppliedToArea` and takes the caster level from `SpellCasterLevel`, except for spell 64 whose level is the roll 1d3*2+1; when the 3D wall view is off (`GameVars^[VarForceWallView]` 0, which is the combat view) it collects the combatants `ScanTargets` finds within two steps of the aim cell `SpellAimX`/`SpellAimY` (through the sort table `SortEntries`, each hit's `Head` an index into `CombatantRecord`) into `SpellTargets[1..SpellTargetCount]`, moves the cursor to the aim point and applies the spell with `RollDiceCounted(level, 6)` and fire damage (kind 9), no message. Element 48 = stub 0096:0115 `CastHaste` (`ovl22:2892`) hands the caster's own `Side` and the slow affect $2A (`SpellTable[55].AffectId`) to `ApplySpellToSideUpToLevel` (`ovl22:2764`) with "is Hasted". `ApplySpellToSideUpToLevel` applies the current spell to up to the caster level of the chosen targets on one side, but first cures the opposite spell's affect from them: a target on the side with budget left spends one level, and one that already carries the affect has it cured (`CureAffect`) and is dropped from the cast; it then applies the spell and runs the affect hooks $12 on the survivors. Haste passes the slow affect $2A and the Slow handler (`ovl22:2C09`, stub 0096:011F) passes the haste affect $27, so each removes the opposite spell before it casts. `DamageTargetInCell` (`ovl22:28BC`), the line spells' per-cell helper (called by `CastLightningBolt`, the Lightning Bolt handler at stub 0096:011A, and by `CastLineBeam`), reads a cell with `GetMapCell` and reports in its var flag that the tile refuses the beam (`CombatTiles[Tile].Cost` = $FF), then damages the combatant standing there with electrical damage (kind $0C), the spell's saving throw and half damage on a save, and plays `DrawCombatIcon($13)`. The other table elements past 48: 49 Hold Person shares `CastHoldPerson`, 50 "Invisibility, 10' Radius" shares `CastInvisibility`, 51 Lightning Bolt is `CastLightningBolt`, 52 to 54 the two "Protection From Evil/Good, 10' Radius" and "Protection From Normal Missiles" share `CastProtection`, 55 Slow is `CastSlow`, 56 Restoration is `CastRestoration`, and 65 to 67 are `CastMonsterMagicDamage`, `CastMonsterNoEffect` and `CastMonsterReadMagic`.

- **The spells 51, 55 and 56 handlers and the line-beam helper** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:295B`, `2BB7`, `2C09`, `2C43`): element 51 Lightning Bolt is `CastLightningBolt` (`ovl22:2BB7`), which rolls one d6 per caster level (`RollDice(SpellCasterLevel(CastingSpell), 6)`), deals it to the combatant on the aim cell `SpellAimX`/`SpellAimY` (`DamageTargetInCell`, electrical, save kind 4) and then runs the beam helper `CastLineBeam` (`ovl22:295B`) with reach 8, the same damage and save kind and the bounce flag 1. `CastLineBeam` starts at the aim cell (the zap animation `DrawCombatIcon($13)`), takes the caster's cell (`CombatantX/Y(SelectedCharacter)`) and walks a `TLineState` (`SetupLine`/`StepLine`) away from the caster for `Range * 2` cells, drawing each step (`WalkCombatLine` step 4, width $32) and damaging it (`DamageTargetInCell`); the walk ends when the line leaves the map (tile 0), at a tile whose `CombatTiles` cost is above 1, at a fresh occupant, at the line's end, or when the reach is spent. When the tile refuses the beam and the bounce flag is set it moves `SpellAimX`/`SpellAimY` to the bounce point, walks a second line back to the caster, turns the beam around once (the step sign `0 - DirSign` becomes the other sign and the bounce flag clears) and gains 8 steps when the return line's `First` is at most 8; it sets `SpellAppliedToArea`. Element 55 Slow is `CastSlow` (`ovl22:2C09`), which hands the opposite side (`OpposingSide(SelectedCharacter)`) and the haste affect $27 to `ApplySpellToSideUpToLevel` with "is Slowed" (the mirror of Haste). Element 56 Restoration is `CastRestoration` (`ovl22:2C43`): the selected character's drained levels are `TCharacter.Pad74[$74]` and the hit points lost with them `Pad74[$75]` (the three hit-point numbers are +$32 max, +$11B current and +$75 lost, ovl33:0004), so one restored level gives back `Pad74[$75] div Pad74[$74]` hit points, added to HpMax, the current hit points and HpB1, and the drained-level count drops; it then picks the class to advance in `ClassLevels` (+$96): over the eight classes it takes the one whose current level is not above the best so far and whose entry `Succ(Level)` in the experience table `ExperienceTable` (DS:409B, `array[0..7, 0..13] of LongInt`, one more than the AD&D threshold) is the smallest positive value (a call to `IsMaxClassLevel` must be False), advances that class by one, raises Experience (+$AC) to at least that entry and recomputes through `RecalcClassStats` with "is restored". QUIRK `ovl22:2C43`: `BestClass` is never initialized, only set by an accepted class. Map correction: `make match WRITE=1` removed `ovl22_fn_2B8B` and `ovl22_fn_2D39`, false boundaries inside `CastLineBeam`'s and `CastRestoration`'s code. Compiler notes: the guard `if (X1 <> X2) or (Y1 <> Y2) then repeat ... until ...` (no else) gives the direct `jne body; ... je after` (probe GUARD.PAS); a signed negation written `0 - X` gives `mov dx,ax; xor ax,ax; sub ax,dx`, not `neg ax` (probe NEG.PAS); and `Succ(Level)` gives `inc al; cwde` where `Level + 1` gives `cwde; inc ax` (probe INDEX.PAS).

- **The monster spell handlers 57 to 60** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:2E02`, `2E4D`, `2EE5`, `2F4D`): the handler table runs past the 56 named spells; elements 57 to 67 are spells with no name (`SpellNames[57]` and above are empty), so they are the monster-only spells, like element 64, the monster fireball, already documented in the 44 to 48 section. Element 57 = stub 0096:0129 `CastMonsterHaste` (`ovl22:2E02`) cancels the slow affect $2A from the first chosen target `FirstSpellTarget` (through `CureAffect`, which shows "is Cured") and, when the target was not slowed, applies the current spell with "is Speedy": the single-target sibling of Haste. Element 58 = stub 0096:012E `CastMonsterHeal` (`ovl22:2E4D`) cures `FirstSpellTarget`: with the Poisoned affect $37 present it also removes the companion affect $16 (the pair ovl04's Neutralize Poison removes) with `RemovingAffects` set, else it cures disease (`CureDiseaseAffects`) and, when neither applies, heals 1d4 + 8 (`HealCharacter`) with "is Healed". Element 59 = stub 0096:0133 `CastMonsterStrength` (`ovl22:2EE5`) gives `FirstSpellTarget` a plain Strength 21 (`RaiseStrength`, no percentile), shows "is stronger" and adds the strength affect $26 for `SpellAffectDuration`. Element 60 = stub 0096:0138 `CastMonsterBeam` (`ovl22:2F4D`) strikes the aim cell `SpellAimX`/`SpellAimY` for 1d6 + 20 (`DamageTargetInCell`, save kind 4) and runs a short `CastLineBeam(3, 20, 4, 0)`.
- **The monster spell handlers 61 to 66** **[V]** (`make match UNIT=ovl22` on `ovl22:2F99`, `2FD0`, `302A`, `3058`, `3092`): elements 61 to 66 of the handler table are monster-only spells whose `SpellNames` entry is empty. Element 61 = stub 0096:013D `CastMonsterParalyze` (`ovl22:2F99`) only applies the current spell with "is paralyzed" (its own affect paralyses, as `CastCauseBlindness` leaves the blinding to its affect). Element 62 = stub 0096:0142 `CastMonsterHealWounds` (`ovl22:2FD0`) heals the first chosen target `FirstSpellTarget` for 2d4 + 2 (`HealCharacter`, no flag) and says "is Healed" (`ShowHitMessage`, Flag 1); it is the plain-heal sibling of `CastMonsterHeal` (element 58), which cures poison or disease first. Element 63 = stub 0096:0147 `CastMonsterInvisibility` (`ovl22:302A`) only applies the spell with "is invisible". Element 65 = stub 0096:014C `CastMonsterMagicDamage` (`ovl22:3058`) applies it for 2d4 + 2 magic damage (kind 8, `RollDiceCounted`, its count in `LastDiceCount`) with no message: that is the roll `CastMagicMissile` deals for a caster of level 3 or 4 (two missiles of 1d4 + 1), fixed here because a monster has no caster level. Element 66 = stub 0096:0151 `CastMonsterNoEffect` (`ovl22:3092`) is an empty handler, so after the picker (which runs for every spell) the cast does nothing.
- **Map corrections and a compiler note** **[V]**: `ovl22_fn_2EEA` and `ovl22_fn_2F57` were not routines. The first is the middle of `CastMonsterStrength`'s `sub sp, 0xe` read as `push cs`; the second is the second half of `CastMonsterBeam`, whose first ten bytes are the prologue and the first push. `make match WRITE=1` removed both and corrected `ovl22:2F4D` from 10 to 63 bytes. Likewise `ovl22_fn_2FD3` was the middle of `CastMonsterHealWounds`'s prologue (`sub sp, 0xa` read as a routine start; nothing calls it and no entry stub points at it, overlay entry 58 jumps to `ovl22:2FD0`); `make match WRITE=1` removed it and corrected `ovl22:2FD0` from 3 to 77 bytes. Compiler note: the local frame is rounded up to a word, so a routine whose only local is an odd-length string temporary reserves one extra unused byte; it must be declared (`CastMonsterHeal`'s `Spare`) for the frame and the temporary's offset to land where the original put them.

### 5.23 The picture unit res07 **[V]** (`src/RES07.PAS`, `make match UNIT=res07`: unit complete)

- **The picture record** **[V]**: `TPicture` holds Height and WidthCols as words at +0/+2, XPos and YPos at +4/+6, ItemCount at +8, the 8-byte palette at +9, ByteSize (bytes per item) at +$11, the pixel-buffer pointer at +$13 and the packed pixel data from +$17 (data indexed with range checks off, like ovl32's TMemPic). `AllocatePicture` (`0464:248E`) allocates `Height*WidthCols*2` bytes per item (doubled when VideoAdapter is not 0) times ItemCount plus $17, zeroes it, stores the header, and with bit 0 of AllocFlags allocates the pixel buffer (a quarter of the size when VideoAdapter is 1); `ReleasePicture` (`0464:2595`) frees both, with a `with PPicture(Pointer(Pic))^` over the untyped var.
- **Loading** **[V]**: `LoadPicture` (`0464:0111`, 404 bytes) frees any picture already in the caller's pointer, calls `LoadDaxBlock(Text, '.dax', BlockId, Size, Block)`, copies Height/WidthCols/XPos/YPos/ItemCount/Palette into locals with `Move`, allocates, stores XPos/YPos/Palette into the new picture and calls `UnpackPicturePixels` (`0464:02A5`, matched, below) with (Block, $11, AllocFlags, Color, Target^); then frees the DAX block and calls FlushKeyboard. It leaves one byte of its frame unused (QUIRK `0464:0114`).
- **The unit is compiled with O+** **[V]**: like res08, res07 is resident but was built with `{$O+}`: a string constant passed to a routine is copied to a stack temporary through `System_StrCopy` (`0AF8:0B0B`) at `0464:0152` (`LoadDaxBlock`'s '.dax' under the overlay-copy rule), and all 14 matched routines match with `{$A-,O+,F+,S-,R-,G-,I-}`. This is more evidence for the res08 escalation (O+ is per unit, not overlay-units-only).
- **The mirrors** **[V]**: `MirrorPicture` (`0464:15C7`, 906 bytes) copies a picture mirrored: the palette as is, then each pixel row reversed per VideoAdapter (each 16-bit unit through `MirrorWord` 0464:1509, which reverses the eight 2-bit groups using `GlyphPixelMasks`; each byte through `MirrorByte` 0464:1484 in 4-byte groups; or through `SwapNibbles` 0464:1596), and the pixel buffer at +$13 mirrored too (word by word, byte by byte within the row, or in place). ovl32 DrawIcon uses it for icon sets above 3.
- **The direct blit** **[V]**: `ShowPicture` (`0464:1951`, 513 bytes) copies a picture's pixel data straight to the video hardware: adapter 0 moves one WidthCols*2-byte row per screen row to the segment `CgaScanlineSegments[Row]` at column `PicCol*2`; adapter 1 writes, per row and per cell column, the four plane bytes of the source cell (the plane-interleaved layout, 4 bytes per cell; each byte holds eight pixels of one EGA plane, written through the map mask `EgaPlaneBitMasks`, new global at DS:25F6, to address `EgaScanlineOffsets[Row]+Col` in segment $A000); adapter 2 is the adapter-0 move with four bytes per column and the `TandyScanlineSegments` table.
- **The changed-region flush** **[V]**: the blitters mark changed screen rows in `RowDirtyFlags` (a byte flag per row), the first (lowest) changed byte offset of the staging buffer `DrawBuf` in `RowFirstChangedOfs` (a minimum, starts $FFFF) and the last (highest) in `RowLastChangedOfs` (starts 0), and the screen-side byte column of that first changed byte in `RowFirstChangedCol` (`LeftCol * 2/1/4` on adapters 0/1/2); `FlushPictureChanges` (`0464:1B52`, 778 bytes) presents them per adapter (row moves to the `CgaScanlineSegments`/`TandyScanlineSegments` segments at that column, or the four planes through port 3C5 plus a Graphics-Controller write-mode-1 copy from $A200 to $A000) and then clears the trackers. The blitters `DrawPictureItem` (`0464:08D4`, matched, below) and `DrawPictureItemClipped` (`0464:0D71`, matched, below) mark the same trackers.
- **Video mode and palette** **[V]**: `SetVideoMode` (`0464:00CF`) saves the current mode (`CurrentVideoMode`) in `SavedVideoMode` and sets INT 10h function 0 with the new mode; `RestoreVideoMode` (`0464:00FF`) sets the saved one back. `SetPaletteRegister` (`0464:2A0D`) writes one EGA palette register through INT 10h AH=10h AL=0 (BH = the colour, mapped from 0..7 as is and 8.. as +8, BL = the register). `ColorMatchMask` (`0464:0000`) returns the bit mask of the eight 4-bit pixels of a 4-byte cell that equal a colour; `PaletteColor` (`0464:007B`) returns a palette colour for a pixel value.
- **Map corrections** **[V]**: 19 of the 39 map entries of res07 were not routines (they have no `55 89 E5` prologue; aligned disassembly runs through them): 066F/0710 inside 02A5, 0A33/0C0C/0D6C inside 08D4, 0E01..140E (11) inside 0D71, 1514 inside 1509, 1708 inside 15C7, 1A19 inside 1951. Six routines were resized (02A5 1583, 08D4 1181, 0D71 1811, 1509 141, 15C7 906, 1951 513) and 00FF is 13 bytes plus a 5-byte literal pool, not 18. res07 has 19 routines plus the empty initialisation part.
- **Unpacking** **[V]** (`UnpackPicturePixels`, `0464:02A5`, 1583 bytes, `make match`): the DAX pixel data (4 bytes = one 8-pixel cell, read from offset $11) becomes the picture's data per VideoAdapter. Adapter 0 turns each pixel's nibble into its palette colour (`PaletteColor`, `0464:007B`) and that colour into 2-bit pairs through `PixelBitsByColor[colour, position]`, two bytes per cell; with a pixel buffer (`Pixels`, AllocFlags bit 0) it also stores the mask of the pixels equal to `Color` (`ColorMatchMask`, `0464:0000`) and clears those bits of the data. Adapter 1 keeps the raw nibble: each pixel's colour bits select the EGA planes through `EgaPlaneBitMasks`, one byte per plane per cell. Adapter 2 copies `ByteSize` bytes per item unchanged. The third argument (LoadPicture's AllocFlags) is never read; the frame keeps 17 bytes that are never touched (QUIRK `0464:02A5`).
- **Fill** **[V]** (`FillPictureRect`, `0464:2632`, 987 bytes): called by `DrawWallDescriptors` (`ovl29:0174`, four times, with FirstCol = 2, the wall descriptor pixel rows and the colours `WallDescriptor1..WallDescriptor4`) and by `DrawIconCell` (`ovl16:34DF`); Width and FirstCol are cells of 8 pixels while FirstRow and Height are pixel rows (one loop pass per pixel row), so it fills a Width-cell by Height-pixel-row rectangle of the picture with the palette colour, clipped at all four edges, marks the trackers, and per adapter writes two bytes (0, colour through `PixelsByColor2Bit`), four bytes (2, `PixelsByColor4Bit`) or the four plane bytes (1, `EgaPlaneBitMasks`) per cell. QUIRK `0464:2707`: a rectangle that starts above row 0 is drawn one row too far (the bound is `Height - |FirstRow|` with an inclusive loop).
- **Item copy** **[V]** (`DrawPictureItem`, `0464:08D4`, 1181 bytes): copies item `PicIndex` of the picture `Source` into `Dest` at column `Col` and pixel row `Row * 8`, one source row of bytes at a time (2 bytes per cell on adapter 0, 4 on 1 and 2). With `Flags` bit 0 and a pixel buffer in `Source`, a destination byte becomes `(dest and mask) or source`; adapter 1 reads one mask byte per four data bytes. It does no clipping of its own (`RightRoom` is a Word, added as it is). Callers: `DrawCellPicture` (`ovl32:00F1`) and `Put8x8Symbol` (`ovl34:015D`).
- **The fill tables** **[V]** (bytes of `start-unpack.exe` at DS:0D02, DS:0D06, DS:25FA, DS:25E5, DS:25F6): `PixelsByColor2Bit[C]` is four 2-bit pixels of colour C (`00 55 AA FF` for C 0..3), `PixelsByColor4Bit[C]` two 4-bit pixels of colour C (C * `$11`); the two overlap (`PixelsByColor2Bit[C + 4]` is `PixelsByColor4Bit[C]`). `PixelBitsByColor` is a 16 x 4 table (`colour, position`): for colours 0..3 the colour's 2-bit value in the bit pair of that pixel (colour 1 at position 0 is `$40`); colours 4..15 do not follow that pattern. `GlyphPixelMasks` (words `C000 3000 0C00 0300 00C0 0030 000C 0003`) and `EgaPlaneBitMasks` (`01 02 04 08`) are the plane and pixel masks. The meaning of the fill bytes for colours 4..15 is **[?]**.
- **Clipped blit** **[V]** (`DrawPictureItemClipped`, `0464:0D71`, 1811 bytes, matched): the clipping version of `DrawPictureItem`. While `ClipChecksEnabled` is set (the loader sets it) it cuts the item to `Dest` on all four sides (source cells cut on the left or right, pixel rows cut above or below), and returns without drawing when a cut is larger than the picture; with `ClipChecksEnabled` zero it draws with no check. Flags bit 2 first copies the destination bytes it is about to overwrite into the screen buffer `IconBackgroundBuffer` (save under; the reader is not decompiled yet). The matched code shows the checks as one `or` per exit, and an `if ... else` whose default is stored twice (`SrcLastRow`).
- **Color remap** **[V]** (`RemapPictureColors`, `0464:1E5C`, 1586 bytes, matched): recolours a picture for every colour index I whose `FromMap` and `ToMap` entries differ: each pixel whose palette colour is `FromMap[I]` becomes `ToMap[I]`, computed into a scratch copy (`AllocatePicture`) that is copied back at the end. Adapter 0 compares 2-bit pixel pairs through `PixelBitsByColor` and `GlyphPixelMasks`, adapter 1 the four EGA planes through `EgaPlaneBitMasks` (a random subset of the matching pixels of a cell when flicker is on), adapter 2 the two nibbles of each byte. QUIRK `0464:1F51`: the header inside the colour loop tests the first item again, so a negative first item recolours item 0 only. Callers: `LoadPictureSlot` (`ovl32:01C8`), `LoadSpriteList` (`ovl28:00A2`), `AnimateAreaDoors` (`ovl18:06B3`), `CopyPictureSlot` and `BuildCharacterIcon`.

### 5.24 The resident character and item helper unit res12 **[V]** (`src/RES12.PAS`, `make match UNIT=res12`: unit complete)

res12 is the resident helper the overlays call for character, item and view work. The unit is
complete **[V]** (`make match UNIT=res12`: 52 routines, code and padding cover the segment).
`RecalcCombatValues` recomputes a character's
combat values and calls `SetArmourMovement` (`00BA:01FB`), `AddReadiedItemBonus` (`00BA:0282`),
`ApplyWeaponBonuses` (`00BA:0000`) and `CapMovementByLoad` (`00BA:03A0`); `IsHelpless`
(`00BA:0B73`) tests the four affects that leave a character helpless; the four panel routines
`DrawPartyRoster` (`00BA:0767`), `DrawArmourClass` (`00BA:08EF`), `DrawHitPoints` (`00BA:0973`) and
`DrawCharacterPanel` (`00BA:09E3`) draw the shop, Temple, combat and inventory character panels.
The unit is compiled with **`$O+`** even though it is resident: every string literal passed to a
`string` parameter is copied to a stack temporary first (proven by a `make tpc` probe: `$O-` passes
the literal's code-segment address, `$O+` copies it), and `DrawPartyRoster`/`DrawCharacterPanel`
matched only with the copy.

- **Movement** **[V]**: the character's base movement is the byte at +$72 (`BaseMovement`);
  `SetArmourMovement` copies it to Movement (+$11C) for an armour (item kind 2) below weight
  $97, uses 9 up to $18F and 6 above, and adds 3 for a magic armour while the movement is at
  most 9. `CapMovementByLoad` then caps Movement by the carried weight (+$102) above the
  strength allowance `StrengthWeightAllowance`: excess up to $200 keeps it, up to $300 caps at 9, up to
  $400 at 6, above at 3, and the cap never raises it.
- **Strength and its adjustments** **[V]**: `StrengthRowIndex` (`00BA:11EA`) turns a character's
  Strength (+$10) plus its exceptional StrengthPercent (+$16) into a 0..30 row of the strength
  table: strength 0..17 is itself, an 18 with percent 0, 1..50, 51..75, 76..90, 91..99 or 100
  becomes 18..23, and strength 19..25 becomes 24..30. `StrengthAttackAdjustment` (`00BA:1282`)
  and `StrengthDamageAdjustment` (`00BA:1325`) pick the to-hit and damage bonus from that row,
  and `StrengthWeightAllowance` (`00BA:13AB`) picks the weight a character can carry in coins
  from it (rows 1..3 give -350, 12..13 100, 16 350, 17..21 step by 250, 22..26 by 1000 and
  28..30 by 3000 from 9000). The byte `StrengthBonusFlag` at +$AA gates only the attack and
  damage adjustments (a 0 suppresses them); what it marks is not proven. `ForgetSpell`
  (`00BA:148B`) clears the first memorized spell (`SelectedCharacter^.Spells`, +$17) equal to a
  spell number; `DealDamage` calls it when a victim loses a pending spell.
- **Readied items** **[V]**: `AddReadiedItemBonus` reads the item kind's ItemTable byte 6: bit 7
  clear means the item contributes nothing; with it set the low seven bits are a bonus class.
  A kind-1 item assigns bonus class plus Plus to a kind bonus; other items with class 0 sum
  their Plus (a kind-9 item keeps the largest instead) and always add PlusSave (+$33, signed)
  to `SaveBonus` (+$101); a class above 0 keeps the largest class plus Plus and, for a positive
  plus on an armour, sets a flag. `ApplyWeaponBonuses` builds the readied weapon's attack values
  from its ItemTable row: HitAdjust (+$110) is reset to Thac0 (+$2D), the flag byte $E adds the
  dexterity roll adjustment (`DexterityAttackAdjustment`, mask `WeaponFlagDexToHit` = 2) and the strength roll adjustment
  (`StrengthAttackAdjustment`, mask `WeaponFlagStrength` = 4), WeaponAttacks (+$115) and WeaponDamage (+$117) come from bytes 9 and
  $A, and the signed WeaponDamageBonus (+$119) from byte $B plus the strength damage adjustment
  (`StrengthDamageAdjustment`) and the plus of the weapon and, by mask `WeaponFlagSlot11Plus` (1) and `WeaponFlagSlot12Plus` ($80), of slot items 11
  (+$F8) and 12 (+$FC). The ItemTable kind column is 0 weapon, 1 plus item, 2 armour, 9 special.
- **Recalculating the combat values** **[V]**: `RecalcCombatValues` (`00BA:0BB8`) is the routine
  every equipment change calls (ovl03, ovl06, ovl08..ovl10, ovl12, ovl13, ovl16, ovl19, ovl21,
  ovl24). It clears the 13 per-kind slots (`SlotItems`, +$CC); walks the carried item list (`Items`,
  +$C8) to count the items (`ItemCountC7`, +$C7), add each item's `Weight` (+$37) times its `Count`
  (+$39) to the carried weight (+$102) and to `ReadiedItemWeight` (DS:4846), and file each readied
  item into its slot (item kinds 0..8 into `SlotItems[kind]`, kind 9 into the first free of slots 9
  and 10, item type $49 into slot 11 and $1C into slot 12), raising `HandsInUse` (+$100) by
  ItemTable[item type][1]. It then adds the seven carried money words (+$88) to the weight (every coin
  counts one), copies the base attack triple (+$A3, +$A5, +$A7) into the readied weapon triple
  (+$115, +$117, +$119) and the base value +$A9 (ovl16:05E4 sets 50) into `ArmourClassValue` (+$111),
  resets `SaveBonus` (+$101), `Movement` (+$11C) and `HitAdjust` (+$110), adds the unarmed strength
  bonuses (`StrengthAttackAdjustment`, `StrengthDamageAdjustment`) when no weapon is readied, and runs `ApplyWeaponBonuses`
  and, per readied item, `SetArmourMovement` and `AddReadiedItemBonus`. A readied item whose
  `NameNum[1]` is $BA (the Bag of Holding [I], which weighs little) instead counts 5000 less and the weight is floored at
  `ReadiedItemWeight`. A positive plus on a readied armour clears the accumulator's `BestPlus`, and
  `CapMovementByLoad` then caps `Movement` by the load. Only after that does `BestValue` reach its
  final value: it is first raised to the base `ArmourClassValue`, which then becomes the sum of the
  accumulator's five bytes (the dexterity adjustment, the kind bonus, the plus sum, `BestPlus` and
  `BestValue`), and `RearArmourClassValue` (+$112) becomes `BestValue + PlusSum + BestPlus - 2` (the dexterity
  adjustment and the kind bonus do not go into it). `SweepAttacks` (+$6B) is set last: the fighter
  level (`ClassLevels[2]`) when it is above 0 and the race is above 0, else 1. The base and
  readied attack values are each a pair of bytes (entry 1 is the one the AI and the weapon code read),
  which is what forced the `TCharacter` fields at +$A3, +$A5, +$A7, +$115, +$117 and +$119 to be
  two-element arrays.
- **Helpless** **[V]**: `IsHelpless` (`00BA:0B73`) returns True when any of the four affects in
  `HelplessAffects` (DS:286B, entries 1..4 = $33, $34, $35, $1F) is on the character's affect list
  (`HasAffect`). ovl13:1FC3 compares the same entries with the 16-byte combat table at DS:3204;
  `DrawCharacterPanel` shows "(Helpless)" for a True answer.
- **The item name table** **[V]**: 256 strings of at most 20 characters at DS:10A7 (the image:
  entry 1 `Battle Axe` through entry 255 `Cursed`, entry 0 unused). `FormatItemLine` (`00BA:0442`)
  builds a menu line's Text from NameNum[1..3] (part 3 down to 1, skipping a part whose HiddenNames
  bit is set), with the readied Yes/No, a '* ' when a party member has affect 5 and the item is
  plus or cursed, the count, and one plural 's ' on the head name part. It draws the line in green
  when ShowText is set. The plural test at 00BA:06A6 is `not Odd(Mask)` (bit 0 of the shown-parts
  mask: `mov al,Mask; shr al,1; jae`), not a test of bit 1. QUIRK 00BA:0442: the LongInt Value
  parameter is never read. The item field at +$33 (`PlusSave`) is signed.
- **The character panels** **[V]**: `DrawPartyRoster` (`00BA:0767`) draws the
  "Name / AC  HP" header and one line per party member (the list `PartyListHead`, linked by
  TCharacter.Next +$104), clearing each line and drawing the selected member in white and the
  others through `DrawCharacterName`; the left column is 1 in the plain full-window layout
  (`ViewModePlain`, the create-character and training screens) and $11 in every other view (the shop
  and the Temple `ViewModeShop`, the camp, the area views and the loot screen). `DrawCharacterPanel` (`00BA:09E3`) draws the combat/inventory box ($17,1)-($26,$15):
  the name line (`ShowCharacterMessage`), the "Hitpoints" value (`DrawHitPoints`, current hit points
  +$11B coloured Yellow below HpMax +$32, Green at it, LightMagenta on a highlight flag), the
  "AC" value (`DrawArmourClass`, `Abs(+$111 - 60)` signed in Green), the readied weapon's text
  (`FormatItemLine` then `ShowPagedText`), and the status name `StatusNames[Status]` (+$10C, the
  eight string[12] entries at DS:1047: Okay Animated tempgone Running Unconscious Dying Dead
  Stoned), or "(Helpless)" when `IsHelpless` says so. The value at +$111 is the one
  `ovl24`'s `RollBeatsResistance` compares a d20+level against.
- **Item-list helpers** **[V]**: the carried-item list is `TCharacter.Items` (+$C8), linked through
  `TMenuLine.Next` (+$2A). `RemoveInventoryItem` (`00BA:1509`) unlinks an item and frees the record
  (63 bytes): it re-points the head when the item is `Items`, else walks for the record whose Next is
  the item, reports through `ShowMessageLine` (Yellow) on a nil item ("Nil Item pointer...") or one not
  on the list ("Tried to Lose item & couldn't find it!"), then always clears `Item^.Next` and frees it;
  ovl07 `LoseItems` calls it for every lost item. `AddItemCopy` (`00BA:15F7`) GetMems a 63-byte
  record, `Move`s the item into it, clears the copy's Next and appends it at the end of `Items`; it is
  the "give a copy of an item" helper, ovl12 and ovl19 call it.
- **Message helpers** **[V]**: `ShowCharacterMessage` (`00BA:16EC`) is the per-character message box:
  in the combat view mode (`ViewMode` = 5) it clears the box ($17,Row)-($26,$15) and in every other view
  a fixed box, top row $11 or $12 (`RestWindowActive` picks and the $11 case first draws the frame); it then
  draws the character's name through `DrawCharacterName` and the wrapped text through `ShowPagedText`
  (Green, cleared first) and, when the wait byte is set, delays `DelayByGameSpeed` and clears the box
  through `ClearCharacterMessage` (`00BA:17E3`, box ($17,$A)-($26,$15) in view mode 5, else
  (1,$12)-($26,$16)). `ShowFlashMessage` (`00BA:1693`) flashes a `String40` on the bottom row 24 in
  Green (clear, draw, `DelayByGameSpeed`, clear); `DrawCharacterName` (`00BA:1816`) draws a
  character's `Name` (+0) at (Col,Row) in the colour of its combat state (LightRed when `Flag10D` is 0,
  Yellow on the party side 1, LightCyan otherwise) and, when its Mode byte is set, appends the literal
  `'s` (the bytes `02 27 73`) after the name at Col plus its length.
- **The combat turn, the enemy scan and the spell level** **[V]**: `EndCombatTurn` (`00BA:2637`)
  ends a combatant's turn (clears `Combat^.PendingSpell` +$0, `Initiative` +$3, the guard byte `B7`
  +$7 and `Moves` +$6) and returns 1; `GuardTurn` (`00BA:2686`) calls it, sets `B7` to 1 and flashes
  'Guarding' (the Done menu's Guard command, ovl08:0E45, and the AI fallback `EndOrGuardTurn`).
  `CollectEnemiesInRange` (`00BA:2433`) runs `ScanTargets` from Who within Range steps, keeps only the
  records whose combatant is on `OpposingSide(Who)`'s side (three bytes each, compacted in place), and
  writes the kept combatant indexes into `CombatantScanList` from entry 1, returning the count; `AiTurn` picks a
  random one and `SpellAccepted` loops them for the area spells. `DistanceToTarget` (`00BA:255B`)
  saves the scan table, opens the WalkLine tile gate (`CombatMap^.Head6`, +$6), scans omni from Who
  over $FF steps and returns half the shortest walk length to Target (the distance in map cells,
  which tests = 1 as melee range); the table is restored before the return. `SpellCasterLevel`
  (`00BA:26C2`) returns the selected character's level in a spell's class (`ClassLevels`: 0 cleric,
  5 magic-user), 12 for spell class 2, and a forced 6 while `ItemAffectUse` is set; SpellAreaSize multiplies the
  spell row's +3 byte by it. QUIRK `00BA:26FB`: a spell class other than 0, 1 or 2 leaves the result
  unassigned.
- **Map artifacts** **[V]**: `00BA:2571` was not a routine but the middle of `DistanceToTarget`
  (`00BA:255B`, whose real size is 220 bytes); `make match WRITE=1` removed it. `00BA:06D5` and
  `00BA:0712` were not routines but labels inside `FormatItemLine` (`00BA:0442` to `00BA:075A`,
  793 bytes; 06D5 starts one byte late, under an `es:` prefix the map dropped);
  `make match WRITE=1` removed them. `00BA:0B4F` was the same inside `DrawCharacterPanel`
  (removed by `make match WRITE=1`).
  `00BA:1857` and `00BA:187D` were the same inside `DrawCharacterName` (`00BA:1816`, whose real size
  is 124 bytes, not the 65 the map guessed; `make match WRITE=1` removed both).
- **The view dispatcher and the screens** **[V]**: `RedrawView` (`00BA:27D9`) redraws the whole
  screen for the current view mode (`ViewMode`, the resident unit's view dispatcher): mode 0 draws the
  plain full window, mode 1 the character portrait screen (`DrawViewWindows`, then the HEAD and BODY
  sprites at (3,3) through `LoadHeadBodySprites`/`ShowHeadBodySprites`), mode 2 the camp (the
  portrait windows, the PIC block $1D sprite list and the roster), modes 3 and 4 the area view
  (`DrawViewWindows`, `ShowAreaView` and the roster; only mode 4 draws the inner window) and mode 6
  the loot screen (the portrait windows, the PIC block 1 sprite list and the roster); modes 2, 3 and
  4 draw the roster and the location line, mode 6 the roster only and modes 0 and 1 neither. `DrawViewWindows` (`00BA:272A`) draws
  the view's window frames through `DrawWindow`: the full screen (1,1)-(38,22), the bottom text
  window (1,17)-(38,22) and the upper-left window (1,1)-(15,15), plus the inner window
  (3,3)-(13,13) that frames the portrait or the 3D view. `DrawWildernessScreen` (`00BA:2B5E`) is the
  wilderness map screen `HandleWildernessKeys` shows: it sets view mode 3 and draws the adventure
  screen's three windows (`DrawAdventureScreen`), the area view, the party marker, the location line
  and the roster. `DrawCombatScreen` (`00BA:2B88`, called by `StartBattle` and `PlayerTurn`) prepares
  the EGA palette (`SwapPaletteColors0And8`), draws the two combat windows (1,1)-(21,21) and (23,1)-(38,21), clears
  the bottom rows (0,23)-(39,24) and places the cursor on the combat map's view origin plus the centre
  cell (margin $FF, the no-step direction 8). `DrawLocationLine` (`00BA:290B`) draws the party
  position line on row 15 of the side window: the position (`PartyMapColumn`/`PartyMapRow`, or the
  game map words `VarMapX`/`VarMapY` shifted by the map page width for `AreaViewKind` 3 and 4), the
  facing (`FacingNames`, the string[2] table at DS:25A8: N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W, NW, indexed by
  `PartyFacing`) and the game clock time as hours:minutes (the game clock digits, each padded to two
  digits), with "column,row " prepended outside the 3D view, " camping" in the camp and " search"
  while the wilderness search bit (bit 0 of `GameVars2^[714]`) is set. `SelectPartyMember` (`00BA:2C48`)
  is the member picker the inventory and other screens use: it loops showing the roster and a
  `ReadPromptChoice` "Select" prompt (with " Exit" when the caller asks), moves the caller's
  `Selected` pointer to the previous member with G or the next with O (wrapping the party list
  DS:5D96), clears it with E or the extended-key prefix, and ends on Enter, Escape or E.

## 6. Open questions

1. Remaining compiler directives (`$A`, `$B`, `$V`, `$X`) and which game unit is the 16-byte unit at `0A31` (2.2). The version is pinned: Turbo Pascal 6.0, June 1991 release.
2. Meaning of the stub field at +0E (3.3) and of the fifth `POOL.CFG` line (2.3).
3. Roles of the 11 unnamed resident units (3.2).
4. How much of the resident code is hand-written assembly (graphics, sound, timer) **[?]**.
5. Field map of `SAVGAMA.DAT` (size known, 4.4); WALLDEF tile-to-pixel expansion (4.2.4); affect (`.SPC`) field meanings (4.2.5).

| Facts | Command |
|---|---|
| File hashes | `make inputs` |
| Units, overlays, stubs, entry points | `make overlays` (writes `build/re/overlays.json`) |
| Start-up interrupt sequence | `make smoke` |
| DAX container and RLE | `make dax` / `make test` (`tools/re/dax.py verify poolrad/`) |
| GEO maps | `tools/re/geo.py verify poolrad/` (also via `make dax` / `make test`) |
| ECL script headers | `tools/re/ecl.py verify poolrad/` (also via `make dax` / `make test`) |
| EGA pictures / font | `tools/re/pic.py verify poolrad/` (also via `make dax` / `make test`) |
| WALLDEF wallsets | `tools/re/walldef.py verify poolrad/` |
| Character / item / affect | `tools/re/records.py verify poolrad/` |
| ITEMS type table | `tools/re/items.py verify poolrad/` |
| SAVE directory | `tools/re/save.py verify poolrad/SAVE` |
| Boot, key script sessions, traces | `make oracle-run SCRIPT=tests/sessions/create-character.keys` |
| Screens vs DOSBox-X | `make oracle-dosbox` |

## Appendix C. The oracle: what it models

`tools/oracle` runs the original `start-unpack.exe` and `GAME.OVR` in Unicorn as a deterministic, headless PC (SPEC 8.2, playbook 25). Everything below is **[V]** by `tests/unit/test_oracle.py` unless marked otherwise.

| Area | Modelled | Not modelled (raises or is ignored, never silently wrong) |
|---|---|---|
| CPU and memory | 16-bit real mode, 640 KB, MZ loader with relocations, PSP and environment at `0FF0`/`0F00`, load image at `1000:0000` (the addresses of this document plus `1000h`) | 8087, protected mode, EMS/XMS |
| Interrupts | Software INTs dispatch through the real IVT, so handlers the program installs run as original code (the Borland overlay manager on INT 3Fh, INT 08h of the sound unit); service vectors end in IRET stubs hooked in Python | hardware IRQs 1 to 7 other than IRQ0 |
| Time | Executed-instruction counter (1,000,000 per virtual second), PIT channel 0 reload honoured, IRQ0 delivered at chunk boundaries with IF set and EOI tracked; ports 40h/42h read as free-running counters; port 3DAh retrace derived from the clock; time of day is 12:00:00 plus the clock, optionally pinned by the session (`clock HH:MM:SS.hh`: the first read returns exactly that). A timer interrupt left pending for 2 million instructions (interrupts never re-enabled) stops the run as stalled. Hybrid runs do not use this clock: they replay the recorded reads (below) | wall clock, host date (reported date is fixed 1991-06-11) |
| DOS (INT 21h, 20h, 27h, 29h, 2Fh) | console, vectors, version 3.30, handle file I/O, find first/next, directories, memory blocks, IOCTL, time, exit | EXEC, FCBs, networking, EMS |
| BIOS | INT 10h (mode set, cursor, scroll, tty, palette registers, font info, EGA info), 11h, 12h, 13h reset, 15h (a few), 16h (read, peek, flags), 1Ah (ticks, RTC, date) | disk I/O, serial, printer, mouse (INT 33h is a bare IRET: no driver), VGA DAC, CGA/Tandy video modes |
| Files | `poolrad/` read-only as `C:\POOLRAD`; every write goes to an in-memory layer (`SAVE\RAD.CHA` etc.) and never reaches disk | |
| EGA | 4 planes, latches, map mask, bit mask, set/reset, rotate, logical ops, write modes 0 to 3, read modes 0 and 1, attribute palette to RGB, CRTC start address, modes 0Dh/0Eh/0Fh/10h, simple text modes; PNG screenshots | split screen, panning, odd/even and chain-4 addressing |
| Sound | PIT channel 2 and port 61h writes logged as a speaker event list (`m.speaker_log`) | audio output |
| Input | Script player (`tests/sessions/*.keys`): keys released when the game is idle, time-based waits, `shot`, `digest`, `wheel`, `quit` | joystick, mouse |
| Randomness | Every call of the generator step is logged `(index, time, kind, caller, range, seed, result)` in `m.random_log` (`tools/oracle/rng.py`), direct `RandSeed` writes in `m.seed_writes`; `summary.json` carries the count and a digest of the `(kind, caller, range, result)` sequence without times, so a run with other timing can be compared on decisions alone; `--trace rng` writes `random.txt` | |
| Tracing | Interrupts, file I/O, port I/O, call and return of every routine of `re/symbols.toml` (resident and overlay, overlay entry points resolved from the stub's load segment), memory watch points, coverage of routines entered | argument decoding by signature (the signatures in `re/symbols.toml` are still empty: four raw stack words are logged) |
| State | `Machine.state_digest()` (registers, conventional memory, video), `oracle.state.canonical_digest()` from `re/state.toml` (DGROUP, the allocated Turbo Pascal heap HeapOrg..HeapPtr at DS:4400/4404, and the video planes; `heap = "compared"`, decision 2026-10-11) | `heap_root` entries (need `re/types/`) |
| Hybrid hooks | `routines.replace(m, id, fn, ret_bytes=, far=)` replaces a routine by Python or C wherever it is loaded (overlay routines are rehooked after every read of GAME.OVR) and declines (`return False` runs the original); `Machine.hook_routine` / `hook_linear` at a fixed address. The C library (`make hybrid`, `tools/oracle/hybrid.py`) is bound to guest RAM. `routines.Profiler` counts each call's own-code instructions (the dynamic timing scan) | a general marshaller from `re/symbols.toml` signatures; the three spike routines have hand-written stack layouts. `orig_call` (C calling an unlifted routine) is not built yet |

Acceptance results (playbook 25):

- **Boots to the title screens**: `tests/sessions/boot-title.keys` reaches the SSI logo, the title and the credits in EGA mode 0Dh after 24, 35 and 44 million instructions. Each of the three screens is **pixel-identical** to a frame recorded by DOSBox-X 2026.10.01 running the same unpacked executable (`make oracle-dosbox`: DOSBox-X's `dx-capture` ZMBV recording decoded with ffmpeg, compared byte for byte). The wheel screen is not compared because its runes depend on the clock.
- **Deterministic**: the same script run twice gives identical stop points, instruction counts, per-key-read state digests, checkpoint digests, final state digest and call traces.
- **Character creation completes from a script**: `tests/sessions/create-character.keys` (about 64 million instructions, 13 s of wall time) ends at the main menu with `SAVE\RAD.CHA` decoded as the character shown on the sheet (5.8).
- **Hybrid timing, first method (2026-10-10, retired 2026-10-11)**: an exact-time mode counted instructions one by one in Python and charged each replaced call the instructions the original had spent in it, from a per-call cost table. It worked for `System_Move` and `System_FillChar`, but needed a cost merge for every callee a C routine absorbs, a one-call spinner allowance at checkpoints, ran 7 times slower than the plain oracle, and could never apply to the native build. It is replaced by record/replay (below). Kept from it: the finding that a hybrid run differs from the original only in the stack segment.
- **Hybrid with C routines** (C track, branch `track-c-hybrid`): `System_Move`, `System_FillChar` and `System_Random` in C reproduced both goldens' recordings at every read and checkpoint. The replacement hook (`oracle.routines.replace`) and the record/replay stay on `main` for the rebuild and the C translation.
- **Record/replay** (decision of the human 2026-10-11, `tools/oracle/replay.py`, `tests/unit/test_replay.py`, SPEC 8.4). Exhume's port method (UW2Decomp `PORT.md`, "The differential test") applied to hybrid runs: time matters to the game only where it reads the outside world, so those reads are recorded and replayed instead of instructions being counted. A survey of both sessions found the reads are only INT 16h (peeks and reads), INT 21h AH=2Ch (106,036 polls in `boot-title`, 181,103 in `create-character`) and the BIOS tick word 0040:006C read by Crt init and Crt.Delay (32,176); no timer or retrace port reads and no INT 1Ah. The recording run is the golden run, and the recording is saved with the golden (`replay.json.gz`, about 115 KB per session) and verified by `make golden CHECK=1`; each read is stored with the number of timer interrupts raised so far, run-length coded per kind. A replay answers each read from its stream and raises the timer interrupt as often as the recording had by that read, so the game's timer handler runs at reads instead of mid-computation (its only writes are stack frames and the BIOS tick, see Time above). Checkpoints sit on read boundaries (after each key read, after the first read that follows a script `shot`, and every 20,000 reads) and compare the no-stack canonical state, all memory outside the stack segment, the screen, the Random count and the interrupt count; the end also compares the Random sequence and the files written. **[V]** Replaying the original alone reproduces both recordings; with Move, FillChar and Random in C (49,966 + 15,138 + 33,431 calls in `boot-title`, 53,352 + 15,740 + 33,548 in `create-character`) both sessions match at every read with no time charged, no cost table and no spinner allowance, in about 10 to 22 s per session. A C Random that answers one wrong value is caught at the `sheet` checkpoint; a corrupted Move is caught when the replay stops reading the outside world (watchdog at four times the recording's longest quiet stretch). A read of another kind than the recording's next one is reported by its number.
- **Goldens recorded** (playbook 40, `make golden`): each session is run twice and written only when both runs agree on stop reason, instruction count, every checkpoint (virtual time, screen, canonical and full state digests, Random call count), every per-key-read state digest, the Random sequence and the final state. A golden is `tests/sessions/golden/<name>/{golden.json, reads.txt, reads_nostack.txt, replay.json.gz, <label>.png}`; `make golden CHECK=1` re-runs the original and compares digests (not PNG bytes). Recorded: `boot-title` (3 checkpoints, 44,049,050 instructions) and `create-character` (5 checkpoints, 26 key reads, 64,701,234 instructions, 33,548 Random calls). `boot-title` has no key reads: the title screens run without input, so its verdict is the three checkpoint digests. The scripts' screenshots were reviewed by eye; one capture point was wrong and was fixed before recording (`sheet` was taken while the character was still being rolled, all scores 0; the script now waits for the "Keep this character? Yes No" prompt).

Known limits **[V]** (test `test_hybrid_hook_python_move_matches_original`):

- On the instruction clock, virtual time is instructions and a replacement executes none, so after a few timer ticks a hooked run places ticks at slightly different program points and tick-derived bytes in DGROUP differ (`DS:C312`, `C404..C407` at the 400th call of `System_Move`; equal states hold up to the 120th call). Session replays remove this: the game's reads of the outside world are answered from the original's recording, so where the timer lands no longer changes what the game decides (acceptance results above).
- **Wait loops** **[V]** (`make timing-scan`, `tools/re/timing_scan.py`, tests `test_timing_scan.py`): the replacement runs instantly at the routine's entry, so a routine that *waits* (spins until a key, a tick, a retrace) cannot be a literal C transliteration: no time passes while C runs, and in a replay the reads it would spin on are recorded values. Static scan over all 1393 routines with the call graph (far calls through overlay stubs resolved): 100 loop around a time or input feature (2 in their own bytes: the keyboard drain `Crt_CheckBreak` and Crt init `0A40:002E`, which spins on the BIOS tick; 98 through a callee: `Crt` KeyPressed `0A40:02FD` and ReadKey `0A40:030F`, the sound routines `0A40:029C` and `08AE:006C`); 369 more read the clock or keyboard outside a loop; 924 do neither. Note that most of these loops are not waits in the sense that matters: draining a buffer or polling a key in a menu loop ends by itself in a replay, where each poll returns its recorded result; what cannot work in C inside the oracle is a loop whose exit depends on time passing (5.14). Dynamic pass over `boot-title` and `create-character`: 228 routines ran, KeyPressed alone 508,736 times; four calls spun a whole timer period or more in their own code (`ovl01:0000`, `ovl01:0627`, the menu loop `ovl25:03BD`, and `UnpackRle` with 342,000 instructions, which the static pass says touches nothing, so it is computation). The game's own timer interrupt writes no DGROUP global (its only writes are its stack frames). Exactly one routine reads the BIOS tick counter at 0040:006C, `0A40:002E` (an earlier version of this paragraph said none: the scan missed the register-indirect access). Decision of 2026-10-10 (human): waits go through the platform layer, `src/platform/platform.h` (5.14); the scan's list (`build/re/timing-scan.md`) says which routines need `platform_*_wait` instead of a spin.
- **Stack segment** **[V]**: a replaced routine does not push the frames the original pushed, so bytes of the stack segment (SS:0000 to 4000) differ between a hybrid run and the original, including slots of live frames that nobody has written yet (SS:3A4D at the first key reads of `create-character`). No byte of globals, heap or video differs. The goldens therefore carry two state digests: `canonical` (everything, as before) and `canonical_nostack`. Decision 2026-10-10 (human): `re/state.toml` sets `stack = "diagnostic"`, and a hybrid run is compared on `canonical_nostack`. The full digest stays in the golden. A read of an uninitialised Pascal local (stale stack content) is a quirk a C lift does not reproduce, and it is not part of the state the port has to match.
- Pinning the clock fixes the seed but not the number of `Random` calls that timing-driven code makes (5.13). In a hybrid run the logged sequence is the first thing to compare: the first differing entry names the call where it diverged.
- Time is exact at chunk boundaries only (chunks end at timer events); an interrupt that falls inside a service call is delivered after it.
- The BIOS font ROM is not in the pinned inputs: text-mode screenshots use a placeholder font (the game itself draws all text in graphics mode with its own font, so game screens are unaffected).
